Guards, tests and docs:
- **Route-affordance walk: the docs say what the code does, and the mount reads park their outcomes (`#2682`, closed by the lane after PR `#2706`, merge `a3d1e9867`; Refs `#2682`, `#2708`, `#1949`, PR `#2673`, PR `#2679`).** Test-only, the three files PR `#2673` created. The header's "stated per row" clause is now true: `home.quick-capture-submit`, `inbox.archive`, `review.approve` and `calendar.next-month` carry the per-row note naming why no mount read can settle their `response` wait, each anchored to that view's own `onMounted`. The token docblock states the real `fillTokens` surface (`url.pathPattern`, `response.urlPattern`, `value.value`, `text.text`, `textChangedFrom.from`, a role selector's `name`; not attributes, enabled selectors, node or focus ids, css or test-id values), that `{openingMonthLabel}` appears in two text fields and in no pattern (the old sentence's actual error), and that `RouteEntry.entryPath` is read by nothing. The `assertEnabled` reads stay as belt-and-braces over a required-literal type, documented at both sites and in the assertion-8 docblock (a canary is unconstructible without a cast, the exact asymmetry with assertion 9's `response` canary). The `textChangedFrom` hypothesis was refuted on Playwright 1.62.1: `expect(locator).not.toHaveText(x)` on zero elements fails with "element(s) not found" (measured in Chromium, plus the `missingReceived` path in `playwright-core`), so no `toHaveCount(1)` pairing was added and `calendar.previous-month` is not masked. A `readOnMount` helper parks the four mount reads' outcomes the moment they are armed (the `activate` pattern), so a `goto` failure leaves no unhandled rejection with no owning test; a scratch probe (with its own 2 s bound) showed the shipped shape leaking one unhandled rejection and the parked shape none. Round 2 corrected three sentences in the new text: the mount reads have no event timeout of their own under this config (`actionTimeout` defaults to 0), so they are bounded only by the test timeout, not the "30 s" the first draft claimed (bounding them like `activate`'s 15 s is `#2708`, filed so `#2682` could close); `{boardId}` also appears, unsubstituted, in `entryPath`; and the verbatim-use list names the `selector` of `value`, `text` and `textChangedFrom`. Guard spec 9/9, typecheck, eslint and diff-check clean; the E2E walk 4 passed locally against the config's own web server with the Gemini variables unset. Review: one fresh-context read-only reviewer (SC-9), SHIP; one MEDIUM (the new text asserted a "30 s" bound that does not exist) and two LOW enumeration slips, all fixed in round 2 as comments only, so no second pass was owed; bounding the mount reads like `activate`'s 15 s is recorded on the issue as a follow-up, not bundled. Not verified: CI's single-worker E2E lane (the second proof); the four per-row claims are read from each view's `onMounted`, not observed on the wire. Exact head `4b1a20003`.

Review and proposal frontend:
- **The Paper review rail never shows the previous proposal's evidence and says loading or failed instead of claiming emptiness (`#1940` two 2026-09-05 residuals, PR `#2705`, merge `a8a3f3496`; Refs `#1940`, D-9 and the parked P-key branch stay open).** `usePaperReviewSelectors` replaces its bare `isLoading` with a batch record (key plus status) and exposes one keyed snapshot, `railEvidence`, that carries the batch state for the active key (idle, loading, failed with the outcome kept, settled) and withholds the confidence breakdown, the similar-past rows and the apply rate in every state but settled. `PaperReviewView` wires the rail and `authorMeta` from that snapshot, `ReviewRightRail` forwards a new optional `evidenceState` prop (default loading) to both cards, and `ReviewAuthorCard` and `ReviewSimilarPast` reserve the confidence-source sentence, the "No comparable past decisions" sentence and the "(none found)" label for a settled read, rendering loading and failed sentences otherwise (new `author.confidence*` and `similarPast.*` keys in en, it and es). Read semantics (the `#2460` keying, the automatic refresh, the same-action retry, `waitForCoreBatch`) are unchanged. Specs red-first (14 failed on the unmodified source, the load-bearing one asserting that the bare reads still held proposal A's row and A's overall while B loaded). Round 2 answered the review's one MEDIUM, a regression from the changed lines: the snapshot compared keys exactly while the key watcher treats a revision retiring to null (a decided proposal's DTO) as not a move, so a rejected revised proposal left the rail on "Reading…" forever; one shared predicate, `selectorKeyStillCovers`, now serves both the snapshot and the watcher's no-op branch, the settled snapshot returns the key its values were read under, the fast path and the publication guards keep exact matching on purpose (the `#2460` Apply contract), and a composable regression that failed on the round-1 head (`expected 'loading' to be 'settled'`) pins it; the view-level reject case is kept as a labelled guard because the reject issues a second evidence batch and so never reproduced. At the fix head the seven named specs 311 passed, the Paper review directory 403 passed, typecheck and eslint clean. Review: one fresh-context read-only reviewer (SC-9), SHIP on the bar with the MEDIUM above taken as the round-2 fix and two LOWs (the STATUS line, recorded by this block; the pre-existing author-card pair, on the issue); a second read-only pass scoped to the fix diff: SHIP, the transition table unchanged and the regression spec falsifiable, its two comment LOWs tracked on the issue. Residuals recorded on the issue: `ReviewMain` still receives the bare confidence read, so its badge can show the previous proposal's number during the same in-flight window; a decision landing while the batch is in flight leaves the record at loading (the superseded read publishes nothing) until another proposal is selected, unreachable for Approve and Apply; the author card's "model-reported average" line above "No model confidence reported" with zero components (pre-existing); no retry affordance for a failed page-load batch; the state lines are plain text with no live region; es and it copy not native-reviewed (`#1770`). Exact head `688bedc58`.
- **A revoked review queue gets one report and one owner, and a count-neutral queue replacement re-keys the live region's node in both skins (`#2214` residual pass, PR `#2710`, merge `5487c263b`; Refs `#2214`, `#2599`, PRs `#2593`, `#2630`, `#2694`, `#2702`).** The lane's worker audited every residual ever recorded on `#2214` against `main` at `a8a3f3496` and posted the table on the issue: 37 rows, 23 shipped (each with the code line or spec on `main` that proves it), 1 superseded, 13 open, of which this PR fixes three. (1) Issue item 4: `useReviewProposals` exposes `awaitingProposalIds` and a `queueAnnouncementKey` (the ordered awaiting ids); Legacy's live region and Paper's `ReviewQueueRail` take their count from that one predicate and wrap the unchanged sentence in a node keyed on the identity inside a region that stays mounted, so a poll that drops one pending proposal and adds another replaces the node and is announced, while a byte-identical queue adds nothing and the `#2593` loading and revoked gates stand; no wording or locale key changed. (2) The explicit list read's 403 raises `recordQueueAccessRevoked()` or the generic toast, never both, the two-reports-for-one-fact shape `#2694` removed on the pin leg; the `failed` outcome still reaches the action composables. (3) The route-hash watcher returns early while the queue is revoked, so the unavailable-pin state is never written under the revoked panel. Specs red-first (seven failed on the unmodified source: missing announcement nodes in three view specs, undefined composable members, the toast and the by-id read called once); six named specs 492 passed, typecheck, build and eslint clean. Review: one fresh-context read-only reviewer (SC-9), SHIP at round 1 with nothing to fix in the diff: the STATUS finding is this block's (the corrections line below retires the false "item 4 still open" clause), and four rows were added to the issue's table (a second explicit 403 under a standing revoked panel is now silent on a user-initiated retry, since the revoke call is idempotent; the watcher guard's recovery half is unpinned; the live-region comments assert what an assistive technology speaks while only node identity is proven; navigating between two deferred deep links flips the announcement key); the reviewer confirmed Vue re-keys and remounts the node inside a region that is never re-created, byte-for-byte predicate parity between the skins, the 403 path enumeration (401 still toasts, the `failed` outcome unchanged, the resume path still reached) and that the polls cannot shuffle the key. Open on the issue after this pass: the Paper polled-403 announcement (no live region or focus move on the revoked branch), the workload-badge lag after a failed entry load and its missing spec, aborted-poll logging in the shared HTTP layer (`src/api/**`, not the lane's), the `#2694` "double announcement" and "empty-queue wording" LOWs (each variant defective or needing es/it copy), `#2599` items 1 and 2, the sticky seam (D-15); row 23, the false STATUS clause, is retired by this block's correction. Exact head `5b88373fb`.

Shell, shortcuts and dialogs:
- **Keys pressed with focus inside a shell surface no longer reach page-level listeners (`#2636`, PR `#2709`, merge `e0508462a`; Refs `#2636`, `#2621`, `#1968`, PR `#2635`).** `AppShell` adds a `document`-level bubble-phase guard beside PR `#2635`'s capture-phase `window` guard: capture stops targets outside the active surface on the way down, bubble stops targets inside it on the way up, so a key pressed with focus in a help twin, a command palette or an `aria-modal` dialog (including CaptureModal over a Legacy board) reaches the surface's own element-level handlers and then stops before the page's `window` listeners (the board keymap's `f` and `n`, the bare-letter navigation set, the g-chord, the Paper Home and Inbox listeners, the review keymap). Escape is never stopped, text-entry targets are skipped (so `#1968`'s no-scan-while-typing rule stands), and the surface scan runs once per event through a shared memo the capture guard populates. The PR body carries the page-level listener audit: eight listeners, two beyond the issue's six (`useShortcutContext`'s dead document listener; `useReviewKeymap` registered by the Paper review view with a `window` target and an `enabled` predicate that gates its own dialogs but not the shell surfaces). Specs red-first from an element inside the surface with `bubbles: true` (four of six failed on the unmodified source: the help dialog, the navigation set and g-chord, CaptureModal over the board, a focused palette option); AppShell spec 66 passed, the shell surfaces and keyboard composables 11 files / 189, the views owning page listeners 9 files / 228, the review keymap seam 3 files / 68; typecheck, eslint and diff-check clean; the keyboard-navigation and workspace-help E2E specs 6 passed locally. Review: one fresh-context read-only reviewer (SC-9), SHIP; one MEDIUM (the guard's docblock claimed safety because "all four surfaces bind their own keys on their own elements", while the Paper help overlay binds its Escape on `window` and survives only through the Escape carve-out, and the guard reaches every `aria-modal` in the app) and three LOWs (two comment mechanisms, Paper-skin from-inside coverage), all answered in round 2 as comments plus one Paper-skin spec from inside the help overlay (`f` and `n` reach neither a window-bubble probe nor the board keymap, Escape still closes; the docblock now states the real invariant, that a surface keeps a key only if it handles it at or below `document` or the key is Escape, and names the guard's reach as every `dialog[open]`, `[role="alertdialog"]` or `[aria-modal="true"]` in the app, with the component families it fires for; the PR body's own grep puts that at 16 files including `AppShell.vue`'s selector constant), so no second pass was owed; the reviewer re-derived the eight-listener audit, confirmed every surface handles its keys at element level, that no default action is prevented, that the shell's own `?` and `mod+k` bindings run at window capture and never meet the bubble guard, and that the `#1968` zero-scan-while-typing pin holds. Residual stated by the PR: a modifier combo pressed in a modal's text field (`mod+;`) still reaches the Paper Home and Inbox listeners, outside the issue's acceptance and not closable without scanning every typed keystroke. Exact head `8da74c1b5`.

- **Not shipped reality:** Nothing under ADR-0057 was touched. Still held or open after this block: `#2638` items 1 and 3 (the Paper decision rail's sticky positioning) wait on D-15; `#1999` item 2 waits on D-13; `#1940`'s D-9 ruling stands, with the `ReviewMain` badge's bare confidence read, the mid-flight-decision loading state, the author card's average-above-"no confidence" pair and two comment notes recorded on the issue; `#2214` keeps rows 7, 8, 11, 13, 31, 32, 34, 35, 36 and 38 to 41 of its posted table (row 23, the STATUS clause, is retired by this block's correction) (the Paper polled-403 announcement, the workload-badge lag and its spec, aborted-poll logging in the shared HTTP layer, the double-announcement and empty-queue-wording LOWs, the sticky seam, `#2599` items 1 and 2, the silent repeat 403 on a user-initiated retry, the watcher's unpinned recovery half, the assistive-technology claim in comments, the deferred deep-link key flip); `#2636` keeps the `mod+;` text-field leak into the Paper Home and Inbox listeners; `#2708` (bounding the route walk's four mount reads) is filed and unclaimed; `#2689` item 5 (six direct `getBoards` callers, re-measured) and the detail read's success-path clear stay per-caller decisions; `#1871`'s review-i18n half, `#1434` finding 1 and `#2007`, `#1284`, `#2009`, `#2141`, `#1984` stay on their rulings. Review and Inbox frontend (alpha lane work, merged by the coordinator):
- **Queue live region: no repeat announcement on a same-scope explicit reload, and focus after the unavailable-pin return control (`#2599` items 1 and 2, PR `#2712`, merge `4ddb68c74`; Refs `#2599`).** `useReviewProposals.ts` gains a landed-scope signal (written only on a successful explicit or poll read for the current scope, cleared on revocation) and both skins' count gates read it instead of the loading term, so a reload of the scope already on screen keeps the live region's node mounted (an unchanged queue is silent and a moved queue is still announced once) and the first render no longer speaks "0 proposals"; after the unavailable-pin return control both skins hand focus back (Paper to a row button, Legacy to the queue section that owns the Arrow handler while the persistent cursor stays where it was; each skin falls back to its empty-state element when no row is rendered; the divergence is recorded for the ADR-0038 drift audit). Round 1 fresh-context review SHIP; round 2 was comments and the rail spec only (the coordinator confirmed by diff that no non-comment production line changed). Evidence: six named specs, typecheck, eslint at the head per the lane; `CI` green at `80631e221`. **Not verified:** no screen-reader pass. Remaining on `#2599`: the reviewer's LOW (Paper's fallback can land focus on `body` when the rail is filtered to zero rows while a decision receipt holds the main column).
- **A dropped Paper Inbox scope-replacement response is repaired once instead of latching (`#2591`, PR `#2713`, merge `9b864ea78`; Refs `#2591`).** `useInboxOrchestrator.ts` keeps a per-replacement latch: a dropped response issues one repair read for the current scope with the flag still raised (the table shows its loading state, not the previous scope's rows); a second drop clears the flag and lets the table state what it has. Two fresh-context reviews: the alpha lane's (SHIP, one MEDIUM comment-truth item: the second-drop clear's guarantee holds only while the orchestrator is the sole Paper-side `fetchItems` caller, which the comment now says) and the coordinator's after the lane closed (FIX-FIRST: `InboxView.spec.ts` still carried the stale `Promise<void>` `fetchItems` mock that the PR had corrected in `PaperInboxScopeTruth.spec.ts`, so under the new code every mount of the real Legacy view read a dropped response; the mock resolves `true`, local run 151 of 151 across the three specs). `CI` green at `032fa4f68`. **Not verified:** the failure-path shape recorded on `#2591` (a batch poll success clears `listError` under a raised flag, pre-existing on `main`) and the repair-after-hash-await ordering stay open there.

v0.3 post-RC integration (2026-08-30 onward; entries are topical, so check the 2026-09-03 block above as well):
- **Production encrypted recovery is shipped (`#2235`, `#2238`, `#2239`, PRs `#2360`/`#2361`, merges `3af00b6c3`/`73371ef7b`).** Packaged Docker images include pre-host `taskdeck-backup` and `taskdeck-restore` commands, and the CLI exposes read-only `--verify-connectors`. Backup uses SQLite's online backup API and authenticated encryption; restore validates integrity, schema, and connector decryptability, removes stale SQLite sidecars, creates a safety archive, and rolls back a failed promotion. The hosted Container Images checks succeeded on both landing PRs, with the production-image smoke proving restored rows, connector verification, and no leftover sidecars; the operator procedure and monthly/quarterly drill cadence live in `docs/ops/DISASTER_RECOVERY_RUNBOOK.md`. **Not verified:** a human production restore drill, measured RPO/RTO, or live access to every external connector provider.
- **Repeated credential-login failures coalesce into one durable diagnostic receipt (`#2373`, PR `#2384`, merge `83fe24505`).** Each failed credential attempt replaces only its prior login-failure toast and retains structured diagnostics when available; a successful credential login clears only that receipt, leaving registration, password, OAuth, and unrelated receipts independent. Evidence: 47 focused frontend tests, the full 357-file / 5,318-test frontend suite, typecheck, production build, scoped lint, exact-head independent review, and a hosted rollup of 24 successes / 11 declared skips including a successful E2E Smoke. **Not verified:** a dedicated manual-browser or screen-reader pass; direct overlapping store calls resolve in completion order, while the only production credential form serializes submissions.
- **The archive-card proposal's complete HTTP lifecycle is pinned (`#2185` partial, PR `#2383`, merge `26dc1e9be`).** The API proof registers a user, creates the card and proposal, approves and executes it, then reads back the persisted blocked card, `Applied` proposal, and `Archived` audit entry. The focused proof passed 1/1, the neighbouring `AutomationProposalsApiTests` suite passed 50/50 on the landing base, and the exact-head hosted rollup was 24 successes / 11 declared skips including a successful E2E Smoke. **Not verified at the time of that slice:** preview seeding, an already-blocked audit-summary case, production-payload fidelity, or the remaining user-facing wording. Those residuals shipped on 2026-09-03 in PR `#2410` (merge `a32b4818d`), which makes the archive-card proposal preview the real block outcome; `#2185` is closed.
- **Partial-date residual coverage now pins January-to-December rollover and same-day retention (`#2193` partial, PR `#2380`, merge `36fd01d41`).** The parser-contract fixtures supply ISO `dueDateHint` values and prove year-window retention across the real prompt-output parser boundary; they do not prove model derivation. Evidence: 34 focused and 4,112 full Application tests plus an exact-head hosted rollup of 24 successes / 11 declared skips including a successful E2E Smoke. **Not verified:** that a live provider follows the derivation instruction; provider/prompt behavior and capture-day anchoring remain tracked on `#2210`.

- **The EF8 runtime guard now includes the SQL Server provider (`#2386`, PR `#2387`, merge `dcd258af2`).** Dependabot's existing major-version cap now names `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.SqlServer` alongside the rest of the coordinated EF runtime stack, and the operator policy names the same boundary, so an isolated EF9 provider bump cannot be proposed as routine maintenance. All 20 exact-head hosted checks passed, including Linux/Windows backend and API suites, migration validation, container images, and E2E Smoke. **Not verified:** an EF9 migration or live SQL Server upgrade; both were deliberately outside this prevention-only slice.
- **The frontend minor/patch dependency group is current and the live Browserslist advisory is cleared (PR `#2364`, merge `6aa97465b`).** Thirteen direct packages moved together, the Material Symbols branding/licence sentinels were refreshed for 0.47.0, and every lockfile path now resolves Browserslist 4.28.7. Evidence: `npm ci` and high-severity audit reported zero vulnerabilities; branding 4/4, coverage 5,318/5,318, typecheck, production build, scoped lint, and diff hygiene passed; all 36 hosted checks either succeeded or were deliberately skipped, including E2E Smoke. **Not verified:** a dedicated manual-browser or packaged-desktop journey for the dependency-only refresh.
- **The maintainer-only v0.3.0 launch kit is copy-ready without claiming publication (`#2242`, PR `#2389`, merge `81a5921bc`).** It carries evidence-bound drafts for r/selfhosted, Show HN, and dev.to; a delayed awesome-selfhosted path; known-gap answers; and a bounded 48-hour triage plan. Docs and GitHub-operations governance, 50 local links, external-link validation, diff hygiene, exact-head review, and hosted CI including E2E Smoke passed. **Not verified:** no post, final v0.3.0 tag/artifact, signing, or repository-visibility cutover occurred; RT-1/2/3 and SC-8 remain explicit human gates.
- **Launch-kit execution and security gates are fail-closed (`#2390`, PR `#2392`, merge `982f40e8e`).** Publication now requires a working public home plus signed-out ZIP/checksum proof, loopback-listener claims name exposure overrides, Compose setup names both required secrets, and ordinary bugs stay separate from private vulnerability reporting. Docs/operations governance, 29 DesktopRuntime tests, diff hygiene, exact-head review, and all 20 hosted checks including E2E Smoke passed. **Not verified:** a real anonymous public-home/SC-8 journey; `#2391` retains copy-current and standalone-draft residuals.
- **Paper quick capture now exposes both its destination and submit action (`#1937`, PR `#2393`, merge `12ffc4e9f`).** The nib has a visible disabled-aware action, platform-correct shortcut copy, and honest active-board versus boardless destination text in English, Italian, and Spanish. Three focused component specs passed 78 tests, the locale guard passed 19, typecheck and diff hygiene passed, and all 36 hosted checks succeeded or were deliberately skipped, including E2E Smoke. **Not verified:** a dedicated manual-browser or screen-reader pass; two declined LOW polish notes remain (Spanish accenting and redundant test-local i18n setup).
- **Review keyboard focus residuals are closed (`#1983`, PR `#2396`, merge `125804d34`).** Apply confirmation via Enter is guarded so only unmodified Enter confirms from container focus; Ctrl/Meta/Alt/Shift+Enter chords remain inert. Revision edit cancel and successful save predictably restore focus to the Request edit rail control, guarded by proposal identity to prevent late-save focus jumps across selection changes. Evidence: targeted component and view integration tests in `ApplyToBoardDialog.spec.ts` and `PaperReviewView.spec.ts`, typecheck, production build, scoped lint, exact-head review, and hosted CI with E2E Smoke passed. **Not verified:** dedicated screen-reader speech or native cross-browser chord quirks.
- **Authenticated API responses are out of every browser-side cache (`#2350`, PR `#2381`, merge `0974eeb8a`).** The NetworkFirst API runtime rule is removed rather than narrowed, so `vite.config.ts` now emits only StaleWhileRevalidate locale chunks and CacheFirst build-owned `/assets/` and `/icons/`; `ApiCacheControlMiddleware` stamps `no-store, private` server-side; and a forced retirement purges any pre-`#2350` worker and its orphaned runtime caches across sessions. 29 files, +1923/-69, including a new generated-worker contract test and a `pwa-api-cache` E2E journey. The retired runtime-cache contract suite is explicitly `describe.skip`, not deleted. Exact-head hosted rollup: 24 successes / 11 declared skips. **Not verified:** a real installed-PWA upgrade journey on a device that still holds a pre-`#2350` worker; `#2382` (keeping the generated-worker contract runnable from a fresh checkout) shipped on 2026-09-03 in PR `#2402` (merge `b66da8fd4`) and is closed. The stale `NetworkFirst for API calls` comment above `runtimeCaching` in `vite.config.ts:86` still needs removing.

v0.3 RC preparation (2026-08-30):
- **MCP stdio known-tool calls now return actionable identity remediation without weakening fail-closed access (`#2228` code, PR `#2316`, merge `5e0d5eba2`).** When multiple active users have no `McpServer:DefaultUserId`, or the configured ID does not name an active user, every identity-gated stdio request still denies access and the provider writes the existing non-secret configuration guidance once at Warning to stderr across the host process; a known `tools/call` also returns that guidance in an `isError: true` tool result. `initialize` remains lazy, but tool/resource discovery and resource reads resolve identity and return generic access-denied JSON-RPC errors. HTTP MCP and every other identity failure retain generic denial. Evidence: 195 focused MCP tests, including production child-process coverage of two denied calls with one stderr warning; Architecture Tests 26 passed / 1 declared skip; exact-current-base patch-identity and adversarial review; automatic Codex review; and hosted Required plus Extended CI green with 21 successes / 11 declared skips including E2E Smoke. **Not verified:** a simultaneous first-failure race or explicit cancellation test; the synchronization and exception-flow paths were inspected. The configuration and upgrade notes in this reconciliation complete the operator-facing half before `#2228` closes.
- **Capture-linked Paper Review provenance is visible without staling the core review surface (`#1284` partial, PR `#2310`, merge `2427108e8`).** The active Queue proposal performs one abortable capture-detail lookup, carries only server-recorded provider/model/prompt-version metadata into the producer footnote and drawer, and shows numeric confidence only when the proposal confidence endpoint supplies a trustworthy value. Missing, stale, inaccessible, Chat, and Manual paths fail closed to no producer claim. The optional capture request no longer holds the six core review responses behind its retry window, late results cannot overwrite a newer selection, and the adjacent ink-token correction restores WCAG AA contrast. Evidence: 265 focused frontend tests; the full 357-file / 5,291-test suite; typecheck, production build, scoped ESLint, and diff check; a fresh Mock-backed native-Chromium/axe journey (1/1); current-base review; and Required plus Extended CI green with hosted E2E Smoke. **Not verified:** real-provider, packaged-desktop, screen-reader, or native Safari/Firefox behavior. `#1284` remains open: `#1987` owns the direct Chat/proposal payload contract, `#2315` owns proposal-scoped authorization/origin/lifecycle states, and `#2317` now supplies the effective-revision producer-copy qualification described next.
- **Producer attribution is qualified after an effective proposal revision (`#2317`, PR `#2318`, merge `f1baf58c2`).** Paper Review withholds the inline capture-producer footnote while revision state is unknown, unavailable, or contains any saved effective revision; the drawer still exposes the immutable server-recorded capture metadata, and the inline footnote returns only after a successful revision read confirms an empty list. Revision badges, decision controls, and Apply semantics are unchanged. Evidence: 169 focused frontend tests; scoped ESLint, typecheck, production build, and diff check; exact-current-base patch-identity and adversarial review; automatic Codex review with no findings; and hosted Required plus Extended CI green with 21 successes / 11 declared skips including E2E Smoke. **Not verified:** a dedicated live-browser or assistive-technology pass. A same-identity stale Queue poll after another reviewer saves a revision can briefly restore the pre-revision DTO and footnote until a later poll carries revision identity; approved/terminal and pending/error render-matrix expansion is also deferred. Both stay tracked in `#2315`, with no authorization or Apply-integrity impact.
- **Batch-execute consent is bound to the reviewed proposal snapshot (`#2283`, PR `#2286`, merge `c1945329b`).** Opening confirmation captures the exact capped proposal ids, approved-revision pins, receipt titles, and canonical board/history scope. A synchronous pre-submit guard closes the dialog when membership, revision, or scope drifts, the click handler revalidates the same snapshot again, and the request is constructed only from that capture; refreshes after the request starts cannot rewrite its payload or receipt labels. Evidence at exact feature head `0c1197bc0`: 156 focused composable/view tests, the full frontend suite (357 files / 5,265 tests), typecheck, build, scoped ESLint, two clean fresh-context reviews, and hosted Required CI including E2E Smoke (21 success / 11 declared skips). **Not verified:** no dedicated manual-browser or screen-reader pass; the explicit 500/501 boundary and genuinely different revision-pin cases remain LOW test-expansion residuals.
- **The backend executes each confirmed item from fresh persisted state (`#2284`, PR `#2290`, merge `88a5c04bf`).** The batch preload now contains only stable authorization identifiers. For each item, the executor performs a fresh pre-transaction proposal lookup, checks the submitter's access and explicit revision expectation, decides the already-applied or Approved path, and materializes the pinned operations. After the item transaction begins, the archive decision guard and submitter's current write permission are rechecked before the first operation. Deterministic barriers cover concurrent Apply, revoked replay, and PendingReview/null-to-Approved/pinned races without duplicate board, audit, capture-sync, or realtime effects. Evidence: 36 focused application tests, 19 focused API tests, Architecture Tests 26 passed / 1 declared skip, a Release solution build with zero errors, targeted format checks, current-base review, and hosted Required CI.
- **The receipt transition is focused, singly announced, durable, and guarded from held-key dismissal (`#2285`, PR `#2288`, merge `8c524bff6`; `#2289`, PR `#2307`, merge `9b8a968b0`; `#2292`, PR `#2309`, merge `3fbe7cae8`; `#2308`, PR `#2321`, merge `fd82d382d`).** Receipts move focus to their sole Done action, mutate one already-mounted polite/atomic status node, suppress duplicate receipt-bearing completion toasts while retaining a fallback announcement when context closes the dialog, and consume a held/repeating confirmation Enter until a real keyup rearms Done. The active dialog now captures that release at window level even when disabling Confirm moves focus outside, while scoping and resetting the listener on close, anchor replacement, and unmount; pointer, Space, and Escape paths remain available. The original transition passed its red/green component state-machine proof (9/9), scoped ESLint, typecheck, build, a focused native-Chromium Playwright journey (1/1), the full frontend suite (357 files / 5,273 tests), exact-head review, and hosted Required CI. The outside-release follow-up passed 3 focused specs / 52 tests, scoped ESLint, typecheck, build, exact-current-base patch-identity and adversarial review, automatic Codex review with no findings, and hosted Required plus Extended CI with 21 successes / 11 declared skips including E2E Smoke. **Not verified:** actual screen-reader speech, a dedicated native-browser reproduction of the outside-focus release, or native Safari/Firefox activation; related real-device dialog/keyboard verification remains open in `#1821`.
- **Docker MCP profile validation is non-starting, fail-closed, and aligned with the six-server default (`#1590`, PR `#2311`, merge `0ea7b3768`; `#1540`, PR `#2314`, merge `f2e71ca87`).** The validator reads the user-scope profile, fingerprints the exact sorted identity set of every container labeled `docker-mcp=true` before and after the check (including stopped containers), and reports only counts plus SHA-256 fingerprints. Inventory failure or identity drift blocks PASS, while helpers and the WSL drill never claim ownership or stop/remove shared-daemon containers. The implicit required-server list is now `docker,docker-docs,time,jetbrains,filesystem,SQLite`; `openapi` and `terraform` remain catalog opt-ins rather than default members. Evidence: 9/9 focused fake-Docker groups; live Windows `205 -> 205` with identical fingerprints and zero added/removed IDs; a real WSL positive baseline plus missing-member negative; exact-head review; and hosted Required CI for both landing PRs with 21 successes / 11 declared skips including E2E Smoke. **Not verified:** remote credential acceptance, gateway startup, or tool execution. `#2312` tracks hosted focused-suite, Bash-path, and implicit-default fixture coverage; this reconciliation closes `#2313` by naming the full all-container identity contract precisely.
- **Per-proposal batch execute is implemented (`#1307`, q-14 C).** `POST /api/automation/proposals/execute` takes a bounded list (500, the same bound batch approve declares) of `{ proposalId, approvedRevisionId, idempotencyKey }` and executes each approved proposal independently through the SAME executor call single execute uses - `AutomationExecutorService.ExecuteProposalAsync` is now a thin projection of a new `ExecuteProposalWithReceiptAsync`, so there is one execution path and one materialization of the approved revision, and preview == apply parity holds per item by construction rather than by a parallel implementation. The response is always 200 with one row per requested proposal (`Applied` / `Skipped` / `Failed` plus `errorCode` and `appliedOperations`), never 207; there is no whole-batch rollback, so a failing item never undoes its neighbours' board writes. `approvedRevisionId` is a REQUIRED wire member with a nullable value - an omitted key is a 400, because reading absence as "approved from the original operations" would wave a drifted proposal past the fail-closed pin check - and a mismatch fails that item with `Conflict`. Idempotency is per item and behaves exactly as single execute's does: an already-Applied proposal is a no-op reported as `Skipped`, so `Applied` is only ever claimed by the call that performed the write. Authorization is the single-execute board-write bar applied per item, rechecked for the CALLER inside each item's own transaction rather than only in the batch's pre-loop snapshot, so a write revoked while a batch is running stops every remaining item. Failure rows are shaped so a batch is not an enumeration oracle: an item on a board the caller cannot read is reported exactly as a missing one (`NotFound`, identical message), and `Forbidden` is reserved for boards the caller can read, where the row's existence is already known to them. A request in which nothing was executable collapses to one status - 404 when every row is `NotFound`, 403 when every row is `Forbidden` - and anything mixed stays 200. This is deliberately NOT parity with single execute, which was measured on this head to answer **403** for a proposal on a board the caller cannot read and for a foreign board-less proposal; batch answers 404 for those cases, which is strictly less leaky. Single execute's own 403-vs-404 distinction is unchanged - it carries one id per request, so it is a one-bit oracle a caller could already build by hand. What this work closed is the 500-ids-per-round-trip amplification, not the underlying distinction; narrowing single execute to match is a separate decision and was not taken. In Paper Review the rail gains an *Apply approved* action beside batch approve, scoped to the same class of work batch approve admits (the reviewer's own, exactly-`Approved`, Low-risk, live, non-deferred, bounded create-card-only proposals, via one shared predicate the two surfaces cannot drift apart on), withheld entirely in read-only archived history, behind one in-app confirmation (no native `confirm`) that then shows per-item receipts - applied / skipped / failed with reason - and refreshes the queue; en/it/es, with it/es machine-translated. ADR-0003 / GP-06 are untouched: this never approves anything, and approve and execute remain two explicit steps. Evidence: Application.Tests 516 `Proposal`-filtered (16 new), Api.Tests 348 `Proposal`-filtered (17 new, end-to-end against the real stack including partial-failure isolation with real board writes, three-call replay producing one card and one audit row, a staged mid-batch access revocation, and per-item preview == apply proven against each proposal's own diff), Architecture.Tests 26 passed / 1 skip, frontend 2045 review/Paper/i18n/api specs, `npm run typecheck` clean, and twenty-six mutation checks each measured to fail the intended specs and restored. **Not verified:** no live browser or Playwright pass, no live-provider run, and no hosted CI at this head at the time of writing.
- **The maintainer's v0.3 RC deck replies are applied (`#1947`).** Milestones renamed to *v0.3 — Accountable Agents + Downloadable Beta* and *v0.4 — Hosted Open Beta + Work Model + Every Artefact* with no due dates ("we ship when the release is ready") — the v0.4 title became *… + Fabric Foundation* later the same day under the ADR-0065 delegation (see the Context Fabric block above); the install-free hosted open beta is v0.4 (`#2243`); the unstarted work-model slices moved to v0.4 with the multiple-assignments sub-slice `#2240` kept; `#1306` closed on its shipped `OpenAICompatible` half and streaming re-filed as `#2241`; `#1879` closed (no in-app provider-key UI in v0.x); 29 Priority I/II fix and dogfooding issues pulled into v0.3; `#2233` (double-click start dies on leftover `Llm__Gemini__*` variables — reproduced on the published v0.2.0 ZIP, even with no selector), `#2234` (release-page layout), `#2235` (spring-cleaning), `#2236`/`#2237` (v0.4 refactoring and performance) seeded. RC scope: `#2215` A/B + `#2142` + the pre-tag docs; per-proposal batch execute (`#1307`, q-14 C) and the `#2004`/`#1949`/`#1940` remainders in full gate v0.3.0 final.
- **The phone-width E2E blocker is resolved (`#2232`, PR `#2244`, merge `2807c0b5c`).** The 390 px Paper Board lane and card-opener sizing contract was repaired before the RC tag, and `#2232` is closed; the old `a90971162` red-main checkpoint is historical rather than current release state.
- **The RC docs shipped with the tag:** `UPGRADING.md` gained the `v0.3.0-rc.1` section (BREAKING: explicit key scopes for new keys; non-null `errorMessage` on successful captures; archive-card operations block; prerelease tag semantics), the Windows quick start is 0.3.x with an RC notice, `docs/TELEMETRY.md` states that v0.3 sends nothing, and `docs/releases/notes/v0.3.0-rc.1.md` carries the curated highlights.
- **The packaged desktop starts on a profile carrying leftover retired provider variables (`#2233`).** Reproduced on the published `taskdeck-v0.2.0-win-x64.zip`: any Windows profile holding user-scope `Llm__Gemini__*` variables — even with no `Llm__Provider` selector at all — made the double-click exit 1 with `TASKDECK_DESKTOP_FATAL code=retired_provider_configuration`, which is the state of an upgrading machine. In the packaged desktop host only, each environment-variables configuration source is now replaced in its precedence slot by a filtering equivalent that drops the retired `Llm:Gemini` section, a retired `Llm:Provider` selector, and the retired Compose marker before any reader — binding, section enumeration, or the registration-time check — can observe them; the app starts, selection proceeds from whatever supported configuration remains (the packaged `Mock` default for a profile carrying only retired keys, an unaffected live selector where one is validly set), and it announces one value-blind `TASKDECK_DESKTOP_WARNING code=retired_provider_configuration_ignored` line plus an additive `retiredProviderConfigurationIgnored` flag on the provider-status DTO that the chat status bar renders; neither surface claims which provider was selected, because the notice also fires when a stale retired child sits beside a valid live selector. The distinction is made at the configuration-SOURCE level, so retired settings in Taskdeck's own `appsettings.json` / `appsettings.local.json` stay fatal and container / `dotnet run` / CI hosts are untouched. Verified against a locally published packaged-desktop build with the maintainer's own contaminated environment: retired selector + children and children-only both reach `TASKDECK_DESKTOP_READY` with the warning, a clean environment and a stale ambient `Llm__OpenAi__*` pin reach READY with no warning, and the same settings written into `appsettings.local.json` still exit 1 with the unchanged fatal contract. Test evidence: Api.Tests 212 targeted (`RetiredProviderEnvironmentConfiguration`, `DesktopRuntime`, `LlmProviderRegistration`, `FirstRun`) plus the whole Api.Tests project 2567 passed / 4 pre-existing skips, Application.Tests 135 targeted (`ChatService`, `LlmProviderSelectionPolicy`), Architecture.Tests 26 passed / 1 skip, and `scripts/ci/test_windows_desktop_archive.py` 48 passed, with eleven production mutations measured across three review rounds. Review narrowed two claims the first cut overstated: the notice never says which provider was selected (it also fires when a stale retired child sits beside a valid live selector, where that live provider is in force), and it promises only that no retired value was kept, logged, or printed - the filter necessarily reads the `Llm:Provider` value to recognise the retired name. The exception is scoped to the process environment alone: a command-line `--Llm:Provider=Gemini`, Taskdeck's own settings files, and Docker Compose all stay fatal. **Not verified:** the post-ZIP acceptance harness itself needs a real signed archive and was not executed end-to-end; no frontend browser pass. **Not in the published v0.2.0.**

Post-v0.2.0 fixes on `main` (since 2026-08-29):
- **The single-turn `RequestProposal` path now preserves a degraded outcome instead of building a proposal from the user's own text (`#1628`, PR `#2294`, merge `42aec57e2`).** On the non-tool-calling `ChatService` proposal path, when a terminal provider outcome is degraded and the turn requests a proposal, the service preserves the returned and persisted `degraded` classification and reason, returns no proposal id, and calls neither planner nor direct proposal creation. If that degraded fallback itself promises a proposal for an actionable turn, the user instead receives explicit copy that no proposal was created and no board was changed; generic degraded non-action content remains untouched. Non-degraded proposal behavior is unchanged. Tool-calling orchestration is outside this guard: a `propose_*` tool can persist a proposal before a later orchestration round becomes degraded, and `#1628` did not change that path or its response-shaping residual. Evidence: 69 focused `ChatService` tests on the final base, the full backend solution at the feature head (Domain 1,520; Application 4,036; API 2,733 passed / 4 skipped; CLI 147; Architecture 26 passed / 1 skipped; Integration 36), one clean current-base independent review, a clean current-head Codex review, and hosted CI with 21 successes plus 11 declared skips including E2E Smoke. **Not verified:** no live-provider or browser journey; the behavior is proven at the application-service seam.
- **Automatic proposal expiry now respects the archived-board guard (`#2197`).** PR `#2168` gave the interactive decision lanes an archived-board guard (ADR-0063), but both automatic expiry paths — `ProposalHousekeepingWorker.ExpireStaleProposalsAsync` and `AutomationProposalService.ExpireProposalsAsync` — still called `Expire()` and saved without it, so the scheduled worker silently decided pending proposals on archived boards. The guard now lives in the single query both paths read (`IAutomationProposalRepository.GetExpiredAsync`), which returns an `ExpiredProposalSweep` partition: archived-board rows are withheld as a count instead of entities, so no save, notification, or audit row is produced for them. The scheduled worker remains the production operator-reporting lane, and both expiry paths emit the non-secret skip count only on transitions; the worker's `taskdeck.proposals.expired_candidate_count` tag counts only the expirable partition, with `taskdeck.proposals.skipped_archived_board_count` carrying the withheld count separately. Board-less and dangling-board rows stay expirable, and restoring a board makes its withheld proposals expirable again. Verified by Application.Tests 3892/3892, Api.Tests 2538 passed / 4 pre-existing skips, and Architecture.Tests 26 passed / 1 skip, with the query guard and the skip log each mutation-checked. This defect was authored on the v0.3 lane and reached `main` in the `#2196` merge; **it is not present in the published v0.2.0.**
- **Partial transcript dates no longer resolve to a hallucinated year (`#2193`, PR `#2206`).** The extraction prompt now states the capture's calendar day (ADR-0058) and tells the model to resolve a day/month with no year to the first such date on or after it, never to guess a year, and never to emit a date outside the contract's plausibility window; `CaptureTriageOutputContract` gained that window (2 years before / 5 years after the reference) plus a `ReviewDueDateHint` that drops an unparseable or implausible hint and returns an honest note, and `LlmCaptureTriagePrompt.TryParseTasks` applies it so one bad date costs the hint rather than the whole extraction. Found by the 2026-08-29 acceptance run of the published v0.2.0 build, where `gpt-4o-mini` answered "Monday 1 September" (spoken in August 2026) with `2023-09-01` and a format-only check carried it onto a card. Evidence at the PR head: 184 triage-filtered, 3,915 Application, 26 Architecture, and 5 transcript-triage golden-path API tests passed, with the window and the rendered reference date both mutation-checked. **Not verified:** no live-provider run against a real model on this head; the notes the parser now produces are returned at the contract boundary and are not yet surfaced to the reviewer — tracked on `#2210`. The reference date is now the capture's own calendar day rather than the server's UTC day at triage (`CaptureTriageAnchor`, PR `#2515`): both triage workers derive it from the queue row's server-stamped `CreatedAt`, so a retry-after-failure re-enqueue (`CaptureService.cs:342`) days later still resolves partial dates as the capture-time run would, and a capture that reported a client UTC offset resolves against its own local day. The prompt's derivation semantics changed without a `llm-triage.v3` prompt-version bump; that bump is a 14-file change across backend, the frontend provenance classifier, the Windows release-acceptance gate (`scripts/ci/windows_desktop_archive.py:949`), and four docs, and is tracked on `#2211`.
- **The triage degradation notice now renders on degraded successful captures (`#2202`).** Both Inbox surfaces gated their message on a *failed* status, so the notice `#2192` stores was API-visible and shown nowhere; this supersedes the "not yet rendered" sentence in the v0.2.0 block below. A capture whose triage completed carrying an `errorMessage` now renders a caution — that a model reading could not be confirmed, the server's notice **verbatim**, what that means for review, and to check the provider settings — in the Paper triage row and the Legacy detail panel, localized in en/it/es. Neither failure gate was widened: the Paper row keeps its clamped `role="alert"` failure span inside the row button and gains a separate `role="status"` block outside it on the note palette, and the Legacy panel adds a plain block above its unchanged error banner rather than reusing `TdInlineAlert`, which hardcodes `role="alert"`. The shared predicate is a positive allowlist over the three statuses that mean the run completed (`Triaged`/`ProposalCreated`/`Converted`) rather than `!== Failed`, because `LlmRequest.Cancel()` accepts a Failed request and clears nothing — a failed-then-ignored capture surfaces as `Ignored` still carrying its failure text, which a negative rule would re-dress as a friendly degradation notice. The copy never asserts WHICH engine authored the result: `CaptureTriageService.ResolveReuseDegradedNotice` (crash recovery) itself reports the author as unknown, so every mention of the deterministic extractor is conditional, and telling the two apart would need a machine-readable degradation kind from the backend (`#2212`) rather than parsing the server sentence, which the frontend never does. The review sentence is status-specific, because "read it before you apply it" is impossible on `Triaged` and stale on `Converted`. Legacy batch triage also reconciles a cached detail against the list snapshot it just fetched when the row reached a terminal status, so an open panel no longer shows a pre-batch `New`/`Triaging` capture without its notice. **Superseded 2026-09-03 (`#2230`, PR `#2299`, merge `4c6ce5f83`): that reconciliation is now a poll.** The sentence that stood here said it was not one; see the batch-triage polling entry below for what shipped and what remains. Evidence: 38 tests in the three degradation spec files (both surfaces plus the predicate) and 3 new capture-store tests for the batch reconciliation, 239 neighbouring Inbox/capture-store/i18n tests, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, ESLint on the touched files, and two measured mutation checks (suppressing the notice fails 12 tests, all of them the degraded cases, with every clean-success and failed case still green; substituting `!== Failed` fails 5, exactly the failed-then-cancelled, pre-completion and out-of-contract cases). **Still not rendered:** the notice is capture-side only — the Review surface discloses the deterministic producer through proposal provenance but carries no pointer back to this capture-side notice, and the outcome remains prose rather than a machine-readable field, so no surface groups or filters by degradation cause. **Not verified:** no live-provider run and no browser or packaged pass; proven by component tests only.
- **The planner validates permissions before it persists a proposal (`#1433`, PR `#2219`, merge `17ed7d560`).** `AutomationPlannerService` checked `BoardAccessBar.Write` *after* creating the proposal on both the single-instruction and batch planning paths, so a denied request could leave a persisted proposal behind while returning `Forbidden`. Validation now runs first on both paths; the existing `Forbidden` code/message contract is unchanged, and one regression per path pins a denied request to zero `CreateProposalAsync` calls. Verified by the focused Application planner tests — 86/86 passed, re-run after merging `origin/main`. **Not verified:** the full backend solution and hosted CI were not run locally. **Not in the published v0.2.0.**
- **The unscoped CLI card move validates the target column's board (`#2125`, PR `#2218`, merge `e81fb0f92`).** `CardService.MoveCardAsync` — the path the CLI uses, not the board-scoped HTTP path — checked neither that the target column belonged to the same board nor whether the target board was archived. A foreign active target now returns `NotFound` and an archived target board `InvalidOperation`; neither path saves or relocates the card, and board-scoped HTTP and proposal behaviour are unchanged. Verified by Application `CardServiceTests` 39 passed and CLI `CardsCommandTests` 13 passed (error code, exit code, unchanged source-card position). **Not verified:** the full backend suite and hosted CI locally; the target-board lookup assumes normal column-to-board referential integrity. **Not in the published v0.2.0.**
- **The Windows launcher no longer reports a false transient-cleanup failure after a completed npm stage (`#2161`, PR `#2221`, merge `ae38d9f43`).** `Invoke-NpmStage` re-read `Process.HasExited` in its `finally` block; that second query can race on Windows and set `TransientStageCleanupUnproved`, retaining `dev-up.pids` after the API port was already released. `scripts/dev-up.ps1` now records the completion established by its bounded `WaitForExit(100)` loop, so only an incomplete — including cancellation-interrupted — wait enters the identity-proven transient cleanup path. Verified by the focused PowerShell transactional suite 6/6 (9.25 s) and the full source launcher suite 85/85 (303.15 s); hosted CI had not run for the PR. **The flake family recurred on `main` at `aadbe6eb2` after this merge, so `#2161` stays open** — this fix removed one proven race, not the family. **Not in the published v0.2.0.**
- **Enter creates the board in the Workspace setup surface (`#1933`, PR `#2222`, merge `683d9566c`).** `WorkspaceSetupModal.vue` is a semantic form, and the board-name input handles `keydown.enter.prevent` so the Enter key invokes the guarded submit routine once and suppresses implicit duplicate submission; Close/Cancel remain non-submit controls, and the submitting lock and Escape cancellation are preserved. Verified at head `824030c10` by `WorkspaceSetupModal.spec.ts` 6/6, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, and targeted ESLint. **Not verified:** no live browser, packaged desktop, or screen-reader run. **Residual:** an IME-composition case is not covered, so `#1933` stays open. **Not in the published v0.2.0.**
- **The two approved due-date fields share one `TdDateField` primitive (`#2006`, PR `#2225`, merge `9d1b9c565`).** The new shared native primitive opens the browser date picker on a field click while preserving the native input's typing, focus, Escape, change, ARIA, class, and disabled behaviour; `PaperCaptureComposer` and `CardModalForm` now use it. Verified by 3 spec files / 50 tests (`TdDateField`, `PaperCaptureComposer`, `CardModal`), `npm run typecheck`, and `npm run build`. **Not verified:** manual Chromium/Safari/Firefox picker interaction and the full frontend Vitest suite; native picker rendering and keyboard segment interaction remain browser-owned by design, and browsers without `showPicker()` keep their existing native behaviour. **Not in the published v0.2.0.**
- **Archive-card proposal operations block the card with a generated reason instead of silently doing nothing (`#2185`, PR `#2216`, merge `f54d88143`).** `OperationHandlerRegistry` maps archive-card to `IsBlocked=true` with `Archived by an approved proposal.`; the readable diff shows the exact blocked-state transition and advances its working card state across sequential archives; and the shared ordered-operation contract now rejects writes after a planned board archive, so Preview == Apply holds for the board-archive-then-card-archive sequence that previously previewed a transition Apply rolled back. Verified by focused Application tests 208/208 after the fix rounds plus `node scripts/check-docs-governance.mjs`; `autodoc/interfaces/proposal-operation-vocabulary.md` was updated. **Not verified:** the full backend solution run was started but never returned a terminal summary. **Two review MEDIUMs remain open on `#2229`:** the post-board-archive validator guard is broader than Apply, and a true-to-true block-reason overwrite audits as "no fields changed". **Not in the published v0.2.0.**
- **Displayed keyboard shortcuts are truthful and centrally registered (`#2008`, `#1968`, PR `#2226`, merge `67278775d`).** One shared platform-aware formatter and one exported binding registry now feed both the Paper key ledger and `AppShell`: canonical mod descriptors render as Command glyphs on Apple and `Ctrl` elsewhere, `H`/`T`/`B`/`I`/`R` and a bounded one-second `G T` chord are implemented from real global keydown events with input, textarea, select, contenteditable, and IME suppression, the false "Settings → Keyboard/remappable" claim is gone, every ledger row is tied to an implemented handler, and the inactive `C`/`R` glyphs were removed from the Paper board actions with their click routes preserved. The board-local `h` binding was removed rather than left shadowed — `AppShell` handles workspace shortcuts in the capture phase — so the ledger advertises Left for previous-column navigation. Verified by 12 spec files / 230 tests, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, and targeted ESLint. **Not verified:** real-browser checks on physical macOS/Windows keyboards, the full Vitest catalogue, hosted CI at that head, and the `P` provenance handler; Legacy help residuals and two quarantined source literals (`PaperInboxView.vue`, `useReviewKeymap.ts`) keep `#2008`/`#1968` open. **Not in the published v0.2.0.**
- **The release lane publishes prerelease tags as GitHub prereleases and keeps the floating tags off them (`#2217`, PR `#2223`, merge `ecc02a6ae`).** `release-desktop.yml`'s `resolve-source` derives a `prerelease` output from the same validated tag string the run is pinned to and threads it into both `gh release create` and the `--draft=false` flip, so `draft=false` and `prerelease=true` travel in one PATCH and an RC is never momentarily the full release; `release-container.yml` additionally gates GHCR `latest` and the `{{major}}.{{minor}}` alias on `!contains(github.ref_name, '-')`. Verified by `node --test scripts/ci/release-desktop-dispatch.test.mjs` 66 pass / 0 fail (63 before), a mutation check that turns it 65/1 when the prerelease flag is dropped, real execution of the changed `resolve-source` and create/publish run blocks against a stubbed `gh` (stable-tag argv byte-identical to the pre-change form), a YAML parse of both workflows, and `node scripts/check-docs-governance.mjs`. **Not verified: the tag workflows themselves were never run** — a blank-tag rehearsal skips `create-release` entirely, so only a real tag exercises the changed calls; no GHCR push was made; `actionlint`/`shellcheck` are unavailable on this box; and `docker/metadata-action@v6`'s runtime handling of `enable=` is taken from documentation. Merged 2026-08-29 from the agent lane under the repo's declared authority (`.agent-harness/tier.json` `merge: free`; CODEOWNERS is advisory), overriding the PR body's "maintainer merge required" note. **Not in the published v0.2.0**; it unblocks the `v0.3.0-rc.1` tag.

v0.2.0 SHIPPED (2026-08-29, tag at `48c05e1dc`):
- **The public v0.2.0 release exists**, under the maintainer's explicit in-session acceptance of the v0.2.0 release deck on 2026-08-29 (guided-walkthrough reply q-1 B; deck record `#1947` comment 5459928603). Annotated tag `v0.2.0` peels to `48c05e1dcd3cc8d3072ba60f0e89258d25ac4422` (the merge commit of the pre-tag docs PR `#2184`, on top of candidate `927236bd0`); the tag was created and pushed by the agent under that reply's authorization — the same reply ruled release mechanics agent-executable (`.agent-harness/tier.json` notes). All four tag workflows completed SUCCESS: Release Desktop 33234264631, Release Container 33234264595, Release Security 33234264652, CI Release 33234264655. The public Release (published 2026-08-29T04:40:53Z) carries `taskdeck-v0.2.0-win-x64.zip` (SHA-256 `445927ca4b608ca9c0a56ad8f3fb55a5002150c1b25b37c3d76c0bc00513604d`), its `.sha256` sidecar, and `taskdeck-v0.2.0-provenance.txt`; the curated highlights were prepended to the auto-generated notes; milestone `v0.2 — Coherent Context-to-Action Loop` closed at 0 open / 15 closed.
- **Exact-head evidence at `48c05e1dc`:** required CI SUCCESS, a fresh blank-tag no-publish rehearsal SUCCESS whose artifact carried the 0.2.x in-zip quick start, and an isolated packaged-exe journey PASS (register → board → capture → triage → approve with the board unchanged → apply → board 1 → 2) with the zip hash unchanged. Post-publish: the published zip re-hashed against its sidecar, its `QUICK_START.md` verified as 0.2.x, and `/health/live` reporting `0.2.0`.
- **Post-tag fix on `main`, not in the v0.2.0 artifact (`#2192`):** a transcript triage whose LLM leg was attempted but could not deliver no longer falls back silently. Every such outcome — kill switch, an unavailable provider, exhausted quota, a degraded provider (the class covering a failed live request, an unknown model, a timeout, and an open circuit, since the providers return those degraded rather than throwing), unusable output, and an extractor exception — now records one notice naming the outcome (`LLM triage unavailable (<Outcome>); using deterministic extractor`) on the capture's `errorMessage`, while the item still completes and burns no retry. **A mock substituted for a *requested* live provider counts as unavailable, not as an offline setup:** when `EnableLiveProviders` is on and selection rejects the configured provider (invalid settings, an environment that declines live providers, an unrecognized selector), `LlmProviderSelectionPolicy` now marks the decision as a diversion and triage reports it, so a misconfigured live deployment is recorded on the capture rather than only in a startup log. Only a deliberate mock — `Provider=Mock`, or live providers switched off — stays silent. The published detail is deliberately coarse; the precise cause, which can name a configured `BaseUrl`, stays in the log, and an unexpected extractor exception publishes a fixed message rather than its own text. The proposal-reuse replay recovers the record from the authoring run's stamped provenance, so a crash between proposal commit and capture stamp no longer completes clean. Proposal provenance and confidence were already honest on `main` after the v0.3 lane's `MakeProvenanceConfidenceHonest` work (`deterministic-extractor` / `capture-triage-v1`, `ProvenanceConfidenceSource.Deterministic`, no model-style number) and are now regression-pinned. Backend only: **the Inbox surfaces still gate their message on a failed status, so the notice is stored and API-visible but not yet rendered** — the frontend half is tracked as `#2202`, so `#2192` stays open. `UPGRADING.md` records the contract change: a Completed/Triaged capture can now carry `errorMessage`, where previously only a Failed one could. The map-chunk budget decline asks the same guardrail chain first, so a merely long transcript on a deliberately offline deployment reports Disabled or ProviderIsMock rather than manufacturing a notice. Evidence: Application 3,925 passed, Domain 1,480 passed, Api 2,535 passed / 4 skipped, Architecture 26 passed / 1 skipped, plus five measured mutation checks (suppressing the notice fails 13 service tests; ignoring a mock diversion fails 3 extractor tests; skipping the pre-budget guard chain fails 4; restoring the raw exception message fails 2; dropping the worker wiring fails 1). Not verified: a live-provider run against a real misconfigured model.
- **Not verified at the tag:** the live-OpenAI transcript loop on this exact head (verified on `3adde232b`; the delta since is docs plus the CI-green `#2144`/`#2145` slices), graceful shutdown of the packaged exe, the non-headless first run, real screen reader / real phone, signing (the release is unsigned by design — `#2148` ruling: SignPath first, no signing gate before v0.3.x).

Post-v0.2.0 Review-queue liveness (2026-08-29):
- **An open Review page now keeps its own queue current (`#2194`, PR `#2208`).** The queue was read once on route entry and never again, so a proposal created server-side while the page sat open stayed behind "Nothing waiting. Good." until the user navigated away and back - the dogfooding run measured 115 s of `QUEUE - 0 AWAITING` against an API already reporting 1 pending. There is no realtime channel to subscribe to: `BoardsHub` is the only hub, its groups are strictly per-board, it emits only `boardMutation` / `boardPresence` / `toolStatus` over board/card/column/label entities, and `AutomationProposalService` never touches `IBoardRealtimeNotifier` - so proposal creation is silent on the wire, and the all-boards queue has no board group to join. The frontend therefore polls: `useReviewProposals` gained `startQueueRefresh`/`stopQueueRefresh` (mirroring the existing `startClock` pair, double-start guard and `onScopeDispose` included) driving a 15 s read that runs only while `document.visibilityState === 'visible'`, reads immediately on tab re-entry, stops on route leave, and is held by a surface-supplied predicate while an action is in flight or a confirm dialog is open. The background read never raises `proposalsLoading`, defers to any explicit load instead of racing it, stays silent on failure (logged, not swallowed), and preserves a `#proposal-` deep-link target that the list page omits. Both Review skins are wired, kept in step so they cannot drift. The shell's `Review - N` badge - a home-summary workload count `AppShell` fetches once at sign-in and nothing on Review ever refreshed - is now re-read whenever the awaiting count moves, via the version-guarded `refreshWorkloadCounts`; the "This week" cadence bars are a pure projection of the same proposals array and follow the refresh with no extra wiring.
- **Hardened in review before merge.** A stale read could revert a decision: every single-proposal path (approve/reject/defer/execute) patches the row locally with a new array and never touches the load counter, so a read issued before the click could land after it and write `PendingReview` back over a row whose receipt already said approved. The poll now snapshots the queue's array identity before its await and discards its answer if the reference moved, and it re-evaluates the surface guard **after** the await so a read cannot land under a dialog opened while it was in flight (the Reject dialog is bound to a computed that closes itself when its proposal leaves the list, discarding a half-typed reason). A `403` is now distinguished from a transient failure - board access was revoked, so the poll stops rather than hammering, unauthorised rows are dropped, and both skins say why, because clearing the queue silently would swap a permission failure for a fresh "Nothing waiting. Good."; a `500` leaves the queue alone and keeps polling. The deep-link pin is restored at its `createdAt` position rather than appended, so the record under review no longer jumps to the bottom of a rail that renders array order. The poll opts out of the shared three-retry interceptor and carries an `AbortController` cancelled by `stopQueueRefresh`.
- **What is both skins, and what is Paper only.** The bounded poll, the badge refresh, the polite `role="status"` live region for the awaiting count, and the revoked-access message are wired in **both** Paper and Legacy - Legacy had no badge wiring at all and no tests for any of it, and it renders no translated strings, so its copy is plain text like the rest of that file. The badge trigger is the queue **array**, not the awaiting count: watching the count missed a board-scoped view, where another board's proposal never moves the scoped number, and a decision that swaps one pending row for another. The "This week" cadence bars are **Paper only** (`ReviewMiniCadence`), and they follow the refresh with no extra wiring because they project the same proposals array.
- **Evidence:** 563 tests across the 25 review, Legacy-Review, i18n-catalog and source-guard spec files, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, and ESLint over the eleven touched files, all green. `scripts/ci/check-bundle-size.mjs` passes: eager JS **385.90 KB** against the 1250 KB hard gate (`origin/main` measures 385.61 KB); its total-JS report-ceiling warning is warning-only and **pre-existing on `origin/main`** at 1314.76 KB, which this change moves to 1318.61 KB. Seven mutation checks each killed their target test and only that test - dropping the decision-identity guard, skipping the post-await guard re-check, appending rather than positioning the deep-link pin, treating 403 as transient, dropping the deep-link pin entirely, ignoring the visibility guard, and removing the badge watch. Introducing the poll first broke three existing expiry specs, whose `mountView` fixture answered the queue with a single `...Once`; a real server keeps answering, so the fixture now does too.
- **Polling restarts after permission recovery (`#2214` item 2, PR `#2298`, merge `bd2541991`).** A current-scope `403` now suspends the Review interval without discarding the owning surface's refresh predicate or queue-replacement hook. The first successful explicit load that proves access has returned clears the revoked-access state and rearms exactly one interval; a permanent stop or scope disposal remains final, so a late recovery response cannot resurrect polling after route leave. Both Review skins inherit the repair through the shared composable. Evidence: 97 focused and 148 broader Review tests, the full frontend suite (357 files / 5,270 tests), typecheck, scoped zero-warning ESLint, build, diff hygiene, clean current-base review, and hosted CI with 21 successes plus 11 declared skips. **This is a bounded delivery, not closure of `#2214`:** deep-link re-authorization, count-neutral replacement announcements, and the recorded low-impact accessibility/diagnostic residuals remain open. **Two items on this list were delivered later by PR `#2388` (merge `4b8075632`) and are struck here so the two entries do not contradict each other:** per-request deadlines (`REVIEW_QUEUE_REQUEST_DEADLINE_MS = 8_000`, `useReviewProposals.ts:53`, raced at `:590`) and the degraded queue disclosure (`consecutiveQueueRefreshFailures` against `REVIEW_QUEUE_CONSECUTIVE_FAILURE_THRESHOLD`, `:543-560`, cleared at `:519-520`). The **stale** copy's Paper localization is still open as `#2214` item 4, since it is hardcoded English. **Not verified:** no live ACL revoke/re-grant browser journey or screen-reader pass.
- **Not verified:** no live browser or packaged run - the 115 s repro was not re-measured against a running stack, the revoked-access path was not exercised against a real 403, and no screen reader was used to hear the live region; the fix is proven by component and composable tests only. The empty-state copy is unchanged: with the refresh in place "Nothing waiting" can no longer be minutes stale while the page is being looked at, so the acceptance bullet's "or is refreshed" branch is taken rather than its copy branch.
- **Background Review queue requests are deadline-bound and degrade politely (`#2214`, PR `#2388`, merge `4b8075632`).** Each background queue request is bound to an **8-second deadline**; three consecutive transient failures preserve the last trustworthy queue and expose a polite degraded notice rather than blanking the list, and any successful poll or explicit load clears it. `403` authority, teardown and supersession semantics are unchanged, and both Legacy and Paper render the state without hiding retained actions or disturbing the Paper three-column layout. Evidence: 293 focused tests across three spec files, 180 fix-diff affected specs, typecheck, build, scoped ESLint, docs and GitHub-ops governance, diff hygiene. Initial fresh-context review found two HIGH layout/conditional defects; one bounded fix commit resolved both and the fix-diff review found no blocker. **Known MEDIUM, deferred:** the Paper stale copy is hardcoded English, carried on `#2214` item 4. **Recorded 2026-09-03 by the coordinator, not by PR `#2388`:** that PR's body claimed it had updated this file, and it did not - merge `4b8075632` changed nothing in `docs/STATUS.md`. **Not verified:** full frontend suite, Playwright or manual browser, live degraded backend, screen reader, translation quality.
- **Legacy batch triage polls to a terminal detail (`#2230`, PR `#2299`, merge `4c6ce5f83`).** `pollBatchTriageCompletion` will not accept cached terminal state as completion before its first authoritative read: `observedPostEnqueueList` starts false, the completion predicate's summary branch returns false while it is unset, and it is set true only after a successful `captureApi.listItems` inside a tick. That was the defect the PR parked on twice. Merged with full exact-head CI green including `E2E Smoke`, both Windows legs and all three branch-protection contexts, after one fresh-context adversarial pass returned no blocker. **`#2230` is deliberately NOT closed.** The poll's own 60-second wall (`BATCH_TRIAGE_POLL_MAX_DURATION_MS`) is shorter than the server's worst case: the worker drains at most `MaxBatchSize` items total per tick and then sleeps `QueuePollIntervalSeconds` (shipped: 5 and 5, shared between capture and non-capture work), while the server accepts batches up to 50, so a 50-item batch needs at least 50 seconds of pure queue delay before any LLM latency. Expiry is silent - no toast, no error state, no exposed ref - so a large batch can expire back into the cached `Triaging` detail that `#2230` exists to remove. The single-item poll allows 15 minutes for strictly less work. Remaining acceptance is on the issue. Two Codex P2s about `403` handling were declined as unreachable: `GET /capture/items` is user-scoped with no board authorization and `GET /capture/items/{id}` returns `403` only on a user mismatch, so board-membership revocation produces no `403` on either read. **Not verified:** the timing above is arithmetic over committed configuration, not an observed batch run.
- **The active proposal no longer changes under a live reviewer (`#2215` parts A and B, the `#2208` residual).** A poll that dropped or reordered away the row on screen used to slide the selection onto the next pending proposal: `ReviewMain` is keyed on the proposal id, so it was re-created and focus left the decision controls, while the window-level review keymap stayed enabled for the newly selected record - the next Enter/Backspace/D/E decided a proposal the reviewer never chose. `useReviewProposals` now signals a landed poll (`startQueueRefresh`'s `onQueueReplaced` hook, fired synchronously after the queue is replaced and cleared by `stopQueueRefresh`); Paper arms a one-shot flag from it, so an active-proposal change observed while armed is by definition not one the reviewer made. It pins the taken proposal, renders an explicit "This proposal left the review queue" notice in place of the decision column (localized en/it/es, `role="status"` + `aria-live="polite"`), and moves focus to the notice's own control. What silences the keymap is `activeProposal` being null in that state - `useReviewKeymap`'s `enabled` predicate requires a non-null active proposal - while the focus move is what stops focus falling to the document body and gives the reviewer a safe target; the two are independent. The copy states only what was observed - the row left the queue - because one poll answer is not proof of a decision: the list endpoint also omits a PendingReview proposal whose `deferredUntil` is in the future, and it is capped at 200 rows. The notice's action re-reads the queue authoritatively rather than only dismissing itself, and no fetch-by-id was added. A deep-linked target is unaffected: `refreshProposals` already pins it. The notice is dropped by any explicit choice - selecting a row, changing filter, changing board scope, following a deep link, or its own "Back to the queue" button, which also clears the stale explicit selection so the ordinary first-pending default resumes. **Part B:** the rendered diff and the revision state are keyed on `latestRevisionId` as well as the proposal id, in **both** skins. Keying on the id alone left a diff on screen that had been computed for the previous revision once a poll brought in one another session had saved, while Approve pins and Apply executes the server's latest; and `useProposalRevisions` kept `revisionCount` and the cached `latestRevision` from page entry, so `editablePayload` offered superseded operations to the next save. The revision resync is deliberately a read, not the full reset the proposal-change path does - closing an open composer over somebody else's change would discard a half-written edit. **Evidence:** 933 tests across all 46 composable and i18n spec files plus 502 across the 21 review/Legacy-Review/i18n/source-guard files, `npm run typecheck` and `npm run build`, all green. Seventeen mutation checks, each killing its target specs and only those: removing the selection guard (4), disarming the poll signal (4), removing the focus move (1), disabling and then forcing the Paper diff's revision comparison (1 each), dropping `latestRevisionId` from the revision-state key (1), disabling and then widening the resync branch (1 and 4), disabling then forcing the Legacy pane's revision comparison (1 and 3), and - from review round 1 - republishing a failed resync as authoritative (1), making that resync loud (1), deleting the no-op key guard (1), and dropping the notice's queue re-read (1). Round 2 adds four more: dropping the null-transition rule (1), dropping the `approvedRevisionId` fallback (1), dropping both (4), and the three round-2 specs staying green against the restored rule. Round 1 also fixed a real defect the first pass shipped: the resync left `revisionsLoaded` true, so ONE transient GET failure under a poll would have published a zero count as authoritative - a false zero-op Apply toast and a fallback to pre-revision operations - and it toasted from a background poll, against `refreshProposals`' silent doctrine. It now clears `revisionsLoaded` first and loads `{ silent: true }`. **Round 2 caught the sharper one, verified against the backend:** `latestRevisionId` is a PendingReview-ONLY value on the wire - `AutomationProposalService.BuildEffectiveProposalDto` sets it to `Status == PendingReview ? effectiveRevision?.Id : null` - so approving a revised proposal moves it `rev-X` -> `null` in the very next read, and all three keys read the reviewer's OWN approval as a collaborator revision change. In Paper that wiped the open diff mid-decision, and because the key watcher runs before the read-only conversion watcher it wiped the pane rather than letting it convert to the decision-time stored presentation. All three now key on an EFFECTIVE identity - `latestRevisionId ?? approvedRevisionId`, shared as `proposalRevisionIdentity` in `utils/proposalIdentity.ts` - and clear only on a genuine move (`proposalRevisionMoved`): an identity reaching null is a status transition, never a revision change, because revisions are append-only and of the exits only `Approve` pins a replacement while `Reject`/`Expire`/`Dismiss` pin nothing.
