Both halves of the rule are separately load-bearing: dropping the null rule reddens the rejected-proposal conversion spec, and dropping the `approvedRevisionId` fallback reddens the stale-pre-approval-read spec. **Not verified:** no live browser or packaged run; no two-session concurrency repro. **Out of scope, still open on `#2215`:** part C beyond the revision-state resync delivered here, and the deep-review selectors (provenance, side effects, conflicts, history), which still key on the proposal id alone.
- **Split residual, resolved by deletion (`#2205`, PR `#2413`, merge `b74b8d688`).** The queue rail's **Apply rate** was a hardcoded empty rather than a staleness bug: `PaperReviewView` never bound `ReviewQueueRail`'s optional `applyRate` prop, so `applyRatePct` was always `null` and "No decisions yet" rendered however many decisions had been made. No refresh could fix it, so the metric was removed from `ReviewQueueRail.vue` and the three locale catalogs instead of being wired up. `#2205` and `#2194` are both closed. The unrelated `applyRate` on `proposalDeepReviewApi` (similar past decisions) is a different field and still exists.

v0.2.0 release preparation (2026-08-29, candidate `main` `927236bd0`):
- **The two closing slices for the last v0.2 milestone issues are merged.** PR `#2145` (merge `cd878e262`) closed `#1938`: a failed capture now carries a real diagnostics payload (HTTP status, method+endpoint, Taskdeck `errorCode`, `X-Request-Id`) in both the error toast and the inline receipt, with aria wiring and per-variant receipts; follow-ups tracked on `#2147`, `#2142`, `#2146`. PR `#2144` (merge `e5c276e59`) closed `#1305`: the never-mapped RFAI-02 intent-envelope vocabulary (8 types, 2 interfaces, the `proposal-batch.v1` schema, 158 dead tests) is deleted, architecture invariant INV-12 is rewired onto the shipped `ProvenanceEvidenceLink` chain with five integrity guards, and the 200,000-character transcript cap plus export/deletion retention are documented. PR `#2153` (merge `927236bd0`) added the Windows-first release-trust programme (`docs/ops/RELEASE_TRUST_AND_DISTRIBUTION.md`, backlog `#2148`–`#2152`, human items RT-1–RT-3/CL-1/BEN-1/DIST-1) with no behavior change. Milestone `v0.2 — Coherent Context-to-Action Loop` holds **0 open / 15 closed**; every closure traces to a merge on `main` (none to `integration/v0.3.0`).
- **Candidate evidence at `927236bd0`:** required CI SUCCESS (run 33111961772); blank-tag no-publish Release Desktop rehearsal SUCCESS (run 33228952652 — resolve/build/win-x64 green, publish skipped); an isolated synthetic desktop journey on that rehearsal's artifact PASSED (register → board with one card → board-scoped capture → Mock triage → Review `.96` proposal → Approve with the board still unchanged → explicit Apply → board 1 → 2 cards; zip SHA-256 identical before and after). **The tag target is not `927236bd0` itself but the merge commit of the pre-tag docs PR `#2184`** — the release workflow copies `docs/releases/WINDOWS_QUICK_START.md` into the zip and byte-compares it (`release-desktop.yml:365,437`), and only that descendant carries the 0.2.x guide. Before the tag, required CI and the blank-tag rehearsal are repeated at that commit; the packaged-exe journey above is candidate-only evidence unless re-run there. The maintainer accepted the v0.2.0 release deck in-session on 2026-08-29 (reply q-1 B, recorded on #1947) and ruled that release mechanics — tag creation and push, workflow observation, Release publication, milestone closure — are agent-executable; the agent pushes the tag once the tag head is re-proven. Known red outside the gate: the optional nightly `mobile-safari` E2E lane has failed since 2026-08-24 (`#2180`, pre-dates v0.1.2). Also found by the journey and tracked, not blocking: headless packaged starts need an external connector key and misreport the cause (`#2181`). Also found by the review of the ADR briefs and tracked, not blocking but named in the release notes: an archive-card proposal operation applies as a silent no-op — `OperationHandlerRegistry.ArchiveCardAsync` sends `IsBlocked=true` with a null reason, which `CardService.UpdateCardAsync` ignores while reporting success (`#2185`, pre-existing since v0.1.x).
- **Correction to the 2026-08-27 `#2141` finding below:** the transcript loop *is* completable inside the Paper skin — the shared shell's command palette (`Ctrl/Cmd+K` → **New Capture**) and `Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+C` open the shared `CaptureModal`, whose Transcript tab creates a board-less transcript capture that lands on the Paper Inbox and its Ask-AI board picker (`AppShell.vue:83-88`, `:145-147`, `:317-323`; spec-covered in `AppShell.paperVariant.spec.ts`). What remains of `#2141` is a discoverability/consistency gap: no Paper-native transcript surface (the Paper nib/composer is Typed-only) and the palette keywords never mention "transcript". Verified from source and committed specs, not a live run.

v0.3 integration closeout checkpoint (2026-08-28; branch `integration/v0.3.0`, last product merge head `778a9f847`):
- **Merged into `main` on 2026-08-29, after the v0.2.0 tag** (this line was added in the merge commit; the checkpoint below is preserved as written on 2026-08-28). Its "promotion is deliberately not authorized yet" statement is superseded by the maintainer's 2026-08-29 in-session authorization to merge the v0.3 lane once v0.2.0 shipped; `v0.2.0` now exists (tag at `48c05e1dc`) and the v0.3 features below are on `main` from this merge onward.
- **The bounded 2026-08-28 integration wave is closed and saved.** PR `#2177` repaired the Windows launcher substrate, PR `#2174` delivered explicit MCP key capabilities, PR `#2178` synchronized the operator-facing capability truth, and PR `#2179` repaired delayed Paper Review focus ownership. Their history-preserving integration merges culminate at `778a9f847`. No additional implementation lane was opened during closeout.
- **Paper Home now has one runtime-proven action path (`#1949`, PR `#2175`).** The seeded capture loop activates the visible carryover/triage card, lands on `/workspace/inbox`, and re-proves the capture row. The exact head passed local Chromium 2/2, PaperHomeView 34/34, lint, typecheck, build, and diff hygiene plus hosted Required CI (21 success, 11 declared skips); merge `2033917a7` preserves the feature commit. Home-summary refresh timing and identity-matched row/card assertions remain MEDIUM test-quality residuals on open `#1949`; the complementary proposal-card → exact Paper Review record journey was not started.
- **Explicit MCP key capabilities are merged on the v0.3 line (`#1309`, PR `#2174`).** New API, UI, and CLI keys require an explicit non-empty combination of independent `read`, `propose`, and `manage` capabilities; migrated legacy keys remain **Full**. HTTP MCP discovery and invocation enforce those capabilities inside request scopes, reject missing/unknown targets before lookup, and reject invalid persisted masks before identity projection, per-key charging, or usage writes. After the launcher repair in PR `#2177`, final head `ec1f833b9` passed 71 affected HTTP/stdio/identity/rate-limit cases, fresh-base review, a Release solution build, and the hosted rollup (21 success, 11 declared skips, including Windows launcher/API and E2E); merge `13c5e7032` preserves the feature commits. A real Claude Code stdio call against synthetic data passed at the fixed feature head; it was not rerun after the launcher-only base merge. Runtime tool-hash approval, public release proof, and the wider dogfooding acceptance remain open on `#1309`, now Project **Next** / Priority II.
- **Paper Review now preserves an intentionally focused connected control when an asynchronous decision completes (`#1940`, PR `#2179`).** Body/document fallback still moves focus to Apply or the terminal receipt. The focused Review suite passed 139/139 locally, with typecheck, scoped ESLint, diff hygiene, exact-head review, and all hosted required lanes green; merge `778a9f847` preserves the feature commit. Hosted E2E smoke is not dedicated browser or screen-reader proof of the focus seam, and no human acceptance is claimed.
- **The saved `#1940` Provenance-shortcut slice later shipped on `main` (`#2323`, merge `221aa88c8`).** Unmodified lowercase/uppercase `P` now opens and closes the active proposal's existing Provenance disclosure instead of emitting the obsolete unwired toast. Manual activation shares the proposal-local controlled state, proposal identity changes reset it, and confidence/similar-history disclosures plus decision/receipt behavior remain independent. The checkpoint regression failed red before implementation; the final head passed 227 focused Review/keymap/provenance tests, typecheck, production build, zero-warning scoped ESLint, diff hygiene, exact-head independent and Codex review with no CRITICAL/HIGH, and 21 hosted successes with 11 declared skips including E2E. No dedicated browser or screen-reader pass targeted this shortcut. Two MEDIUM follow-ups remain on open `#1940`: suppress `P` while the Provenance drawer owns focus, and provide visible/live-announced feedback for shortcut changes to an off-screen disclosure. The issue is Project **Next** / Priority I.
- **Promotion is deliberately not authorized yet.** At this checkpoint `main` is `927236bd0`, no remote `v0.2.0` tag or GitHub release exists, milestone `v0.3 — Open Beta + Accountable Agents` has 20 open and 1 closed issue, and parked PR `#2165` is the only open PR targeting the integration branch. Final integration → `main` waits for the v0.2 release and a v0.3 completion audit.
- **The remaining queue has explicit restart boundaries.** `#1309` has a LOW archive-validator candidate, but the terminal-newline contract must first choose LF-only versus LF/CRLF; `#1307` remains owner-decision gated (`#1992`'s last open decision was ruled on 2026-08-30 as v0.3 RC deck q-10 A — ADR-0064); `#1949` has no acceptance-ready next contract; and `#2090` remains Blocked behind parked PR `#2165`, with its recovery worktree preserved.

Acceptance walkthrough + dogfooding re-scope (2026-08-27, maintainer-directed agent run on main `3adde232b`):
- **Dogfooding is a standing tracker, not a release gate (maintainer ruling, recorded on `#1271`).** The maintainer's own assessment after using v0.1.2: it is not yet enough for his real workflow — features and polish come first. `#1271` is unmilestoned and stays open as the standing tracker for the dogfooding *practice* (usage days, sprint findings, the pivot's acceptance signal); `#1947` keeps its distinct role as the standing tracker for the 2026-08-22 UX-sweep *finding backlog* — two trackers, two subjects, neither gates a release. The ≥10-day floor survives only as a long-horizon signal; this exercises the checkpoint rule's "mixed outcome → explicit maintainer assessment" branch and is explicitly **not** an archive trigger. `REVIVAL_PLAN.md` (horizon checkpoint row, §4, §6(i), §8) and `docs/dogfooding/LOG.md` carry the same record.
- **The transcript→proposals→evidence→apply loop is live-verified end-to-end, and `#1304` (REVIVAL-08) is closed on its acceptance matrix.** On a fresh isolated instance with live OpenAI at the shipped default `gpt-5.6-luna`: a speaker-labeled transcript produced **7 typed create-card operations** (verbatim quotes as descriptions, the recorded decision extracted, the explicitly-parked question correctly not extracted, confidence .81 with breakdown), all 7 evidence spans resolved to "View in transcript" opening the stored transcript highlighted at the exact span, and two-phase approve/apply landed all 7 cards atomically. The degraded contract was also live-proven: a misconfigured model yielded "LLM triage unavailable (InvalidOutput)" and an honestly-labeled `deterministic-extractor/capture-triage-v1` fallback proposal, never an exception. Code-level: every M1–M3 AC mapped to main with its tests **re-run** (118 + 5 passed). That walkthrough predates `#1307` AC1, so its ".81 with breakdown" is historical evidence, not a claim about the current confidence implementation. At integration merge `41441ae` (PR `#2160`), validated v2 per-item confidence crosses an application-only, HTTP-inaccessible seam into nullable, source-labelled proposal provenance and Paper Review: model-reported values remain evidence, deterministic/not-reported paths carry no number, and confidence never gates Approve or Apply. That newer path is test-verified but was not re-run live in this walkthrough. v2 `type` and `assigneeHint` remain transient; `dueDateHint` now materializes into the reviewed create-card proposal parameters before Apply. AC2 risk ordering had already shipped via PR `#1648` / merge `61f7cd53`: the queue is stable Low → Medium → High → Critical, and unknown risk fails safe to Critical. At integration merge `07a848b8` (PR `#2173`), the batch-approval half of AC3 became fail-closed: stale, unauthorized, or otherwise invalid selections reject the whole approval request before mutation, and approval never executes or applies proposal operations or changes user-visible board content. Explicit batch execute and its public atomic-versus-partial failure contract remain open. Other recorded caveats are unchanged: dedupe is a title-set, not the idempotency-key scheme; `#1987`/`#1284` drawer metadata still dark. M4 stays gated and files its own issue when taken.
- **`#1967` is closed on live verification.** Applied-card `Open Proposal` renders exactly the hash-named proposal in `APPLIED · READ-ONLY`; RECENTLY APPLIED rows are native buttons opening the read-only record; no silent substitution anywhere. Thread-only residuals were transferred to `#2130` (with `#2121` still open) before closing.
- **New findings filed from the run:** `#2141` — transcript capture has no Paper-*native* entry point (the Paper nib/composer hard-codes `source: 'Typed'`); *as recorded that day the finding overstated this as "not completable within one skin" — see the 2026-08-29 correction above*; `#2142` — the 401 hard navigation destroys the preserved capture draft and its failure receipt (a hole in `#1938`'s "text never lost" for the auth-expiry class). Also observed live: the `#1938` failure path now preserves the draft with a persistent, aria-associated inline receipt and a non-dismissing error toast — but the toast's details expander is dead (nothing populates `toast.details`), which the in-flight `#1938` diagnostics slice closes.
- **Closing slices for the two remaining v0.2 milestone issues went in flight the same session:** `#1938` capture-failure diagnostics (frontend) and `#1305` orphan intent-envelope vocabulary removal + transcript-cap documentation (backend+docs); their delivery records land with their merges.

DCO enforcement pause (2026-08-23, explicit maintainer decision; restore tracker `#2019`):
- **DCO no longer runs or gates merges.** The PR-only `DCO (advisory)` job, its upstream action, and
  its native verifier invocation are removed from `ci-required.yml`. `Signed-off-by:` trailers are
  optional; missing trailers are not repaired, reviewed, or considered merge eligibility.
- **The restore point is explicit and inert.** The verifier and synthetic fixtures remain dormant
  under `scripts/ci/` so a future proposal can evaluate the prior contract. [`#2019`](https://github.com/Chris0Jeky/Taskdeck/issues/2019)
  records the work required to restore enforcement but is not authorization to reactivate it. The
  GPL-3.0-only licence, prior MIT grants, and all non-DCO CI/review/security gates are unchanged.

v0.1.2 SHIPPED (2026-08-25, tag at `9766edbb5`):
- **The public v0.1.2 release exists**, under the maintainer's explicit in-session acceptance of the release deck on 2026-08-24 (issue `#1947`, guided-walkthrough reply q-1 A). Annotated tag `v0.1.2` peels to `9766edbb54a02baae87d1cc20e78e45f92a6aa9c`; all four tag workflows (CI Release, Release Security, Release Container, Release Desktop) completed SUCCESS; the public Release carries `taskdeck-v0.1.2-win-x64.zip`, its `.sha256` sidecar, and a provenance file naming the workflow run. Milestone `v0.1.2` was groomed to zero open members and closed the same night: residual halves of `#1967` `#1949` `#1938` `#1940` `#2004` and all of `#1992` re-milestoned to v0.2, `#1242` closed on record acceptance, tracker `#1947` kept open unmilestoned as the standing dogfooding tracker.
- **The published artifact passed a live end-to-end journey on an isolated data root (2026-08-25):** ZIP checksum matched the public sidecar; register → board → board-scoped capture (scope chip present, `#1966`) → triage → 0.96 proposal with provenance → two-phase approve/apply with in-app confirmation (`#1818` banners) → exactly one card, only after Apply; the `#2073` applied read-only decision record rendered natively; the `#2076` archive disclosure and read-only archived-history lanes worked. One discoverability finding — the Archive surface is reachable only through the command palette — is recorded on `#2075`. Not verified on this artifact: Explorer/SmartScreen acceptance, live provider, a second machine, screen-reader speech.

v0.1.2 Priority I burn-down and v0.2 residual deliveries (2026-08-24 to 2026-08-26, through main `006ab8303`):
- **The ordinary-profile startup correction is shipped (`#1876`, PR `#2016`, merge `909e33f9`); v0.1.2 has since been released — see the SHIPPED block above.** Packaged startup now emits a stable secret-free retired-provider diagnostic, keeps explicit retired selectors fail-closed, and tolerates inert retired child keys only after an explicit supported selector. The exact PR head passed 8,094 backend tests with 5 intentional skips, hosted Required CI including dynamic E2E, and bounded review. The v0.1.2 tag and public artifact now exist under the accepted deck (see the SHIPPED block above); ordinary Explorer/SmartScreen acceptance and inherited-profile retired-provider migration remain unclaimed.
- **Checked-in environment defaults no longer mask retired-provider configuration (`#2021`, PR `#2042`, merge `3b81756d`).** Provider provenance now uses the registered runtime environment plus the shared built-in file provider to treat only the exact `appsettings.json` and current `appsettings.{Environment}.json` values as defaults. Explicit environment, command-line, in-memory, and operator JSON selectors retain higher precedence; stale Gemini child settings still fail closed when the effective selector has not moved to a supported provider. Focused API/Application suites, the full backend suite, exact-head hosted CI including dynamic E2E, and bounded review passed.
- **Visible board scope is honest in Paper Inbox and Review (`#1966`, PR `#2017`, merge `dde85e38`).** Board-scoped counts and empty states name the scope, Inbox preserves the column context from `+ Capture`, and keyboard-accessible clear actions restore the unfiltered queue. This is automated component/store evidence; the live browser journey remains unverified.
- **Inbox scope replacements no longer present stale board metadata or prior-scope rows (`#2022`, PR `#2043`, merge `b18d7696`).** Board metadata requests are generation-owned and bind their captured board ID, while Inbox list states are exclusive and retained rows stay mounted but hidden during a replacement load/error so an in-progress edit draft survives. The refreshed-base four-suite run passed 172 tests alongside typecheck, production build, scoped lint, exact-base hosted CI including dynamic E2E, and a fresh adversarial review. Page-level counts during replacement remain on open `#2022`; valid same-scope rows hidden after refresh failure are tracked separately by `#2044`.
- **Due dates now keep their intended calendar day across local timezone boundaries (`#2028`, PR `#2048`, merge `80da1f1a`).** Card due values use a canonical UTC `YYYY-MM-DD` key while Today and Calendar send the user's local day explicitly. Focused backend/frontend checks, typecheck, production build, lint/diff hygiene, exact-head hosted CI including dynamic E2E, and bounded review passed. Exact-head synthetic desktop proof covered Los Angeles, Tokyo, and UTC clocks: the card remained on August 23 while its Today/overdue classification followed each local day. Maximum-range `localDate` validation and unchanged legacy-value audit noise remain tracked by `#2049` and `#2050`.
- **Failed quick captures keep their draft, shared error feedback is inspectable, and persistent toasts do not replay announcements after a skin remount (`#1938`, PRs `#2023`, `#2033`, and `#2067`; initial wave through merge `e9b1390b6`).** Home and Paper Inbox distinguish a failed create from a post-create refresh failure, so a saved capture does not invite a duplicate retry. Default errors persist until dismissal; Legacy and Paper receipts can expand/copy existing detail text, and every Paper toast has a generic dismiss path. Each toast container now baselines the IDs already present when it mounts and announces only later non-error additions. An exact-main Paper-to-Legacy manual remount preserved the persistent toast while the announcer remained empty; actual screen-reader speech was not verified. Receipt localization and announcement roles then shipped as well (PR `#2037`, merge `a6f0dc998`): the persistent error-receipt controls and detail-region names are localized in English, Italian, and Spanish; each disclosure controller is associated with its details region only while that region is mounted; and error toasts announce as assertive alerts while non-error toasts announce as polite statuses, in both Legacy and Paper. The existing persistent-error lifetime and dismiss/copy behavior is unchanged. That slice passed typecheck, a production build, the full frontend suite (336 files, 4,752 tests), a focused Legacy/Paper/catalog run (3 files, 47 tests), lint, diff hygiene, and hosted Required CI at its exact head; it touched no backend file and ran no Playwright pass, and actual screen-reader speech is still unverified. Pinned-build root-cause reproduction, capture-input association/retention, receipt retention policy/ticker efficiency, stale per-toast state cleanup, clipboard-fallback hardening, and the remaining recorded a11y/state-cleanup gaps stay on open `#1938`; Italian and Spanish speaker review stays the human gate on `#1770`. Two further `#1938` slices then merged on 2026-08-26. Toast copy-to-clipboard now degrades honestly in legacy DOM (PR `#2102`, merge `709a098a7`): the asynchronous Clipboard API remains the fast path, and the `document.execCommand` fallback is contained as explicitly best-effort rather than reported as a success it cannot prove, with per-stage regressions covering each branch. Inbox disposition guidance was refreshed alongside the capture-disposition work (PR `#2103`, merge `66f6d7c89`). Both are slices of a still-open `#1938`, whose residual class is unchanged.
- **The source guard catches wider classes of enabled-looking inert controls (`#1949`, PRs `#2027`, `#2032`, `#2038`, `#2055`, `#2068`, and `#2072`; latest merge `aae515012`).** Native buttons require meaningful action handlers and proven form ownership while retaining narrow style-guide specimens; labelled custom buttons require static focusability plus non-empty canonical lowercase `.enter` and `.space` bindings. Mixed-case Vue modifier spellings remain violations, and Ctrl/Alt/Shift/Meta-modified Enter or Space handlers no longer satisfy the custom-button keyboard contract. The recorded parser-hardening residual is now closed for its own class (PR `#2072`, merge `aae515012`): the guard tokenizes opening-tag attributes before scanning, so only actual Vue event directives (`@...` / `v-on:...`) count as handler evidence and directive-looking text inside an ordinary quoted attribute - `data-note='@click=run'` - no longer satisfies the click or keyboard contract. Two false-negative regression cases hold it, the focused guard spec passed 13/13 at that head, and hosted Required CI passed at the exact head. Runtime route actionability, dynamic/compiler-expanded bindings, the AC3 disabled/validation contract, dead keystrokes (`#1968`), and the other acceptance criteria remain on open `#1949`.
- **Lowercase machine-prefix routing is corrected in the installed PWA and split-container topology (`#1992`, PRs `#2029`, `#2030`, and `#2065`; latest merge `c7a34d55c`).** PWA navigation excludes lowercase `/hubs` and `/health`; both nginx templates route lowercase bare and descendant `/api`, `/hubs`, `/health`, and `/mcp` paths to the API, preserving URI, forwarded headers, hub WebSockets, and unbuffered MCP SSE. Required container CI runs the checked-in static/rendered reverse-proxy contract before compose validation and image builds. The live split-container request/SSE proof was run at integration base `927236bd0` (reverse-proxy contract, PWA denylist 26/26, Terraform baseline, `SpaFallbackRoutingApiTests`, and a rebuilt isolated Compose stack returning JSON 200/404/401 and a session-bound `text/event-stream` GET through nginx). **Case parity is now ruled and shipped fail-closed: ADR-0064 Accepted (maintainer ruling 2026-08-30, v0.3 RC deck q-10 A, recorded on `#1992`)** — the four prefixes are exact lowercase, and a case variant (`/API/boards`) or a prefix-boundary encoded slash (`/mcp%2Fmessages`) answers `404` at nginx, is kept off the service worker's navigation fallback so that `404` reaches the user, and answers the JSON `404` contract at the API from a guard that runs ahead of CORS, static files and every authentication middleware — in the co-hosted pipeline and in the standalone `--mcp --transport http` host alike. The same rule covers leading duplicate or encoded separators (`//api/boards`, `/%2fapi/boards`, which nginx merges onto the machine location) and percent-encoded prefix letters (`/%61pi/boards`, which nginx AND Kestrel both decode to the canonical path, so only the raw request target still witnesses the spelling). Nothing is normalized. One divergence is recorded rather than removed: a double-encoded `%252F` reaches the API as the single-encoded form (the host decodes `%25`, keeps `%2F`) and answers `404` there while nginx and the service worker keep it SPA-side. **The wrong-verb 404-versus-405 contract is ruled and shipped: ADR-0059 Accepted (maintainer ruling 2026-08-24, recorded on `#1992`) via PR `#2079`** — a wrong-verb request on a real machine-facing route answers `405` with an exact `Allow` that never advertises a verb the route does not declare (HEAD included), a genuinely missing machine route answers the JSON `404` contract under every verb, and both answers are verb-independent for anonymous callers: the framework's metadata-less synthetic 405 endpoint is replaced on machine paths so the global FallbackPolicy cannot re-hide the contract behind a 401, while a real route under its declared verb still answers its normal 401-first auth ordering. The PWA denylist boundaries align with the four nginx prefixes; under ADR-0064 a percent-encoded descendant such as `/mcp%2Fmessages` is no longer treated as machine surface anywhere — it is rejected at nginx and the API and denied the app shell by the service worker, so the 404 reaches the user (double-encoded `%252F` stays SPA-side at nginx and in the service worker).
- **Review deep links resolve the exact proposal or an explicit unavailable state; they never silently substitute another actionable record (`#1967`, PRs `#2034`, `#2056`, `#2066`, and `#2070`; initial identity wave through merge `55dbf6e14`).** Proposal-only identity matching is case-insensitive across hash resolution, loaded records, selection, Legacy diff state, and both Review skins; board/user/session identities retain their existing contracts. An empty Paper queue filter clears a retained hash. A requested proposal that returns 404, or whose single-proposal response carries a different ID, keeps the requested ID in an explicit unavailable state, inserts/selects no other proposal, exposes no decision controls, and clears the deep link only through `Back to Review`. Applied proposals then became reachable as explicit read-only decision records (PR `#2073`, merge `f45a1fbb0`): a dedicated `ReviewAppliedDecisionRecord` renders the applied record, RECENTLY APPLIED rows become real controls that open it, a hash deep link resolves to the same record, and a terminal record stays inspectable while exposing no decision controls - wired through both Paper and Legacy Review and all three shipped locales. At that head, 370 tests across the 19 touched-area spec files passed, typecheck was clean, and hosted Required CI finished 21/21 after one E2E rerun (the first attempt failed on a chat-surface timing flake unrelated to the changed seam). Applied records also gained historical labels and descriptions in all three locales (PR `#2101`, merge `ef99855f`) — proven on the fallback path only: the applied lede was already status-aware, but the change-section body still preferred the prospective `presentation` copy that `MapToDto` always populates in API-shaped payloads, so a real applied record kept showing prospective body text. That residual was tracked as `#2117` and closed by PR `#2120` (status-aware preference plus an API-shaped-payload regression test). PR `#2101`'s 122 focused Review tests, 16 locale tests, typecheck, build, lint, exact-head CI, and bounded review passed. **This is a merged slice of a still-open `#1967`, not its closure:** the unpinned-legacy audit contract, Dismissed deep-link preference, list-population breadth, applied-actor friendly-name contract, and remaining stale/terminal lifecycle cases stay recorded on the issue. **The `#2117` residual is now closed (PR `#2120`, merge `85babeca2`).** Applied records render their historical copy status-aware over the always-populated prospective `presentation` payload, so an API-shaped response no longer defeats the historical wording that PR `#2101` had proven only on the fallback path; the same slice pluralizes `bodyApplied` across English, Spanish, and Italian, closing `#2132`. This was the Codex P1 raised on PR `#2113`'s review round, carried as `#2117` and delivered rather than deferred. It remains a slice of an open `#1967`. The equivalent Legacy-side defect — `ReviewProposalCard` still rendering prospective presentation copy for settled records — is an **open** residual tracked on `#2121`.
- **Paper Review retains truthful post-decision receipts instead of silently jumping to the next proposal (`#1940`, PR `#2069`, merge `3ab5da5d9`).** Approved, applied, rejected, and deferred outcomes remain at the decision locus; only an approved receipt offers explicit Apply, while terminal receipts and the global review keymap remain nonactionable. Receipts are board-scoped, and a late approval completion cannot replace a newer reviewer selection. `#1940` remains open for progressive disclosure, focus/lifecycle races, and the recorded non-blocking residuals. **The authoritative All/Mine membership contract is no longer among them - it shipped, and the next entry is its record.**
- **Paper Review's All/Mine pair is gated on a server-computed membership contract rather than a heuristic (`#1940`, PR `#2077`, merge `2bdc5d979`).** `GET /api/workspace/collaboration` returns `{ memberCount, hasCollaborators }`, where `memberCount` counts the distinct people who can reach at least one board the caller can reach - the union of `Board.OwnerId` and `BoardAccess.UserId` over every readable board, the caller included, never below 1, archived boards counted, and no other identity disclosed. Owners are read from `Board.OwnerId` and not from access rows, because a board owner deliberately holds no `BoardAccess` row; identity is claims-first and an unauthenticated call is 401. The recorded assumption holds: visibility is not derived from proposal authors, ACL rows alone, or online presence. On the client, `useWorkspaceCollaboration` exposes `idle | loading | ready | unavailable` and accepts an answer only when it is well-typed **and** internally consistent (`memberCount` a whole number of at least 1, and `hasCollaborators` equal to `memberCount > 1`); anything else is `unavailable`. The pair is withdrawn **only** on a positive single-member answer - loading, idle, unknown, malformed, and failed lookups all keep every control - and it is kept anyway whenever the pre-filter queue still contains a foreign-authored proposal, so a departed collaborator's left-behind records keep `Mine` meaningful. While the pair is hidden, Stale becomes a toggle instead of a one-way switch, and a partition that collapses while `Mine` is selected falls back to the unfiltered queue without rewriting a deep-linked hash. Reactivity is a mount-time read plus a 30-second-throttled refresh when the document becomes visible, because **there is no membership event to subscribe to**: `BoardsHub` publishes nothing when board access is granted or revoked, and `BoardAccessService` takes no realtime notifier at all. Evidence: a clean Release build, 30 `WorkspaceServiceTests`, 37 real-SQLite repository and API cases (owner-counted, second-member, no-double-count, archived, ownerless, and cross-user isolation), 26 architecture cases, focused Review/composable/API frontend runs, typecheck, and production build; three mutations were proven to fail the new tests (treating an unknown count as solo, disabling the Mine-to-All fallback, and reverting the consistency guard to a shape-only check). **Two evidence gaps are explicit:** there was no live browser pass - the visibility refresh is proven with synthetic events and a faked clock, not a real tab switch - and at the merged head the Windows `Frontend Unit` lane was **cancelled** rather than completed, so `E2E Smoke` skipped; the Ubuntu `Frontend Unit` lane and every other Required lane passed. A server-side membership broadcast, and the deliberate re-reveal of the pair after a failed refresh, are the recorded residuals.
- **Archiving a board no longer hides its captures and its applied-proposal ledger, and `#1973` is closed (PR `#2076`, merge `45a132f5f`).** The mechanism was confirmed by direct inspection before any fix: `board.Archive()` is a soft delete, captures use `DeleteBehavior.SetNull`, and `AutomationProposal.BoardId` is a bare indexed scalar with no foreign key - so archiving made those rows *unreachable*, never destroyed. Archive rows now carry the archived board id into scoped capture history (Inbox) and decision history (Review) through an explicit `history=archived` mode, and both Legacy and Paper archive confirmations state that captures and decision records are retained, hidden from the unfiltered active queues, and reachable from Workspace -> Archive. Archived history is read-only in both skins: capture triage/edit/batch controls and proposal approve/reject/apply/defer/dismiss controls are suppressed, the mode is announced in-surface, `Open Board` is withheld from archived decision history, Paper capture history expands into a full inspection panel (retained text, triage provenance, decision-record link) loaded through a non-caching `peekDetail`, and leaving archived scope drops the proposal deep link so an archived record cannot re-enter the live mutation-enabled queue. No backend source changed - the backend already scoped correctly - and the new `ArchivedBoardHistoryApiTests` pins unscoped omission and scoped retrieval for both a capture and an Applied proposal. Evidence: 44 files / 990 tests across the review/inbox/capture surface, 16 files / 578 tests across the touched seam, the locale-parity guard with the new history keys, clean typecheck and build, lint at its unchanged six-warning baseline, both new regressions mutation-checked against their reverted fixes, and hosted Required CI green at the exact head including E2E Smoke. **NOT verified:** no live browser or Playwright run - the archived-history journey rests on unit/integration tests plus a synthetic SQLite/API proof - and the `it`/`es` strings are machine-translated. Seven polish residuals are tracked on `#2075`. The direct CardService write gap is now closed (`#2080`, `#2106`, `#2109`; successor PR `#2110`, merge `d75d0783`): direct card mutations reject archived boards, archive races invalidate stale writes without serializing independent card writers, the Board concurrency token migration is provider-native for SQLite and SQL Server, and concurrent delete conflicts map to HTTP 409 instead of 500. Exact-head required CI and E2E Smoke passed after one bounded CI fix round; no live SQL Server application run was performed. Bulk writers in external import, starter-pack apply, and archive restore still need the same race predicate and are tracked by Priority I `#2114`. **That predicate is now extended and `#2114` is closed (PR `#2134`, merge `77252b459`).** External import, starter-pack apply, and archive-item restore — including the restore path's column half — join the same archive-race conditional board update, proven by six deterministic race/independence tests that were mutation-checked against their reverted fixes; ADR-0063 was amended in the same PR. **The guard's own timing dependency is also closed (`#2123`, PR `#2137`, merge `006ab8303`).** `Board.RecordCardMutation` advances a non-user-visible `CardMutationMarker` instead of calling `Touch()`, so the conditional update always changes a column and a same-tick `Touch()` can no longer silently no-op the archive-race predicate; the guard is deterministic rather than clock-dependent. The same change reverts `Board.UpdatedAt` to metadata-only recency, which makes the board-list cache coherent without invalidation and closes `#2115`. Migration `20260826201256_AddBoardCardMutationMarker` carries the column; `UPGRADING.md` was updated in-PR, and the earlier Board concurrency-token migration was recorded there separately (`#2116`, PR `#2122`, merge `3d05c6f08`). The product question of whether recent-boards recency should be activity-based or metadata-based is filed as `#2138` and is not decided here. **Two archive-guard gaps remain OPEN and are not fixed by this wave:** `#2124` — proposal decision writes are still unguarded on archived boards — and `#2125` — an unscoped `MoveCardAsync` still accepts a target column on a different, possibly archived, board via the CLI. Both are Priority II and neither may be read as delivered.
- **An unbound Automation Chat session now says it cannot act, instead of answering with prose that reads like completed work (`#2004` honesty slice, PR `#2074`, merge `17a8c2acf`).** The honest copy already existed in `ChatService` but was unreachable on the turn that needs it: the clarification path returned before the proposal path, and the proposal attempt required the provider to flag its own reply actionable, so a user asking for a board action in a session with no board received prose and no signal at all. The notice now fires on every turn that asks for an action in an unbound session - a clarification turn, an explicit `RequestProposal`, a skip request that forces best effort, and a user message the local intent classifier reads as a board action - and its wording states that nothing was created or changed **on any board**, scoped that way deliberately because the same turn does persist chat messages and any `@mention` notifications. Bound sessions, the proposal and parse-hint paths, and the degraded path are untouched, and both new notice sites are guarded on non-empty provider content so a textless turn keeps its empty-content placeholder and its `degraded` label. Evidence at the merged head: 95 focused `ChatService` cases, 3,865 Application, 2,443 API (4 skipped), 1,656 Domain, and 26 architecture cases; two mutation checks proving the four new-behavior tests and both guard tests fail without the change; one fresh-context adversarial review with no CRITICAL and no HIGH; one Codex round (1 P1, 5 P2) answered on-thread with no code change; and hosted Required CI green at the exact head including E2E Smoke. **NOT verified:** no browser or live-provider run - the behavior was reasoned from the recorded transcript and covered by unit tests, not re-observed against a live OpenAI session. **This is the honesty half only.** The redesign half of `#2004` - board binding from natural language, default-to-act, grounding-before-generation, and clarification termination - remains an open maintainer decision, and `#2004` stays open with the recorded residuals: a self-disarming frontend `requestProposal` opt-in, a bound session that can still end an actionable turn in silent prose, tool-calling turns that can imply a change with no proposal created, and `StreamResponseAsync` (exposed by `ChatController`, unused by the shipped web UI) never applying the notice. `docs/api/CHAT.md` drift found during the work is tracked separately on `#2078`.
- **An exact-main no-publish Windows candidate and isolated manual core loop passed (`789cad4d`, run `32679854224`).** The blank-tag dispatch built and tested the untouched Windows ZIP, uploaded `release-win-x64`, and skipped release creation. The downloaded ZIP matched SHA-256 `334707099073a8b656aa496e47afe520179c0578cadc78097a2d22de4b5103f2`. A fresh synthetic account in isolated `%LOCALAPPDATA%` registered, created a starter board, captured and triaged a linked task, reviewed it, and proved approval left the board at one card before explicit Apply moved it to two; the packaged process then emitted both safe-stop markers. This was a controlled extraction/launch with deterministic Mock, not Explorer/SmartScreen, inherited-profile migration, live-provider, public-download, or signed-artifact evidence. That run also exposed the typography/CSP defect subsequently repaired and re-proved below.
- **The bundled font/CSP repair is shipped and its Material Symbols visual-parity residual is closed (`#2035`; initial PRs `#2039` and `#2041`, merges `53ef459c` and `3fa60717`; visual follow-up PR `#2046`).** The API CSP permits same-origin fonts, active Paper and Material Symbols faces are self-hosted, Google font/style routes are gone, and Windows/Pages artifacts carry the reviewed Apache-2.0 licence. No-publish run `32692308935` produced the untouched ZIP with SHA-256 `9981177c734300d1f97fd51f3a1b77a882d57ea5d640276c2c7e1339c9d3cbd5`; a fresh isolated package loaded 12/12 same-origin WOFF2 resources with no font/style CSP violation or Google/Gstatic request, and the offline `cloud_off` ligature rendered as a glyph. The final follow-up pins the local 200-weight face, removes the package's unlayered 24px override, and proves the intended 16px/18px/20px call-site sizes in a production preview. The separate packaged-manifest CSP residual remains tracked by `#2045`.
- **Capture metadata now survives the full review-first loop (`#2005`, `#2051`, `#2052`; PR `#2058`, merge `6cc7544a`).** Requested due dates and labels persist through capture triage, failed-edit recovery, proposal review, and explicit Apply; every draft control freezes while a save is in flight. Recovery revisions serialize against approve/reject decisions, and both triage workers record retry state from a fresh scope after a failed processing scope. Final-head focused runs passed 48 Application and 120 API tests, independent reviewers passed another 75 API, 87 Application, and 136 frontend tests without a CRITICAL/HIGH finding, and hosted CI including dynamic E2E passed. The synthetic browser journey proved unknown-label correction, lossless `Sales, EMEA` metadata, approval without board mutation, and exactly one explicit Apply on UI-equivalent head `fb065547`; the final backend concurrency/worker hardening is automated-test evidence rather than a second browser run. Closed PRs `#2026` (`ab1db61e`) and `#2047` (`9ac1fb5f`) remain historical lineage only. Comma-containing composer labels and clearing an uncommitted label draft after success remain on `#2053` and `#2057`.
- **One broader lineage remains parked; the other was superseded by a shipped successor.** PR `#2025` still preserves the broader `#1967` applied-proposal detail lineage at `a70f88ad`; its mixed-case identity, Legacy diff, and unavailable-deep-link blockers were extracted and shipped through PRs `#2034`, `#2056`, `#2066`, and `#2070`, and the applied read-only record shipped separately through PR `#2073` (`f45a1fbb0`), while that broader parked head itself remains unmerged and is not shipped behavior. PR `#2031`, which preserved the `#1973` archived-history lineage at `ecf44ea2` after parking at the two-round review ceiling with two confirmed HIGH paths open, is now **superseded**: PR `#2076` closed both HIGH paths, shipped the disclosure, and closed `#1973`. `#2025`'s issue comments remain its exact resume point.
- **Both previously saved successor heads have been published and gated.** `origin/issue-1949/directive-attribute-tokenization` (`d89bd7cc3`) became PR `#2072` and merged as `aae515012`; `origin/issue-1967/applied-read-only-detail` (`010021a745`) became PR `#2073` and merged as `f45a1fbb0`. Neither is a saved-but-unpublished head any longer.
- **Captures now carry explicit dispositions, and `#2085` is closed (PR `#2098`, merge `c1d94ee08`).** The M1 disposition set — Ask AI, Keep, and Archive, together with target, link, and propose — is available on a capture rather than implied by triage, which is the v0.2 M1 slice the release cut assigned to `#2085`. Two independent reviews came back clean. Its required CI was initially blocked by a GitHub runner-assignment outage rather than by anything in the change; a close/reopen retriggered assignment and the exact head then completed green before merge.
- **Archived-card 409 responses are declared in the OpenAPI contract, and `#2105` is closed (PR `#2107`, merge `85f58e930`).** Create, Move, and Delete card routes declare the 409, Update's existing declaration is reworded for the same case, and swagger contract tests assert the response resolves to the `ApiErrorResponse` schema reference; the assertions were mutation-checked. This PR was rescued from the dead `#2080` stack base rather than rebuilt: all stale superseded content was removed, none of the contract tests changed, and the old API Integration redness on the abandoned base was proven to be `#2080`'s own token-advance defect and not a fault in this change.
- **The Windows launcher TERM/KILL regression is evidence-based in CI, and `#2111` is closed (PR `#2112`, merge `36a055395`).** The EBUSY teardown race was root-caused rather than retried around: a live process still held the fixture root as its working directory, and the MSYS-pid kill was unsynchronized with native process exit. Teardown now waits until the directory is actually removable against a deadline and names any surviving native pids when it gives up, so a failure reports what held the tree instead of a bare EBUSY. Five review P2s were tracked to `#2136` rather than fixed in-PR; a related Linux/macOS stub-server shutdown gap is `#2135`. Both are open.
- **The backend dependency floor moved (dependabot PR `#2064`, merge `04dbc829b`).** The NuGet minor/patch group brought FsCheck 3.4.0, PdfPig 0.1.16, and OpenTelemetry 1.18.0 among others, and the PdfPig version sentinel moved 0.1.15 → 0.1.16 with it. The base was refreshed before merge specifically so FsCheck 3.4.0 ran against the new capture contracts rather than against a stale tree. PdfPig evidence-version drift is noted on `#1429`. A separate Stryker 9.6.1 → 10 mutation-toolchain bump is deferred to `#2139`.
- **The 2026-08-26 review sweep seeded its findings as tracked issues rather than silent deferrals.** The merged-wave sweep and the individual review rounds filed `#2119`, `#2121`, `#2124`–`#2131`, `#2133`, `#2135`, `#2136`, `#2138`, and `#2139`; `#2117`, `#2123`, and `#2132` were closed by merges in the same wave. They are not enumerated here — read them on GitHub — except where a specific seam above names one.
- **Post-merge `main` CI: SUCCESS at `44aceede6`; the run at this wave's final tip `006ab8303` (run `33015017508`) concluded `cancelled`, so the wave's final tip has NO green post-merge `main` run.** It was superseded by the next merge landing — frontend dependabot PR `#2118` (merge `d37954730`), which is outside this wave's manifest and is not recorded as a delivery here. Several intermediate post-merge runs in this wave concluded `cancelled` for the same reason, which is the same release-proof debt already recorded above. Per-PR exact-head Required CI was green for every one of the eleven merges; it is the post-merge `main` confirmation at the wave tip that is missing, and it stays owed before any release deck.

Repository realignment + guided walkthrough (2026-08-23):
- **Fifteen maintainer decisions were applied in one guided walkthrough** (decision map `map:v1:2f43345a`; the per-item record is the `OUTSTANDING_TASKS.md` changelog). The rulings that change execution: **q-1** - `#1858` decided as *lazy-load the locale catalogs* with the bundle gate held at 1250 KB, **shipped as PR `#2002`**, which unblocks ADR-0054 rollout step 3; **q-3** - **v0.1.2 is re-scoped** to the `#1876` startup fix **plus the open Priority I tranche**, delivered PR-only, with maintainer deck acceptance required before any tag (the `v0.1.2` milestone exists; **no `v0.1.2` tag exists**); **q-2 / q-8** - the dogfooding sprint **started 2026-08-22** and the checkpoint is re-anchored to a **floor** of 2026-09-01 - a floor, not eligibility, because ADR-0044's own conditions still control; **q-4** - `#1278`'s exit criteria are **ratified as written**, with the archive plan remaining only the checkpoint fallback; **q-5** - `#1270` closed as substantially delivered; **q-6** - the **Taskdeck name is kept**, with the remaining legal residuals re-scoped to the commercial gate (`#1482`, still open); **q-7** - a GitHub `production` environment now exists with deliberately relaxed protection (maintainer-only reviewer, self-review allowed, no wait timer, admin bypass on); **e-5** - the 2026-04/2026-05 dev-key exposure window was closed as an **explicitly accepted risk**, not as a verified all-dummy finding; **e-7** - the `it`/`es` locales are marked machine-translated until a speaker reviews them (`#1770` stays open).
- **The strategy spine is now a document rather than an inference.** [`docs/strategy/PRODUCT_DIRECTION.md`](strategy/PRODUCT_DIRECTION.md) is canonical for product identity: the three-layer framing (adaptive work OS as destination, context-to-action engine as the mechanism, transcripts/notes/captures as the wedge) and the release-theme ladder - **v0.1.x** Honest Windows Beta, **v0.2** Coherent Context-to-Action Loop, **v0.3** Accountable Agents + Downloadable Beta, **v0.4** Hosted Open Beta + Work Model + Fabric Foundation, **v0.5** Speak, Type, Paste, or Drop, **v0.6** Under Your Rules, then v0.7–v1.0 as epic-level themes (renamed/extended 2026-08-30 under ADR-0065). `REVIVAL_PLAN.md` stays the execution plan; this file stays shipped reality.
- **ADR-0057 (user-sovereign delegated authority) is `Accepted` as direction (maintainer ruling 2026-08-24, with an explicit openness caveat), and NOT in force as implementation.** The shipped trust model is unchanged and remains review-first (ADR-0003 / GP-06 / ADR-0056): nothing in the running product delegates board-write authority to an agent, and no auto-approval surface may be built until an implementation slice is separately gated. Read ADR-0057 as a ratified future direction, never as a description of current behaviour.
- **This file was restructured, executing the standing `#1138` head-lean scope.** Delivery records dated before 2026-07-01, the 2026-02 to 2026-04 wave sections, the pre-pivot "Phase Progress (Reconciled)" Phase 1-4 model, and the "Recently Resolved (This Cycle)" block (whose entries were all from 2026-02) moved **verbatim** to [`archive/status-history/STATUS_HISTORY_2026-02_to_2026-07.md`](archive/status-history/STATUS_HISTORY_2026-02_to_2026-07.md). The rotation itself was lossless (every rotated line appears verbatim in the archive) and every removed section leaves a pointer; separately, three stale claims in the retained text were corrected in place: the v0.1.2 scope, the `ChatService` provider list (ADR-0055 - Gemini's executable surface is gone), and the pre-dogfooding baseline that still read as a current status claim.

Windows v0.1.1 ordinary-profile startup incident (2026-08-22):
- **The public ZIP is intact, but v0.1.1 is not reliably double-click-to-use on an upgrading Windows profile.** Normal launches from both Downloads and Desktop inherited a user-scoped retired `Llm__Provider=Gemini` selector plus `Llm__Gemini__*` names, emitted only generic `TASKDECK_DESKTOP_FATAL code=startup_failed`, and created no listener. A fresh isolated `LOCALAPPDATA` failed identically, ruling out extraction path, port collision, database state, and AppData writability. The complete sanitized record is [`V0_1_1_WINDOWS_STARTUP_INCIDENT.md`](platform/V0_1_1_WINDOWS_STARTUP_INCIDENT.md).
- **The earlier public-artifact journey was hermetic, not ordinary-profile migration proof.** Its child environment intentionally removed every inherited `Llm__*` name to protect credentials. That still proves archive integrity, clean bootstrap, persistence, review-first live OpenAI behavior, and browser handoff under the documented isolated conditions; it did not exercise Explorer inheritance of retired Taskdeck provider settings. Issue [`#1876`](https://github.com/Chris0Jeky/Taskdeck/issues/1876#issuecomment-5380396057) was reopened for this incident and closed again only when PR `#2016` shipped the correction.
- **An explicit compatibility migration works without deleting credentials.** A child-only OpenAI selector with retired Gemini child names omitted made the unchanged public executable ready on `127.0.0.1:5000`; `/health/ready` and `/` returned 200. The persistent non-secret user selector is now OpenAI, while all credential-bearing Gemini and OpenAI variables remain untouched. The visible v0.1.1 compatibility instance is transient runtime evidence, not a replacement release.
- **The startup correction shipped on main, and v0.1.2 remains larger than that slice.** PR `#2016` merged as `909e33f9` after the full backend suite, hosted Required CI including dynamic E2E, and bounded review passed. **Per walkthrough q-3, v0.1.2 = the `#1876` fix *plus* the open Priority I tranche**, delivered PR-only, with maintainer deck acceptance required before any tag; the `v0.1.2` milestone exists and **no `v0.1.2` tag does**. Exact-main no-publish and isolated Mock core-loop evidence now exist; tag, public-download, inherited-profile migration, and ordinary Explorer/SmartScreen proof remain unverified.

Dogfooding UX fix wave — 7 PRs merged (2026-08-22, tracker `#1947`, main `69ac993a` → `62f12012`):
- **The first burn-down pass over the 2026-08-22 dogfooding sweep is shipped: dead affordances are wired or removed, and copy that advertised behaviour the code does not have is corrected.** Every defect in this wave was reproduced against a running instance before it was fixed, and each fix is held by tests that were mutation-checked against the pre-fix behaviour. The sweep's remaining items stay open on `#1947`; the wave changed no backend contract except the audit-actor stamp below.
- **The Paper topbar's three controls are real controls (`#1932`, PR `#1954`).** Bell, gear and avatar had no `@click` at all — measured clicks left the DOM byte-identical. The bell now routes to `/workspace/notifications`; the gear routes to `/workspace/settings/appearance` and is renamed `Appearance settings`, because `paperIconPaths.ts` renders `settings` as a ring with eight spokes that is indistinguishable from a sun glyph at 14px and therefore read as a theme toggle; the avatar becomes a real `role="menu"` button (Profile / Appearance / Sign out) with Escape-to-trigger, outside-`pointerdown` close, and arrow-key roving. The `Profile` item renders **only** when the `newAuth` flag is on, because `/workspace/settings/profile` is flag-gated and the router silently bounces a flag-off navigation to Home — shipping it dead would be the same defect one layer down.
- **A completed first-loop block no longer dominates Home (`#1936`, PR `#1950`).** `PaperHomeView` rendered the onboarding block on step count alone, with no completion branch, no control, and — a second latent defect — no read of `onboarding.visibility`, so a dismissal made from Legacy Home or Today was ignored by the Paper skin entirely. It now has three states: unchanged while any step is incomplete, auto-collapsed to a single line once the server's `isComplete` **or** every step is ticked, and not rendered at all when `visibility === 'dismissed'`. Dismiss reuses the server-persisted `workspaceStore.updateOnboarding('dismiss')` write Legacy Home already makes — no new client preference, so it survives reload and applies on another device. Expand is deliberately session-scoped. `#1936` stays open: revised AC2 (promote live aggregations into the vacated space) and AC4 (coordinate Home vs Today with `#1322`) are product decisions the PR was scoped away from, and Paper has no `Replay Setup` restore affordance after a dismissal.
- **Capture triage states why a blocked action is off and what a decision did (`#1944`, PR `#1952`).** Accept-on-board with no board picked was *already* `disabled` and already returned early — the defect is that `.pbtn` in `paper-tokens.css` has no `:disabled` rule, so a disabled ember button kept its fill, its `cursor: pointer` and its `:hover` change and absorbed the click in silence. One `boardPickBlock` computed (`noBoards` / `noBoard` / `viewOnly`) is now the single source of the `disabled` binding, the handler guard **and** a visible `aria-describedby` reason line, so they cannot drift apart; disabled is visibly disabled. That fix originally shipped as a triage-scoped `:deep(.pbtn:disabled)` override rather than an edit to the shared token sheet; **`#1953`/PR `#1988` has since deleted that override and moved the treatment into `paper-tokens.css` itself** (second pass below), so the stopgap no longer exists. Each row carries `data-row-state` and, once decided, a status line naming the next step — including the honest `Triaged, but nothing to propose` terminal state, which no longer sends such a capture to Review at all. Source tags (`TYPED`) are visually and semantically separated from state tags, and an out-of-contract status maps to `unknown`, never to `undecided`. Deferred deliberately: pre-triage capture-body editing is a Paper detail-surface porting gap tracked as **`#1951`**.
- **Dead sidebar and provenance controls removed; the Paper primary loop is ordered as it is walked (`#1934` + `#1941`, PR `#1957`).** The workspace chip was a `<button aria-label="Switch workspace">` with a chevron that opened nothing — Taskdeck is single-workspace (`workspaceStore` holds a *mode*, never a list), so it is demoted to a non-interactive `<div>` identity chip; restore the button, chevron and label together with a real switcher. Paper's `primaryItems` are reordered to **Home, Today, Inbox, Review, Boards** so the rail follows capture → review → board instead of stranding Inbox last. The phone tab bar carries a reduced set in the same relative order — Home, Today, Inbox, Review, with Boards behind the More button, not a fifth tab — and because `availableNavItems` drives it, command-palette ordering follows too. The Review right rail's dead "tune heuristics" link is removed, and a repo-wide `deadAnchors` spec guards against re-introducing placeholder hrefs. **The Legacy shell is untouched and keeps its old order** (`ShellSidebar.vue`: Today, Review, Boards, Inbox, with Home not `sidebarPrimary`) — deliberate, since ADR-0038 froze Legacy and Paper is canonical.
- **The sidebar version string is the running backend's, or nothing (`#1948`, PR `#1956`).** `PaperSidebar.vue` declared `version?: string` defaulted to `'v0.7.2'`, copied verbatim from the Paper design mock, and no caller ever passed the prop — so every running instance displayed `v0.7.2` while the shipped release was `v0.1.1`, which made dogfooding reports unattributable to a build (it already confounded `#1933`). The footer now reads `GET /health/live`'s `version` through `versionApi` → `useProductVersion`, completing a chain `#1804` had already stamped end-to-end on the backend side. **There is no literal fallback by design:** when no version can be established — backend unreachable, or the Pages demo built with `VITE_API_BASE_URL: ''` — the span is not rendered at all, because a wrong version string silently misattributes every bug filed against that build. `/health/live` sits outside the `/api` prefix, so the request overrides `baseURL` with the API root, the same derivation `useBoardRealtime.resolveHubUrl()` uses.
- **Paper can manage boards, not just read them (`#1945`, PR `#1959`, ADR-0056).** This was a **skin-porting gap, not a missing feature**: the controllers, `cardsApi`/`columnsApi`/`boardsApi` and `store/board/*` were complete and green for the whole life of the gap, because API coverage cannot see a missing button, and Legacy was fully wired. Paper now ships a per-column inline `+ card` composer (with `+ capture` demoted to a secondary link), a column settings dialog (rename, WIP limit, delete), explicit move-left/move-right column reorder alongside the drag handle — drag was Legacy's only reorder and is unusable without a pointer — and a board settings dialog (rename, description, archive/restore). Confirms are in-dialog two-step rather than `window.confirm`, and Escape goes through the shared escape *stack* so it closes the dialog instead of falling through to `BoardView`'s page-level Escape. The `n` shortcut is un-gated for Paper by having `PaperBoardColumn` render the exact DOM contract `createCardInSelectedColumn` drives, so one code path serves both skins. **Zero backend, API-client or store changes.** Two things deliberately not built: no permanent board *delete* (`DELETE /api/boards/{id}` is `board.Archive()`, so Paper says "Move to archive"), and no `canWrite` gating, since neither skin gates board edit controls today and gating Paper alone would trade one divergence for another (recorded in ADR-0056 §4). A **capability-parity guard** walks each skin's component import graph from its root view and fails if a covered `boardStore` board-mutation action is unreachable from either skin. The covered set is derived from the store's board-surface action groups plus a name-pattern backstop, minus an explicit `NON_BOARD_SURFACE_MUTATIONS` exemption list (e.g. `createBoard`, label management — actions whose home surface is not the board view), so the guarantee is scoped, not total; a future action group outside the covered prongs would be silently exempt (recorded limit, see the guard's header). The same PR also shipped horizon finding H-03 — a populated Paper board previously had **no** add-column affordance at all, because the only one lived in the zero-column empty state; a secondary `+ column` control now sits at the end of the lane rail. Issue **`#1965`**, which reported exactly that defect, was **not** auto-closed by the merge (`#1959` merged 19:08Z, `#1965` was closed separately at 19:18Z the same day) — the earlier claim here that it was "still open" was already stale when it was written.
- **Direct-CRUD audit rows now carry the acting user (`#1960`, PR `#1978`).** ADR-0056 §5 *recorded the gap* — most direct-edit audit rows were unattributed, with `#1960` tracking the fix; PR `#1978` is what closed it. The write was already per-user everywhere, but the audit row mostly was not. `SafeLogAsync` was called with `userId: null` for card create/move/delete, every column action including `ReorderColumnsAsync`, and board update/archive/unarchive plus the `DELETE` soft-archive lane — all of which now stamp the actor. Two measured corrections to the issue text: board-*update* did not stamp an actor (board-*create* did, via `ownerId`), and the board-update lane also covers unarchive. The pattern reuses `UpdateCardAsync`'s existing shape — an optional `Guid? actorUserId` before the cancellation token plus a companion positional-token overload — so **no existing call site changes meaning**: the CLI and the proposal apply pipeline (`OperationHandlerRegistry`) keep the no-actor overloads and keep writing `userId: null`, and the proposal-lane `ReorderColumnAsync` (singular) is asserted to still do so. The actor is claims-first and server-side, never a request-body field; no route, DTO, status code, response shape or authorization behaviour changed. Held by `DirectCrudAuditActorTests` — **the "13 tests" first recorded here was a mid-branch measurement**: 13 was the count at `4ddcc7bb`, but `#1978` actually merged with **15** `[Fact]` cases at `62f12012`, and the file carries **20** at `main` today because `#1979` added five label cases in the second pass below. It is therefore no longer a card/column/board-only guard. The set includes two overload-resolution regressions (the new `Guid` parameter sits where a `Guid` already lived on `DeleteCardAsync`/`DeleteColumnAsync`).
- **Correction to this file: Taskdeck has never had an auto-seal.** The 2026-07-17 `#1298` note below claimed the Today cover's "Auto-seals in…" copy was "the separate, real day-sealing feature". Measured 2026-08-22: `useTodayDossier`'s `buildHonestDossier` hardcodes `autoSealsIn: null` and nothing ever overrides it, and the only caller of `IDailySealService` is `TodayController`'s explicit `POST /today/seal` — no `BackgroundService` seals a day. The copy was decorative and unreachable, and the note has been amended in place. `#1939` (Today seal state machine, honest empty states, removal of that copy and of the `sealed at end of session` eyebrow) **shipped as PR `#1976`** — see the second pass below.

Dogfooding UX fix wave, second pass — 6 PRs merged (2026-08-22, tracker `#1947`, main `7db4f82b` → `42067bd4`):
- **The second pass over the same sweep closes its truth defects: copy that named an actor it could not know, stamps that named a tone instead of an outcome, two counters that contradicted each other, and a fallback that answered machines with an HTML page.** One HTTP contract changed (`#1971`) and one audit-write lane widened (`#1979`); everything else is frontend copy, tokens and store wiring. **All six are automated evidence only — component/service specs plus `#1990`'s API-integration suite, none browser-verified**, unlike the first pass, whose defects were each reproduced against a running instance. The sweep's remaining items stay open on `#1947`.
- **Today's seal is a four-state machine behind a confirm, and its empty panels say which of them are not built (`#1939`, PR `#1976`).** The cover sealed on the first click, then left the button *enabled* and answered further clicks with `Day is already sealed.` — the `#1949` enabled-but-inert class — and claimed `Day sealed. The dossier is archived.` when nothing is archived. `PaperTodayView.vue` now drives `idle → confirming → sealing → sealed` from two refs, with the irreversible step behind a prompt that says so in as many words (`Taskdeck has no unseal action, so today stays sealed once you confirm.`), a failed seal leaving the prompt open and retryable, and the sealed state disabled with a `role="status"` reason rather than a toast. A `watch` on `dossierLocalDate` closes an open prompt when the local day rolls over, so a prompt opened at 23:59 and confirmed at 00:01 cannot seal a day the warning never named. The empty states split into two deliberately different classes: panels with **no query behind them** (`ledger`, `decisions`, `boards`) carry a `Not built yet` chip and point at the surface that does hold the truth, while the query-backed panels (`stats`, `cadence`, `streak`, carry-over) carry no chip — their empty state can mean a valid zero (carry-over renders `No overdue cards…` on a successful summary with none) or an unavailable live query, and a missing feature is never dressed as either. `Write a note` now focuses the field it actually writes to instead of promising a morning briefing that does not exist. Held by 26 + 11 cases in `PaperTodayView.spec.ts` and `TodayCover.spec.ts`, up from 13 + 6. **Loose end:** `autoSealsIn` survives as a dead field on the `useTodayDossier` DTO with no producer and no consumer — the copy is gone, the field is not.
- **The Review provenance footnote is driven by the recorded triple, and says nothing when the record cannot support a claim (`#1963`, PR `#1985`).** It was one unconditional string asserting `a deterministic offline extractor for captures, or your configured AI provider for chat-driven automation` — false for exactly the transcript-source captures `LlmCaptureTriageExtractor` triages with a live provider. A new `provenanceActor.ts` classifier maps the recorded `provider`/`model`/`promptVersion` triple to a discriminated union (`deterministic` / `mock` / `provider` / `unknown`) and the component renders a per-kind i18n key, or **nothing at all**. Three paths fail closed to silence: absent metadata, a blank or literal-`unknown` provider, and — the sharpest — an **incoherent** record stamping `deterministic-extractor` alongside `llm-triage.v2`, because the one claim the module must never make on bad data is the offline one. All three shipped locales were rewritten together. An egress guarantee (`No AI provider was called.`) was drafted and then **removed on the same branch**: a degraded live run carries the deterministic triple only *after* the transcript was already dispatched and billed, so the record cannot support it — a checkout race reverted the `en` half of that removal and a follow-up commit re-applied it; `main` carries no egress claim in any locale. **The footnote renders nothing in production today** — `ReviewMain.vue` is its only caller and passes no `metadata` prop, so every proposal classifies `unknown`. The PR removes a false claim and leaves the honest branch dormant; wiring the triple into the deep-review payload is tracked as **`#1987`**. Held by 28 cases in `ReviewProvenance.spec.ts`, up from 1.
- **Label audit rows carry the acting user too (`#1979`, PR `#1986`).** `#1978` closed the card/column/board half of the ADR-0056 §5 attribution debt and left `LabelService` writing `userId: null` at every one of its `SafeLogAsync` call sites. Five methods (create, both update overloads, both delete overloads) now take `Guid? actorUserId` before the cancellation token with a companion positional-token overload — the identical shape `#1978` used, so no existing call site changes meaning — and `LabelsController` passes the claims-derived `userId` it already held for `CanWriteBoardAsync`. No route, DTO, status code, response shape or authorization behaviour changed. Two measured corrections to the PR's own text: there are **three** `SafeLogAsync` call sites, not five (the fourth and fifth rows are delegating overloads); and unlike `#1978`, **no production lane deliberately keeps the no-actor overload here** — neither the CLI nor `OperationHandlerRegistry` touches `LabelService` at all, so the unattributed path is exercised only by tests today. Labels created by `BoardJsonExportImportService` and `StarterPackApplyService` bypass the service, and its audit rows, entirely.
- **A disabled `.pbtn` Paper button is disabled everywhere, from the token sheet (`#1953`, PR `#1988`).** `#1944`/PR `#1952` fixed the visible symptom inside capture triage with a scoped `:deep(.pbtn:disabled)` override; the systemic defect was that `.pbtn` in `paper-tokens.css` had no disabled treatment at all, so every *other* disabled Paper button still kept its fill, its `cursor: pointer` and its `:hover` change. The sheet now guards each interactive rule with `:not(:disabled)` on the selector — a disabled button never enters a hover or active state rather than having one undone afterwards — and adds two `:disabled` rules neutralising fill, ink, border, `box-shadow` (killing the ember inset) and `transform`, with `cursor: not-allowed`, built from the existing `--paper-2` / `--mute` / `--line-soft` / `--faint` tokens. The family boundary matters: buttons styled outside `.pbtn` (for example `PaperCaptureComposer`'s `paper-composer__*` attachment controls) still have no disabled treatment — the systemic gap is closed for the `PaperHLBtn`/`.pbtn` family, not for every control that can carry `disabled`. The triage-scoped override is **deleted**. A deliberate delta from the stopgap: a disabled ghost keeps a transparent fill, because giving it the same substrate fill as a live ember sibling made the dead control the louder of the two. **Two scope limits worth stating:** the rules match the native `:disabled` pseudo-class only — there is no `[aria-disabled]` or `.pbtn--disabled` selector, so an `aria-disabled`-only or link-shaped control gets nothing — and every selector is prefixed `.paper` / `.paper-night`, so the treatment is app-wide whenever the Paper skin is on and inert when it is off. Held by 49 cases in `tests/paper-disabled-button.spec.ts`, which parses the token sheet off disk and resolves the cascade itself because happy-dom resolves neither `var(--*)` nor real specificity, and which derives the variant list *from the sheet* so a future `.pbtn-quiet` cannot ship interactive rules the guard never exercises.
- **Toast stamps name the outcome, and the two Inbox counters stop contradicting each other (`#1970` + `#1974`, PR `#1989`).** The stamp was read from the toast's *tone*, and the success tone is named `applied` — so an inbox save, a pre-apply approval and an actual apply all stamped `APPLIED`, while errors stamped `Overdue` and info stamped `Captured`. A `ToastLabel` union plus an options argument let each call site stamp its own word: capture create is `SAVED`, triage enqueue is `QUEUED`, review approve is `APPROVED`, and execute is `APPLIED`, now the only path allowed to say it; unlabelled toasts fall back to `Done`/`Noted`/`Warning`/`Failed`. The tagstamp colour still comes from the tone, so nothing moved visually. On the counters: the sidebar badge reads the server's `capturesNeedingTriage` (`New + Failed`) while the Inbox header rendered `items.length` — every capture fetched, applied and ignored ones included — and called **both** of them a queue; the badge was also fetched once per session, so no capture mutation refreshed it until a full reload. A new `refreshWorkloadCounts()` writes back only the `workload` slice (never re-applying server preferences over newer local intent, never flashing a skeleton), guarded by a module-scoped request version that `fetchHomeSummary` and `clearHomeSummary` also bump, so a slow badge refresh can neither rewind fresher counts nor resurrect a cleared summary; `captureStore` calls it from every mutation that can move `New + Failed`. The eyebrow becomes `{pending} awaiting triage · {total} captured`, borrowing Home's own phrase so badge, Home line and eyebrow name one thing. **The two numbers are not forced equal** — the Inbox list is limit-fetched and can be board-scoped, so it counts rows on screen while the badge is workspace-wide; what they now share is one definition of *pending* and two distinct labels. All three locales updated, including a plural form chosen on `{total}` after `1 catturati` / `1 capturadas` read wrong at a single capture. **Still partial:** the review badge refreshes only as a side effect of a capture mutation — no review-side mutation calls the refresh itself.
- **Unknown machine-facing paths now 404 instead of 200 + the app shell — a deliberate HTTP contract change (horizon finding H-09 / `#1971`, PR `#1990`).** The SPA fallback matched every unmatched path, so a typo'd, renamed, or removed route under `/api`, `/hubs`, `/health`, or `/mcp` returned `200 OK` with `text/html`; such a path now returns `404` with the standard `ApiErrorResponse` JSON shape (`errorCode`/`message`, `application/json`), and the four catch-alls are excluded from the OpenAPI document (measured 2026-08-22: 160 documented paths become 156 — a measured claim, not one an automated test covers). A matched real route still reaches its normal authentication/authorization path, but `GET`/`HEAD` on a real POST-only route now reaches the fallback and returns 404 rather than the framework's former 405; that residual was ruled on 2026-08-24 — ADR-0059 (Accepted) restores the `405` with an exact `Allow`, see the `#1992` entry above. **Accepted cost:** the fallbacks are anonymous, so route existence is now distinguishable without credentials (404 vs 401) — information the published OpenAPI document already carries; `/mcp` remains gated by `ApiKeyMiddleware` ahead of routing, so its 404 is reachable only with a valid key. ADR-0036's anonymous opt-out list is amended accordingly. Held by 41 cases in `SpaFallbackRoutingApiTests`, whose fixture supplies a marked `index.html` — load-bearing, because the plain test host ships no wwwroot and the suite would otherwise pass against the *unfixed* pipeline. **Deployment correction:** PRs `#2029` and `#2030` now bypass the installed-PWA shell for lowercase `/hubs` and `/health` navigations and route lowercase bare and descendant `/api`, `/hubs`, `/health`, and `/mcp` paths through both split-container nginx templates. Required container CI now executes the static/rendered proxy contract. The remaining topology bounds are closed: the wrong-verb 404/405 decision by ADR-0059, the live request/SSE proof at integration base `927236bd0`, and case/encoded-slash parity by ADR-0064 (fail closed — see the `#1992` entry above).

OpenAI-only live-provider surface (2026-08-20):
- **OpenAI is the supported live provider and its default model is now `gpt-5.6-luna`** (GPT-5.6 low-cost reasoning tier; reasoning effort is left at the model default, `medium`). The OpenAI adapter now sends `max_completion_tokens` instead of the legacy `max_tokens` (valid for all current OpenAI models) and omits `temperature` for reasoning-family models (`gpt-5*`, `o1*`, `o3*`, `o4*`), which reject non-default temperature on chat completions — without this the new default model would fail every live request and degrade to fallback. Held by payload-shape tests in `OpenAiLlmProviderTests` (`IsReasoningModel` classification plus outbound-payload assertions for both model families).
- **Gemini's executable surface is removed by `#1879`.** The adapter, DI/config/egress registration, demo selection, and deployment settings are gone. An effective stale `Llm:Provider=Gemini` selector, or retained `Llm:Gemini` settings without a higher-precedence supported-provider selection, fails startup with migration guidance instead of silently falling back to Mock. Checked-in `appsettings.json` and the current `appsettings.{Environment}.json` are defaults rather than operator evidence; explicit environment, command-line, in-memory, and operator JSON selectors still take precedence. Ambient `GEMINI_API_KEY` no longer selects a live provider. Historical free-form provider values remain readable, and the generic `x-goog-api-key` redaction and redirect-stripping defenses remain in place.
- **The remaining automated `#1879` migration/evidence gaps are closed without widening the provider surface.** A non-empty retired Compose wrapper is reduced to a boolean presence marker, never forwarded, and fails startup with fixed removal/OpenAI guidance. `Verify LLM` now returns a nullable whole-millisecond server duration measured only around the explicit probe; the UI and packaged acceptance retain only a bounded integer and never a key, provider body, or raw error.
