- **The token budget is reasoning-aware, so no call site had to change.** `max_completion_tokens` caps the reasoning pass and the visible output *together*, so every budget in the tree was sized wrong the moment the default model became a reasoning model — most sharply `ProbeAsync`, whose 4-token probe could never emit "OK" and would have reported a correctly-configured API key as **unhealthy** on the *Verify LLM* button. `OpenAiLlmProvider.ResolveMaxCompletionTokens` adds a `ReasoningTokenHeadroom` of 4096 on top of the caller's visible-output budget for reasoning models only, leaving non-reasoning models byte-identical. It is a ceiling, not a reservation — unused tokens are never generated and never billed — so chat (2048) and capture triage (4096) keep their visible-output budgets intact instead of silently truncating into `finish_reason=length` and degrading to the static classifier / deterministic extractor.
- `IsReasoningModel` excludes `-chat` variants: `gpt-5-chat-latest` is the non-reasoning chat model inside the GPT-5 family and still receives `temperature`.

Windows v0.1.1 public release (2026-08-21):
- **v0.1.1 is publicly shipped from exact `main` `e338566d`.** The annotated tag (`65c55bfc`) peels to `e338566d0f3d1fc65004beb5a29c10778e6e61bc`; the [GitHub release](https://github.com/Chris0Jeky/Taskdeck/releases/tag/v0.1.1) is latest, non-draft, and non-prerelease. Its Windows ZIP is 53,258,784 bytes with SHA-256 `e8bcbf36dfce28b75be7ee89842a6d5b2c248535f6c11678d06b2cb91c4c607b`; checksum and provenance sidecars are published beside it. The public `ghcr.io/chris0jeky/taskdeck:0.1.1` image is anonymous-pullable at digest `sha256:a04edc297416d149934b688227cf8c34f0e7add80320f7e2350f59d7f09bdd8e` with version `0.1.1` and revision `e338566d` labels.
- **Every tag-triggered release lane is green.** Release Container `32505993267`, Release Security `32505993558`, CI Release `32505993464`, and Release Desktop `32505993397` all succeeded on the tagged commit; the desktop run published the three release assets.
- **The actual public download passed the Windows host journey.** The [durable public-artifact record](https://github.com/Chris0Jeky/Taskdeck/issues/1876#issuecomment-5373235996) distinguishes this proof from the earlier dry-run ZIP. Chrome supplied an internet-zone (`ZoneId=3`) ZIP whose rehash matched the published checksum. Windows Shell extracted exactly 147 expected files with 0 forbidden files; the extracted executable retained `ZoneId=3`, reported file version `0.1.1.0` / product version `0.1.1`, and is intentionally unsigned for this beta. The supplied shortcut worked from an unrelated working directory, the process listened only on `127.0.0.1:5000`, state went to isolated local app data, and the default browser opened `/login?redirect=/workspace/home`. This host showed no blocking SmartScreen intervention; reputation/policy can differ on another host.
- **The unchanged public ZIP passed required live OpenAI acceptance.** Schema-6 evidence binds the run to `OpenAI` / `gpt-5.6-luna` / `llm-triage.v2`, a non-Mock probed/verified provider, proposal states `0 -> 1 -> 3`, review-first card counts `0/0/1`, one applied operation, and one card after restart. Separate clean-install and synthetic v0.1 migration journeys proved identity create/reuse, adjacent-config retention, durable app-data import, database reuse, board creation, signed-in restart, and no forbidden credential, prompt, transcript, provider-response, configuration-path, filesystem-path, or application-log evidence field.
- **Clean Windows Sandbox isolation is explicitly waived, not passed.** Windows Sandbox remained unable to create an environment after supported Store-package reset and retry, with deterministic Filter Manager `0xC03A001C` (`STATUS_VHD_INVALID_STATE`) failures. The maintainer [authorized the strongest truthful host fallback](https://github.com/Chris0Jeky/Taskdeck/issues/1876#issuecomment-5372863400); the host evidence above must not be generalized into a genuinely clean-machine or universal SmartScreen claim.
- **PRs `#1880`–`#1889` are merged.** The wave isolates queue-shutdown persistence (`#1641`), moves desktop bootstrap identity to durable app data (`#1242`), limits the 0.1.x desktop train to Windows x64 (`#1878`), records the JSON-enforcement boundary (`#1702`), fixes first-specific proposal-feedback semantics (`#1636`), ships the packaged runtime and archive-local quick start (`#1876`/`#1877`), retires Gemini, and proves the remaining OpenAI migration and packaged-identity seams (`#1879`/`#1876`).
- **The release proof exercises the artifact after ZIP creation.** PR `#1885` finished with 62 focused runtime/FirstRun tests, Python 11/11, release-structure 58/58, 33/33 hosted checks, and an exact-head blank-tag post-ZIP rehearsal. PR `#1886` finished with release-structure 60/60, 37/37 hosted checks, an exact-head clean-runner content/untouched-ZIP rehearsal, and a local 147-file inventory with all 7 required paths and 0 forbidden files. PR `#1888` then proved the downloaded ZIP with OpenAI / `gpt-5.6-luna`, non-Mock/probed/verified, review-first card counts 0/0/1, and one card after restart.
- **PR `#1889` proves identity reuse without retaining identity material.** Exact-head no-publish run `32419422433` tested the untouched ZIP and skipped release creation; the downloaded inner archive stayed byte-identical. Evidence schema v3 records only `jwtCreated` and `connectorCreated`: both are `true` on the clean first launch and `false` on restart against the same isolated app-data state. No secret value, secret-derived hash, configuration path, or application log enters the marker or evidence.
- **PR `#1901` adds value-free packaged migration evidence without claiming the human gate.** Evidence schema v4 proves a synthetic v0.1 adjacent config is retained, its identity is imported into durable app data, the existing app-data database is reused, and a board is created there. Exact-head no-publish run `32444016514` passed the untouched-ZIP gate and skipped release creation; local offline and live-OpenAI rehearsals also passed.
- **Merged PR `#1908` closes `#1903` with separate mandatory clean-install and migration journeys.** Clean install proves bootstrap identity is created once and reused on restart; migration separately proves the adjacent source is retained, durable app-data identity is imported, the existing app-data database is reused, and a board survives create/restart. The full parsed durable JSON object must equal the synthetic fixture, including the non-identity `ArchiveAcceptance.Sentinel`; formatting-only reserialization remains valid. The archive harness passed 26/26 and the release-structure contract passed 60/60. Exact-head no-publish run `32453648121` succeeded through the untouched-ZIP gate and skipped `Create GitHub Release`; that pre-release checkpoint is now superseded by the public-artifact proof above.
- **PRs `#1902` and `#1909` bound the identity-bound demo seeder without weakening its one-socket guarantee.** Ordinary run-bound responses keep a 10,000 ms deadline; only the exact provider chat-message `POST` receives 700,000 ms, covering up to three 30-second tool rounds, the supported 600-second Ollama fallback, and 10 seconds of response-delivery allowance. Expiry destroys the transport and fails without retry or a replacement connection.
- **PRs `#1913`, `#1915`, and `#1920` make protected demo reset repeatable without surfacing retired artifacts or mutating before authentication succeeds.** Archived reset generations remain available as explicit history but disappear from active Inbox/Review lists and Home/Today counts; reset inventory validation and collaborator authentication both complete before quarantine or replacement writes. PRs `#1926` and `#1928` separately keep the cross-platform launcher gate deterministic and make Windows stop tolerate only the proven transient post-`taskkill` unknown-identity state while retaining fail-closed PID-reuse checks.
- **PRs `#1922` and `#1924` make the packaged live-provider gate fail closed and credential-contained.** A local `-LiveOpenAI` request cannot turn into a credential-unavailable skip, and the packaged journey now proves Transcript capture -> Inbox triage -> review -> apply -> restart with exact OpenAI provenance. The Playwright child receives no supported, retired, deployment, or compatibility provider credential alias, including mixed-case legacy Gemini aliases.
- **Final pre-release candidate proof passed on exact `main` `f9d9b84c`.** Required CI run `32488801335` completed with all 18 jobs accepted (16 success, 2 intentional skips), including the dynamically scheduled E2E Smoke job. No-publish workflow run `32488879613` built and tested the untouched inner `taskdeck-v0.0.0-dryrun+f9d9b84-win-x64.zip` (artifact `9449039716`, SHA-256 `d4e5ea79eb8932e0a434ef7630fa5e3eb88377564b16f72730def7dfc10b79af`) and skipped `Create GitHub Release`. A separate mandatory local `-LiveOpenAI` run of that downloaded ZIP passed schema-6 evidence; the tagged public-artifact run above is the release proof that supersedes it.
- **The current desktop workflow builds one self-contained `win-x64` ZIP.** The four-platform v0.1.0 release remains historical fact; it is not the current 0.1.x topology. The 2026-08-21 reconciliation satisfied `#1877` and treated `#1876` as satisfied through public proof plus the clean-Sandbox waiver; the 2026-08-22 ordinary-profile incident above supersedes only the `#1876` disposition and reopens it. Issue `#1242` remains open for its separate maintainer acceptance of the redacted identity record. Issue `#1878` remains closed/Done after the Windows-only topology and canonical record were directly reconciled.

Continuation pass 2 — 15 PRs merged (2026-08-19, main `f8ce5317` → `0fa9ccfa`):
- **i18n rollout step 2 is done, step 3 is blocked on a budget decision.** The Paper **Review** surface is extracted into `review.*` catalogs for `en`/`it`/`es` — 258 keys per locale, all-or-nothing for the surface per ADR-0054 §8, covering visible copy, `aria-label`s, `aria-live`/`role="status"` text, keyboard hints, `window.prompt` and toast text, and the copy the composables own as data (`#1852`). `ApplyToBoardDialog` and two shared Review composables are mounted by the *Legacy* shell too, so Legacy's phase-2 dialog and toasts now follow the active locale; the `en` strings are unchanged byte for byte, so a default-locale user sees exactly what they saw before. `CohortDashboard` is deliberately step 3 (an analytics page, not part of the decision loop), stated so its absence does not read as an oversight. At this delivery point, two strings that were resolved **once** into a plain `ref` at fetch/error time — `mapConfidence`'s then-current operation-safety label and `TranscriptEvidenceViewer`'s error copy — were changed to follow a live language switch instead of freezing the locale that produced them (`#1869`, `#1857`). PR `#2160` later retired that synthetic operation-safety confidence label: `mapConfidence` now keeps exact model-reported operation keys stable across locale switches, while the transcript error still re-resolves from its raw key. **Step 3 (secondary surfaces) is blocked:** the Review extraction alone took total JS from 1163.18 KB to 1201.15 KB, the CI gate was deliberately raised 1200 → 1250 KB (`scripts/ci/check-bundle-size.mjs`, dated rationale in the constant's comment), and how the rest of the rollout is budgeted against that gate — including whether the gate should measure the eager graph instead, which would make lazy loading a real lever — is `#1858`. Translation *quality* for it/es is still unreviewed and `#1770` stays open for it.
- **Paper substrate and dialog geometry.** The last four ADR-0053 per-token follow-ups shipped and `#1842` closed (`#1865`): the eyebrow-token ruling was **measured before it was applied** — all four core-loop surfaces already rendered `.tk-eyebrow` at `--mute`, so thirteen deviating roots moved to it and all 20 Paper roots that have an eyebrow now agree, with `--ember` deliberately reserved for genuine card-level accents; notification badge hues moved to `--td-notify-*-{bg,fg}` tokens; and two guards that could pass vacuously were made honest. `TdDialog` — the shared primitive behind every confirmation — is now bound to the **visual** viewport instead of the layout viewport, so a software keyboard can no longer sit over its Cancel/confirm actions; the `visualViewport` observer `#1741` added inline to `CardModal` is extracted to `composables/useVisualViewport.ts` and both call sites share it, with an explicit no-`visualViewport` fallback per call site and the viewport-derived heights inside an `@supports (height: 100dvh)` block (`#1821`). Real-mobile keyboard verification is human-only and remains open.
- **Write-access lanes finished, and the evidence viewer stopped advertising dead ends.** `#1836` closed across two PRs. `AutomationPolicyEngine.ValidateBoardAccessAsync` now takes a required `BoardAccessBar` lane: the **Write** lane (proposal creation, validation, execution) moved from mere membership to `UserRole.Editor`, so a Viewer's proposal now fails at validation with an explicit `Forbidden` outcome rather than surviving to execute, while the **Read** lane (terminal stored-preview and MCP `proposal_detail`/diff reads) deliberately stays membership-gated so a demoted-to-Viewer member keeps reading their own proposals — defense in depth behind the `#1794`/`#1827` API bar, and items enqueued by read-only members before `#1827` are rejected at validation, which is the intended tightening (`#1861`). `BoardDto` gained a **server-computed** `CanWrite` — Owner/Admin/Editor plus board *ownership*, the case a `BoardAccess`-derived signal gets wrong because owners hold no access row — served by a new batched `GetWritableBoardIdsAsync` so the list endpoint does not become N+1; the Paper Inbox pickers now render read-only boards visibly **disabled and labelled `view-only`** rather than filtering them silently, and mapping sites with no acting user fail closed at `false` (`#1870`). Separately, provenance is board-authorized while `GET /api/transcripts/{id}` stays owner-only, so a board collaborator was shown a "View in transcript" button that could only land on "no longer available"; transcript read was **not** widened to board scope — `ProvenanceEvidenceLinkDto` carries a server-computed `Viewable` flag and the affordance is suppressed instead (`#1863`, `#1837`).
- **Backup retention, release refs, and agent-tooling guards.** Pre-migration snapshots are now keyed on the database's **full file name** and ordered by a monotonic sequence suffix rather than by wall-clock time, so two differently-named databases sharing one backup directory cannot prune each other's snapshots (identically-named databases from different directories pointed at one absolute backup directory still share a key — see `CONFIGURATION_REFERENCE.md` for the exact mechanism) and a clock that steps backwards cannot make the newest snapshot look oldest and delete it first (`#1849`, `#1839`; `CONFIGURATION_REFERENCE.md` updated). `release-container.yml` built its smoke-test image ref by stripping only a leading `v`, retaining `+<build>` metadata that is not a legal Docker tag character — a failure reachable only on a real tag ref, which no dispatch rehearsal exercises (`#1847`, `#1843`). The checkout-fingerprint guard had a blind spot that is exactly the leak class it exists to catch: a clean-to-clean `git switch` or a commit between Capture and Compare reported `unchanged` for a checkout now pointing at entirely different content; Capture now records HEAD's commit and symbolic ref, Compare reports `ref-moved`/`head-moved`, and the harness is wired into `reusable-docs-governance.yml` (`#1850`, `#1832`). `worktree_guard` now validates a worktree by **substance** rather than path shape (`#1851`, `#1833` — see `docs/WORKTREE_AGENT_PROTOCOL.md`). The read-only inventory wrapper's argument policy gained a CR/LF carve-out so a multi-line GraphQL document can reach `gh api` (`#1848`, `#1838`); the `PYLAUNCHER_*` trio joined the fail-closed launcher-input gate in `check-codex-path-trust.ps1`, since the install pair can make `py -3` fetch a Store interpreter the verifier never inventoried and `DRYRUN` turns a validated execution into a no-op that still exits 0 (`#1862`, `#1651`). Also: an expiry-boundary integration test de-flaked (`#1845`, `#1822`) and `@vue/devtools-api` declared as an explicit dependency rather than relying on `vue-router` hoisting it to satisfy pinia's required peer (`#1846`, `#1834`).

Post-ship open-PR reconciliation + backlog burn-down (2026-08-19, autonomous continuation pass, main `4353ecdb` → `9c3bfb86`):
- **The open-PR queue is empty.** All 15 PRs left open after the v0.1.0 ship were reconciled through the review-and-ship gate (one fresh-context adversarial review each — Codex was quota-dead the whole pass — plus green exact-head CI and the aging floor): ready ones merged, four Dependabot PRs recreated on current main so no stale lockfile could revert the `#1811` alert sweep, and `#1697` closed as superseded by `#1762`. Recorded review defects were fixed *and re-verified* before merge: the two Codex P1 execution-escapes in the read-only inventory wrapper (`#1759`), the verbatim-string tokenizer false-negative in the CLI launch-invariant guard (`#1696`), and both `#1651` trust HIGHs in the trusted-only Codex PATH (`#1675`).
- **The dogfooding-quality UX pack shipped:** the two-phase Apply is now legible (`#1818` — the approve→execute invariant is unchanged, only the missing feedback was the defect), Paper/Legacy substrate legibility is guarded across every restyled view root (`#1813`/`#1815`), the five `--td-surface-*` depth tokens are declared at `:root` (`#1814`, fixing transparent Legacy login/MFA modals), wave-2 spec assertions were hardened (`#1816`), and the review cadence chip now shows real weekly data (`#1802`).
- **Security/hardening for the shared instance:** board-targeted triage now requires write access, not just read (`#1794` — a read-only member can no longer queue proposals into a board they cannot approve into); the `release-desktop.yml` dispatch path is hardened and its fail-closed no-publish behavior was proven by two live rehearsal dispatches (`#1795`/`#1806`); the dual-major-`ws` line was investigated and documented-accepted (`#1812` — no upstream `@microsoft/signalr` release rides `ws` 8.x, and the nested 7.x copy is provably never bundled into the browser).
- Every non-blocking review finding became a tracked follow-up issue rather than a fix-commit cascade.

i18n seed — Italian and Spanish selectable on Home, Inbox and Boards (2026-08-19, `#1770`, **ADR-0054**):
- **Taskdeck is no longer English-only.** `vue-i18n@^11` runs in composition mode (`legacy: false`, `globalInjection: true`) from a single instance in `frontend/taskdeck-web/src/i18n/index.ts`, installed in `main.ts`. Message catalogs are per-surface TypeScript modules under `src/locales/{en,it,es}/<surface>.ts`; `en` is both the source and the fallback, and missing-key/fallback warnings are **off on purpose** — during a surface-by-surface rollout a locale with no entry for a not-yet-extracted surface must fall back to English silently, not shout in the console.
- **Extracted so far: Home (`PaperHomeView`), Inbox (`PaperInboxView`), Boards (`BoardsListView`), and the Appearance/preferences surface.** The **Review** surface was deliberately NOT extracted *in this seed* — deferred because three Review PRs (`#1830`, `#1825`, `#1835`) were landing the same day and a parallel copy extraction there would have collided line-for-line. **Superseded later the same day: Review shipped as ADR-0054 rollout step 2 via `#1852` (see the continuation-pass entry at the top of this file).** `#1770` stays open for the remaining surfaces and for native it/es translation-quality review. Legacy (Obsidian) shells are not extracted at all: ADR-0038 froze them, so translation budget does not go there.
- **Language is selectable in Settings → Appearance and persists**, on exactly the pattern `paperThemeStore` already set: a Pinia store (`store/localeStore.ts`) writing `localStorage` key `td.locale.v1`, validating on read, defaulting on garbage, and applying to the i18n runtime plus `<html lang>`. Switching applies **live**, no reload. It is a client display preference — not sent to or stored by the backend. Options are labelled with endonyms (English / Italiano / Español), each carrying its own `lang` attribute.
- **Structural parity is a CI check, not a hope.** `src/tests/i18n/catalogs.spec.ts` walks all three catalogs and fails on missing keys, stale keys, empty values, interpolation-placeholder mismatch (set membership, not order — word order legitimately moves between languages), and plural-segment mismatch. It reads whatever is in `src/locales`, so it grows with each extracted surface with no registration list. What it does **not** check is whether the Italian and Spanish are any good — tone is a review responsibility, and the catalogs carry a per-locale register note and a per-surface glossary to review against.
- Board dates format through `Intl` against the active locale, and `BoardsListView` preserves the browser's *region* only when the browser's primary language subtag matches the active app locale — so an `en-GB` browser on app-locale `en` keeps 19/08/2026 rather than jumping to 8/19/2026. Residual: the default app locale is `en` with no `navigator.language` detection, so a user on a `de-DE`/`fr-FR`/`pt-BR` browser who never opens the switcher *does* move from their own date format to US format. Seeding the default from `navigator.language` is tracked follow-up (browser-language default seeding is a product decision; behavior unchanged by this seed).

Pre-migration SQLite auto-backup + `UPGRADING.md` (2026-08-19, `#1803`, data-trust pack part 1):
- **A schema migration no longer rewrites the user's database with no way back.** Every host mode (API, CLI, MCP HTTP, MCP stdio) goes through `SerializedMigrator`, which now takes a consistent snapshot of the SQLite file **before** `Database.Migrate()` opens any DDL connection, inside the existing cross-process migration lock. The snapshot uses SQLite's **online backup API** (`SqliteConnection.BackupDatabase`), not a file copy: Taskdeck runs in WAL mode (`#1130`), so a raw copy of the main file silently drops committed rows still sitting in an uncheckpointed `-wal`. The destination's WAL is checkpointed away before the file is moved into place, so each snapshot is a **standalone single file** — which is what makes the "backup = copy this one file" guidance in `UPGRADING.md` true. Prior art and the same conclusion for the dev-sandbox export/import path: `#1166`.
- **It runs only when it is worth running, and it fails closed.** Nothing is copied on an ordinary boot with an up-to-date schema, and nothing is copied on a first run that creates the database (there is no prior state). When migrations *are* pending against an existing file and the snapshot cannot be written, `PreMigrationBackupException` propagates out of startup and **the migration is not applied** — the error names the database, the backup directory, the cause, and the settings that resolve it. Retention *pruning* is deliberately not fail-closed (the protective copy already exists); pruning failures log a warning.
- **Configurable, with sane defaults:** `Database:Backup:Enabled` (`true`), `Database:Backup:RetainCount` (`5`), `Database:Backup:Directory` (default: a `backups/` folder next to the database file; a relative value resolves against the *database* directory, not the process working directory, because the four hosts do not share one). Documented in `docs/platform/CONFIGURATION_REFERENCE.md`; validated with `ValidateDataAnnotations().ValidateOnStart()` in `AddInfrastructure` so every host mode fails fast on a bad value.
- **`UPGRADING.md` now exists at the repo root**: where the database file lives per install mode, why Taskdeck must be stopped before copying it (WAL), the general upgrade procedure, downgrade/skip-version guidance, a v0.1.0 section (BREAKING: none) and a v0.1.1 section that now also records the Gemini-removal configuration migration, and **export documented as a headline** — full account GDPR JSON export, board JSON/CSV export, and the Settings → Export & Import view — all already shipped, pointer only.
- Coverage: 11 backend tests in `PreMigrationBackupTests`, including the ordering assertion that the snapshot's `__EFMigrationsHistory` does **not** contain the migration that was about to run, a WAL test that holds a writer connection open so the committed row is provably still uncheckpointed, standalone-snapshot (no `-wal`/`-shm`/`.tmp` sidecar) assertions, retention pruning keeping the newest N, non-managed files in the backup directory left untouched, and fail-closed (migration provably not applied after a failed backup).

**v0.1.0 SHIPPED** (2026-08-19, REVIVAL-07 `#1303`, maintainer q-2 ruling):
- **The first release tag exists**: `v0.1.0` on `a2ac87e8` (main + the `#1787` first-run triage fix, deliberately ordered so the release contains it). Post-merge CI on that exact commit: success. Public GitHub Release with all four self-contained platform archives (win-x64/linux-x64/osx-x64/osx-arm64) + SHA256 sums: https://github.com/Chris0Jeky/Taskdeck/releases/tag/v0.1.0 . `ghcr.io/chris0jeky/taskdeck:0.1.0` published by the tag run with a passing smoke test and **public** (anonymous-pull verified) visibility; `release-security` deep scan green on the tag.
- **Two latent release-lane defects surfaced by this first-ever tag push** (rehearsals structurally could not reach either): `ci-release.yml` failed at plan time (caller granted less than `reusable-gitleaks.yml` declares — GitHub refuses, it does not intersect), and `release-desktop.yml`'s publish job globbed the repo-tracked `artifacts/` directory into the asset upload (`read artifacts/openapi: is a directory`) after all four platform builds went green. The release was completed manually from the same run's checksum-verified artifacts (`sha256sum -c` OK ×4); both workflow fixes are in PR `#1801`.
- **The `release-desktop.yml` dispatch path is hardened** (`#1795`, `#1806`). A `resolve-source` job resolves one `(tag, commit)` pair before any build: the untrusted `inputs.tag` reaches Bash only through a step `env:` var and must clear `scripts/ci/validate-release-tag.sh` (strict release-tag grammar) before it touches a path, ref, output or `gh release` argument, and the tag is then resolved through the exact `git/ref/tags/<tag>` API with annotated tag objects dereferenced to their own commit — read once for both type and SHA, so a force-moved tag cannot pair fields across the move. Every downstream checkout is pinned to that commit and fails closed on a HEAD mismatch, no checkout persists Git credentials, and the resolved commit ships as a release provenance asset plus a pre-publish re-check. Publish is resumable — draft (adopted even when a prior failed run left one, via a listing that includes drafts), then per-asset `gh release upload --clobber` with bounded retries, then a flip to published — so the v0.1.0 create-and-roll-back failure mode cannot strand a release. Held by `scripts/ci/release-desktop-dispatch.test.mjs` in required CI (55 assertions; 16 of them fail against the pre-hardening workflow). Live blank-tag rehearsals now prove the fail-closed path; the latest exact-main run `32679854224` succeeded and skipped release creation.
- **Deliberately not in v0.1.0** (spun out at `#1303` close): pre-migration SQLite auto-backup + `UPGRADING.md` (`#1803` — **since delivered on main, see the entry at the top of this file**; it is in the next release, not in the v0.1.0 tag), version stamping (`#1804` — the shipped binaries carry default assembly versions; a self-hoster cannot yet ask "what version am I running?"). Binaries are unsigned (accepted for beta, `#1167`).
- `#1123` and `#1139` closed with re-scope notes per `#1303` AC6.

Board-less capture triage now rejected at the seam (2026-08-19, `#1764`):
- **Accepting a board-less capture no longer creates a doomed async triage job.** `POST /api/capture/items/{id}/triage` now resolves the target board synchronously: a capture with no board (Home quick-capture lands `BoardId = null`) is rejected up front with a `400 ValidationError` and a clear message, instead of returning `202` and letting the worker fail it permanently with a bare `FAILED` badge. This supersedes the CAP-05 line below that described board-less triage as failing deterministically at the worker under retry policy.
- **The endpoint accepts an optional `{ "boardId": "…" }` body.** When supplied for a board-less capture, the server verifies board **write** access (tightened from read by `#1794`, below), links the board (`BackfillBoard`), then triages in one step. The worker-side board requirement in `CaptureTriageService` remains as defense in depth. The Paper Inbox now requires choosing a board (inline picker) before accepting a board-less capture and surfaces the reason on a `FAILED` capture; `CaptureItemSummaryDto`/`CaptureItemSummary` now carry `ErrorMessage`.

Board-targeted triage now requires write access (2026-08-19, `#1794`, coordinator ruling on the issue):
- **Board-targeted proposal creation requires write access to the target board; approval still requires the approver role.** `CaptureService.EnqueueTriageAsync` gates the *effective* board — the board the generated proposal will target — on `IAuthorizationService.CanWriteBoardAsync`, so read-only (`UserRole.Viewer`) membership can no longer queue automation proposals into a shared board's review queue that only owners/approvers can clear. Write-capable is exactly what `BoardAccess.CanWrite()` admits — `Owner`, `Admin`, `Editor` — plus the board owner. The gate covers both entry points: the optional `{ "boardId": … }` triage body added by `#1787`, and a capture that already carries a board (capture-with-board, then accept with no body). A read-only caller gets `403 Forbidden` with an explicit reason and no board link is persisted; a capture with no board at all keeps its `#1764` `400 ValidationError` for every user. Approval and execution authorization are untouched — ADR-0003's proposal approve-then-execute gate is unchanged, so write access buys the right to *suggest*, not to mutate. The MCP server exposes no triage path (`create_capture` calls `CaptureService.CreateAsync`; the read resources call `ListAsync`/`GetByIdAsync`), so every triage entry point runs through this one gate. Capture *creation* against a readable board is deliberately still read-gated: a viewer may capture a note against a board they can see, and only the accept step is refused.

Transcript evidence deep link in Paper Review (2026-08-19, `#1305` AC4):
- **A proposal's evidence quote now opens the transcript it came from.** `GET /api/transcripts/{id}` returns one transcript owned by the authenticated caller — the canonical LF-normalized text plus its line-indexed speaker/timestamp segments. Identity is claims-only and ownership is part of the repository lookup predicate, so another user's transcript returns `404` with a response body identical to a nonexistent one; the endpoint cannot be used as a cross-user existence oracle. The text is returned whole (the domain's existing 200,000-character cap bounds it); there is no paging. This is a read surface: nothing on it mutates a transcript, a proposal, or a board.
- **The Paper provenance drawer consumes the evidence links it has been served since the typed-evidence slice.** `ProvenanceRowDto.EvidenceLinks` was already on the wire but no frontend type declared it and `ReviewMain` never passed it down (`#1284`); the selectors now flatten each row's links and the drawer renders them with a **View in transcript** affordance whenever a link is transcript evidence *and* carries a resolved span. The viewer fetches through `api/http.ts` and highlights the span in place, scrolled to centre. Spans are .NET `char` offsets and JavaScript string indices are the same UTF-16 code units, so multi-byte text highlights identically on both sides; a span landing on the trailing half of a surrogate pair widens to the whole code point. A link with null offsets — which is exactly how the backend records an ambiguous or unresolvable quote — renders as plain metadata with no affordance, so an unresolved span can never present itself as an exact quotation. Legacy Review is untouched.
- **Left open on `#1305` at the time of this block:** the `LlmRequest.Payload` retention boundary, provenance export, removal of the unused intent-envelope graph, and the human dogfooding follow-through. `#1305` was closed on 2026-08-27 by PR `#2144` (merge `e5c276e59`); the payload retention boundary is now carried by CF-01b `#2345`.

CI estate right-sizing verdict (2026-08-19, ARCHIVE-07 / `#1275`, **ADR-0052**):
- **The scheduled CI estate is right-sized to a keep/fix/kill/gate verdict per lane; every remaining workflow is green or its schedule is removed with a dated comment.** `ci-required.yml` (required gate) and `release-desktop.yml` are unchanged. `ci-nightly.yml` is green — both k6 jobs (Load and Concurrency Harness, Performance Regression Gate) passed on the last 8 consecutive nightlies (2026-08-11..08-18); the summary-export permission bug was fixed by `#1358`/PR `#1359` (`--user $(id -u):$(id -g)` plus a fail-closed `require-k6-summary.mjs` gate) and the tail thresholds were recalibrated by `#1449`/`#1445`. The board-write p95 signal is **not a code regression**: it is the documented single-writer SQLite write-convoy capacity ceiling (median ~12 ms, heavy-tailed p95 ≈ 2.0-3.0 s at 20 VUs), gated at a 4500 ms tail (1.5×) with the always-on aggregate `p(95)<2000` as a near-capacity warning.
- **The `mutation-testing.yml` weekly schedule is removed (manual `workflow_dispatch` retained).** The mutation score is a non-blocking calibration signal, not a merge gate, and the backend lane carries a 180-minute ceiling; the `#1500` repair (pinned Stryker.NET 4.16.0 + 33-check preflight) remains intact and provable on demand. `ci-release.yml` and `release-security.yml` are already gated to `push: tags: v*` + `release: published` + `workflow_dispatch` with no schedule trigger — appropriate for the parallel v0.1.0 tag ship — so they are kept as-is rather than removed. `pages-frontend.yml` is kept: the "Deploy to GitHub Pages" lane is green (e.g. 2026-08-19 run in ~40 s), superseding the stale 2026-07 deploy-timeout report. Branch protection on `main` (`#1173`) is classic protection with required checks limited to the three security scans (Dependency Security Signals, SAST Semgrep, Gitleaks), `required_approving_review_count: 0`, `enforce_admins: false`, and force-push/deletion disabled; `ci-required` is not itself a required context.

Execution authority and backlog admission (2026-08-18, **ADR-0051** / `#1269`):
- **Ordinary PR merge eligibility no longer depends on a human reviewer or owner click.** The live T3 declaration remains `push: free` / `merge: free` and now explicitly covers dependency, workflow, governance, and major-version PRs within task scope after exact-head `ci-required.yml`, canonical `review-and-ship`, and seam-specific proof. `CODEOWNERS` remains advisory review routing rather than merge eligibility. Blind check-only platform auto-merge is not enabled. DCO was removed from eligibility by the later 2026-08-23 owner decision recorded above.
- **The active plan now has a bounded autonomous backlog lane.** An authorized coordinator may promote acceptance-ready existing issues under ADR-0051 and `REVIVAL_PLAN.md` §5 without another owner decision, with at most four issue items in `Now` and eight in `Next`; `Now` requires complete dependencies and `Next` may be sequenced behind a named `Now`. The issue's single Priority label and Project `Priority` field must match before promotion. New tables/endpoints/mutation paths/views/security posture still need existing plan authority or an Accepted ADR. Credentials/private data, production mutations, release tags, legal/licensing/trademark decisions, repository/environment settings, destructive work-loss actions, and subjective dogfooding/beta judgments remain separately human-scoped.
- **The initial autonomous admission record is populated.** The first admitted set was `Now`: `#1206`, `#1633`, `#1661`, `#1664`; `Next`: `#1495`, `#1626`, `#1639`, `#1641`, `#1652`, `#1665`, `#1666`, `#1670`. This is a dated seeding record, not a current-status mirror: live ProjectV2 state is authoritative as issues move through `Review`, `Blocked`, and `Done`. Each admitted issue had exactly one Priority label; `#1652` follows the `#1661` sanitizer contract and `#1665` follows the `#1664` CLI-test seam.

Architectural-surprise watch list (2026-08-19):
- **Connectors:** the registry direction taxonomy (`ConnectorDirection`) is `Inbound`, `Context`, and `Outbound`, and is distinct from `ConnectorType` (`BrowserClipper`, `MarkdownImport`, `WebClip`, `GitHubIssueIntake`, `WebhookInbound`, `Custom`); registered connector records are lifecycle-managed only and do not currently ingest, route, or forward content. Adding a `ConnectorDirection`, a `ConnectorType`, or a connector runtime requires `REVIVAL_PLAN.md` §7 or a later Accepted ADR/plan amendment.
- **Top-level views:** the sidebar renders exactly four primary items — `Today`, `Review`, `Boards`, and `Inbox` (the `sidebarPrimary` entries in `ShellSidebar.vue`); search is served by the command palette, not a top-level view, and other advanced surfaces likewise remain reachable through the command palette or settings/footer. Adding or promoting a top-level view requires `REVIVAL_PLAN.md` §7 or a later Accepted ADR/plan amendment.
- These are authority boundaries, not a redesign of the shipped connector or navigation contracts; the canonical review-first and global review-and-ship pipeline still governs admitted work.

Log sanitization expansion (2026-08-18, `#1661` / PR `#1695`):
- **Application and API log wrappers now share one control-character contract.** The shared Application helper removes C0, DEL, and C1 code points before bounded telemetry, log-query, and unhandled-exception values reach structured sinks. Printable Unicode remains intact, and the change does not rewrite persisted user text or public API payloads. Unicode line/paragraph separators remain the sequenced `#1652` follow-up; surrogate-safe truncation is tracked separately in `#1700`.

Windows API timing diagnostics (2026-08-16, first bounded `#1682` slice):
- **Successful and failed API integration lanes now retain a sanitized timing summary for 14 days.** The reusable Ubuntu/Windows workflow derives class, method, outcome, and duration metadata from its TRX and uploads only the bounded JSON summary on every completed test run; stdout, error details, attachments, theory arguments, and other test content are never copied into it. Raw TRX remains failure-only. This instrumentation responds to two green exact-main Windows runs that took 32-34 minutes versus a contemporaneous 17-minute PR run; it does not identify or fix the cause, alter timeouts or parallelism, quarantine tests, or reduce coverage. `#1682` remains open for evidence from a subsequent recurrence and the narrow causal repair.

Transcript source linkage (2026-08-16, first bounded `#1305` slice):
- **Every newly processed transcript capture now gets one durable, immutable Transcript snapshot before provider work starts.** `LlmRequest.TranscriptId` is a nullable unique FK with `ON DELETE SET NULL`; the worker creates the LF-normalized, valid-UTF-16 Transcript and its queue linkage in one save, reuses that row on retry/replay, and sends the stored canonical text through triage. Transcript persistence now accepts the same 200,000-character limit as transcript capture. User edits are rejected after linkage so later evidence cannot drift away from the source snapshot. Capture detail exposes that server-computed edit capability without disclosing `TranscriptId`; Inbox hides the edit action and any stale open editor once linkage makes the capture immutable, while linked failures offer retry-or-ignore guidance instead of a dead-end edit path. The legacy queue payload still retains its text for compatibility, so portability/retention continues to account for both stores. Proposal approval/execution remains review-first.
- **Successful schema-v2 LLM triage now persists one opaque Transcript evidence reference per proposed operation.** The server resolves each verbatim quote to a unique absolute .NET UTF-16 `[start,end)` range over the linked LF-normalized Transcript; overlapping repeats or conflicting same-title observations retain the Transcript reference with null offsets instead of guessing, while a missing quote invalidates the LLM leg and deterministic fallback creates no evidence link. The existing provider/prompt contract remains v2 and never supplies authoritative offsets. Board-authorized provenance reads expose only the source type, GUID reference, fixed non-content label, and optional coordinates, never Transcript text. Each canonical Transcript source also has a typed FK enforced by a database check and `ON DELETE CASCADE`: erasure removes existing links, and a stale post-erasure proposal/link save fails atomically. Paper quote resolution/deep links shipped later the same week (see the 2026-08-19 entry above); provenance export, payload retirement, and removal of the unused intent-envelope graph remain later `#1305` slices.

Reliability and Paper review delivery (2026-08-13, merged PRs `#1655`, `#1667`, `#1668`, `#1663`, `#1669`, `#1648`, `#1659`, `#1672`, and `#1649`):
- **ProjectV2 priority synchronization now fails closed on unstable or malformed snapshots without hiding nontransactional Apply outcomes.** Bounded whole-snapshot restarts are limited to recognized count/stamp drift (`#1655`, merge `a76cca06`); malformed outer or nested pagination booleans are non-retryable (`#1667`, merge `226786ce`); and intrinsic overflow/premature-count faults outrank drift and cannot restart or write (`#1668`, merge `bbe578bd`). The authentication-free suite is 102/102. Pre-write restart exhaustion proves zero writes; post-Apply audit exhaustion may follow writes and therefore reports final state unknown.
- **The Windows CLI test runway is both bounded and materially faster.** Every real-process contract uses the shared launcher, whose 60-second outer deadline is derived from the 30-second migrator lock bound plus a 30-second command-completion budget. Default harnesses copy isolated bytes from one fully migrated, disposed SQLite template, while an explicit cold-start test still runs the complete migration path (`#1663`, merge `c51b3170`; `#1669`, merge `a4c2ac80`). The exact-head hosted Windows lane passed 127/127 in 2m28s after the prior recurrence failed after 7m33s.
- **Paper Review ordering and filter fallback now preserve review-first attention.** Risk ordering is stable and malformed, null, or unknown risk values fail safe to Critical (`#1648`, merge `61f7cd53`). When a queue filter removes the current proposal, the view selects the first non-expired actionable proposal in risk order before any read-only fallback, while preserving an explicit/hash selection that remains visible (`#1659`, merge `412bdff3`). A later filter change now preserves the proposal actually displayed after that fallback instead of restoring a removed stale selection source (`#1672`, merge `6f46bc6d`).
- **The data-model reference now states the current MFA-at-rest boundary.** `MfaCredential.Secret` is stored as plaintext SQLite `TEXT`; production MFA remains blocked on the encryption, key-management, migration, rotation, and fail-closed work tracked by `#1653` (`#1649`, merge `a565f3cd`). This is a truth correction, not an encryption claim, and the maintainer-owned `#1470` record checkbox remains open.

Licensing decision (2026-08-12, maintainer-decided, **ADR-0050**):
- **The current Taskdeck open-source core is GPL-3.0-only.** The full GPLv3 text is now the root `LICENSE`; package and API metadata, contributor guidance, public documentation, desktop release archives, and runtime container images are aligned. Earlier versions and copies received under MIT keep those grants, with the former notice retained in `LICENSES/MIT.txt`; the current project is not offered under MIT as an alternative. `LICENSING.md` records the free-core and separately licensed `ee/` boundaries. ADR-0050 explicitly supersedes ADR-0044 Decision 3 and the MIT-forever portion of REVIVAL-03.

Frontend spec type-checking (2026-08-12, `#1468` / `#1634` / ADR-0049):
- **The frontend spec tree is type-checked by Required CI for the first time.** Before this, no gate type-checked a single spec file: `tsconfig.app.json` excluded `src/tests/**` from `vue-tsc -b`, and Vitest transpiles specs without checking them, so a spec could reference a property that does not exist while typecheck, lint, build and the test run all stayed green — which is exactly how `#1462` shipped. A new `frontend/taskdeck-web/tsconfig.vitest.json` covers `src/tests/**` and is referenced from the root `tsconfig.json`, so the existing `npm run typecheck` step in `reusable-frontend-unit.yml` builds it on both matrix legs. **No CI workflow file was changed.**
- **244 of the 302 spec files a full Vitest run executes are gated; the current 40-file quarantine is explicit and shrink-only.** PR `#1634` fixed the 24 one-error files and reduced the quarantine from 64 files / 415 errors to **40 files / 391 errors**. The historical 2026-08-07 baseline was 415 pre-existing type errors across those 64 files, measured over the 286 TypeScript files under `src/tests/` (284 specs plus `setup.ts` and one mock; repo `types` unchanged), zero outside `src/tests/`. The files remain named individually in the project's `exclude` array rather than suppressed by a loosened compiler option — the project mirrors `tsconfig.app.json`'s options exactly, including `noUnusedLocals`/`noUnusedParameters`/`erasableSyntaxOnly` (which account for 4 of the historical 415, not enough to justify a second strictness bar). A **new** spec file under `src/tests/` is checked by default, because it cannot be on a list written today; that property is the point. Burn-down is `#1607`, which records the per-file counts and the config changes that must not be made — notably adding `"node"` to `types`, which clears the `process` errors and emits exactly one new one: `PaperHomeView.vue(238,5): TS2322: Type 'number' is not assignable to type 'Timeout'`, because `greetingTimer` is annotated `ReturnType<typeof window.setInterval>` and that resolves to node's `Timeout` while the call still returns the DOM `number`.
- **`expectTypeOf` is now usable in ordinary specs, and is used.** It erases at runtime, so the assertion is discharged by `vue-tsc -b` rather than by the Vitest run — `vitest --typecheck` was not needed. `src/tests/api/automationApi.spec.ts` now pins `Proposal.approvedRevisionId`, closing the last `#1462` gap: its runtime tests structurally could not pin the declaration. The `#1469` derived-alias workaround in `types/automation.ts` is retained as a belt-and-braces guard that holds from inside production source. Mutation-verified three ways: deleting the member (`TS2339`), making it optional (`TS2344`), and dropping its nullability (`TS2344`) each fail the gate. Two further probes confirm the gate itself fires — a type error in a non-quarantined spec, and a brand-new spec file, both fail `npm run typecheck` at exit 2.
- **Not covered, stated so the gate is not overread.** A full Vitest run executes **302** spec files (284 under `src/tests/`, 18 under the frontend-root `tests/`); this project gates **244**. The **58** it does not gate are the 40 quarantined files plus those **18 frontend-root specs**, which Vitest runs but no tsconfig includes — they import the `.mjs` files under `scripts/` and use `process`/`NodeJS`, so they need a Node type environment and therefore a separate fourth project (measured with this project's options: 54 errors in the run — 42 across 15 of the 18 specs, plus 12 in three `playwright.*.ts` helpers pulled in as dependencies — dominated by `TS7016` on the untyped `.mjs` imports). `src/stories/**` (17 files) is likewise unchecked and is a distinct surface with a distinct type environment (ADR-0030). All three residues are tracked in `#1607`. The quarantine itself is enforced by convention, not by a check — nothing mechanically stops a future PR from appending to `exclude`, and TypeScript never reports a quarantine entry whose file was renamed or deleted.

Correctness and dependency wave (2026-08-12, merged PRs `#1622`, `#1623`, `#1624`, `#1627`, `#1629`, `#1630`, `#1631`, `#1634`):
- **Current shipped state includes the dependency refresh and bounded correctness repairs.** The npm minor-patch group (`#1622`, merge `580022f9`) and NuGet/.NET minor-patch group (`#1623`, merge `225b42c7`) are merged; #1623 updates `ModelContextProtocol` and `ModelContextProtocol.AspNetCore` from 2.0.0 to 2.1.0. `#1624` (merge `20f6ae40`) makes `OUTSTANDING_TASKS.md` section lettering unambiguous. The buffered-provider refusal path now persists a sanitized degraded assistant message (`#1627`, merge `a976a79d`); standalone MCP HTTP requests no longer fail with an endpoint-stage 500 (`#1630`, merge `09426292`); and shutdown-abandoned queue claims are released without charging a retry (`#1631`, merge `587da0a5`).
- **`#1629` (merge `0b6ba54b`) recertified `DATA_MODEL.md` against the live EF model and snapshot.** The GitHub issue is closed, but its maintainer-owned record checkbox remains unchecked; this status note does not infer that acknowledgement.

Initial log sanitization delivery (2026-08-12, PR `#1650`, merge `c03e3347`):
- User-controlled request metadata is sanitized before API exception logging, with bounded application telemetry/log-query sanitization and CR/LF regression coverage. Focused evidence passed 32 Application and 4 API middleware tests; the 2026-08-18 `#1661` expansion above supersedes this section's character-set boundary. The accepted local-first browser-token follow-up remains tracked separately in `#1644`.

OpenAI-compatible provider replacement shipped (2026-08-10, PR `#1537`; `#1306` closed 2026-08-30 on this shipped half — the streaming remainder is `#2241`):
- **A separately configured `OpenAICompatible` provider now supports public OpenAI Chat Completions endpoints without weakening the safe `Mock` default.** Selection validates the explicit base URL, model, API key, timeouts, response/SSE byte ceilings, and optional non-secret gateway headers. Buffered completion, structured instruction extraction, board context, real upstream SSE, and explicit buffered-stream degradation share sanitized error behavior and preserve token/provider/model provenance.
- **The registered client extends the direct-only telemetry boundary and adds fixed-origin egress enforcement.** The effective chain is circuit policy → protected telemetry → `EgressEnvelopeHandler` → dispatch tracking → direct sockets; it disables proxies and redirects, validates DNS at connect time, preserves the configured origin for transport, and records whether a request crossed the transport boundary so quota reservations are released only for proven pre-dispatch failures. Compatible-provider Polly state and the companion provider circuit state are tracked independently with generation-safe half-open leases. Exact `localhost` plain HTTP is limited to `Development`, `Test`, or `Testing` with the explicit live-provider gate; numeric loopback and other private/link-local origins remain blocked.
- **The guarded implementation is on `main`; real-provider validation and true streaming remain open on `#2241` (re-filed from `#1306`, closed 2026-08-30).** PR `#1537` merged as `0b0c9c70` after exact-head hosted CI completed with 26 successes / 11 intentional skips / 0 failures. Local exact-head evidence passed 157/157 focused Application tests, 81/81 focused API tests, 22/22 Architecture tests with one intentional skip, and Actionlint across all 32 workflows; the earlier provider tree also passed the full serialized backend with 7,660 tests, five intentional skips, and no failures. At the time of this block, `#1306` was held open until a maintainer supplied a compatible-provider key and verified a visibly incremental stream in the real UI. It closed on 2026-08-30 on its shipped `OpenAICompatible` half, and that remaining verification moved to `#2241`, which is still open. The six nonblocking MEDIUM compatibility/readiness follow-ups were recorded on `#1306`; the buffered-refusal consumer defect was tracked as MEDIUM `#1617`, now closed. LOW `#1618` tracks the stale status sentence corrected in this docs-only slice.


Transcript map-reduce delivery (2026-08-02, REVIVAL-08 M2 / `#1304`):
- **Long transcript triage is now bounded map-reduce, not an oversized single provider request.** Transcript input accepts up to 200,000 characters while ordinary capture bounds remain unchanged. The planner uses a conservative token estimate, prefers speaker-turn and blank-line boundaries only when a budget-forced split is needed, retains bounded overlap, and caps provider calls. An under-budget transcript remains exactly one provider request even when it contains speaker turns. Each map leg goes through the existing provider-health, kill-switch, quota, provenance, and schema-validation guards; a failed leg discards all partial map output and retains the deterministic fallback for the complete capture. Provider-call progress pulses keep `/health/ready` bounded to one selected-provider timeout rather than falsely evicting a healthy long map-reduce run. The result still creates reviewable proposals only: approval and execution remain separate explicit actions. This completes the `#1304` M2 long-transcript milestone; the subsequent M3 schema-v2/evidence-quote contract and REVIVAL-09 linkage are separate scoped work.

Transcript triage visibility delivery (2026-08-02, `#1574`):
- **The Inbox keeps its existing two-second triage poll active for up to 450 scheduled attempts (roughly 15 minutes at the normal cadence) rather than stopping after 30 seconds.** That keeps default hosted-provider map-reduce work visible while retaining terminal/error/cancellation handling, one active poll, manual refresh, and the proposal-first review boundary. Provider-aware elapsed deadlines for slower supported configurations are deliberately tracked separately in `#1585`.

Transcript schema-v2 contract delivery (2026-08-02, REVIVAL-08 M3 contract slice / `#1304`):
- **LLM transcript triage now uses a strict, server-stamped `llm-triage.v2` contract while the deterministic fallback remains v1.** Model output must contain an exact task property set: title, lowercase type (`action`/`decision`/`question`), nullable bounded assignee and calendar-date hints, bounded confidence, and a bounded nonblank evidence quote. Unknown, duplicate, missing, wrongly typed, noncanonical, or over-limit output falls back for the whole capture/map leg rather than being normalized, truncated, or partially retained. Each quote must be an ordinal substring of the exact provider chunk; the existing proposed-card description preserves it for review. At this 2026-08-02 slice, classification, assignee, due-date, and confidence metadata were transient and excluded from executable proposal parameters. Update 2026-08-23 (`b6c8699f`): `dueDateHint` now materializes into the reviewed create-card proposal parameters, while type and assignee remain transient. Update 2026-08-28 (`#1307` AC1 / PR `#2160`): confidence now crosses a trusted application-only seam into source-labelled provenance and Paper Review; it remains presentation-only and excluded from executable parameters. The original slice added no migration, API/UI evidence surface, identity resolution, or automatic board mutation; `#1305` was still open for its separate durable-linkage residuals at the time of this block, and closed on 2026-08-27 via PR `#2144`.

Post-merge reconciliation (2026-08-01, PRs `#1556`, `#1558`, and `#1559`):
- **REVIVAL-09 has a durable transcript persistence foundation and live capture linkage.** (`#1305` was still open when this block was written; it closed on 2026-08-27 via PR `#2144`.) A user-owned `Transcript` stores normalized LF text and optional line-indexed speaker/timestamp segments. It may reference a board, originating capture, and `SourceArtefact`; deleting the board or artefact nulls the corresponding FK without deleting the transcript. PR `#1556` (`bfd4e95a`, merge `3182c3ac`) shipped user-scoped deterministic reads, buffered/streaming export, account deletion, and reversible bootstrap-tested persistence. The first linkage slice now creates and reuses the canonical Transcript during triage while retaining `LlmRequest.Payload` as a compatibility duplicate. Evidence spans, the additive provenance API, and the Paper deep-link UI have since shipped. The trailing `#1305` list that used to sit here is historical: PR `#2144` (merge `e5c276e59`) deleted the unused intent-envelope graph and documented the retention boundary, and the `LlmRequest.Payload` retention boundary itself is carried by CF-01b `#2345`.
- **MCP `create_card` now persists one usable column contract through proposal review and Apply.** When `column_id` is omitted, the write tool resolves the board's deterministic first column before saving the proposal; inaccessible boards, wrong-board columns, and columnless boards fail before an unusable proposal can be created. Preview, approve, and execute therefore consume the same canonical `columnId`, while the MCP path remains proposal-first and never mutates the board directly. PR `#1558` (`a5ebc160`, merge `cbf12e5d`) closed `#1354`; the current-main `McpToolsTests` run passed 33/33.
- **Hosted PostgreSQL evidence now fails closed on skipped container cases.** PR `#1559` (`5f721cae`, merge `6a92516c`) closed `#1520`: the reusable lane sets `TASKDECK_REQUIRE_DOCKER=true`, proves a forced-unavailable negative control fails for the expected reason, and validates the positive TRX by fully qualified PostgreSQL test identity rather than accepting same-name host-native tests. The hosted exact-head lane passed; current-main local proof passed the verifier's 5 tests and the expected Dockerless 7 passed / 28 skipped contract. Dockerless green remains graceful-gate evidence only, never PostgreSQL parity proof.
- **Agent merge authority is declaration-driven, not owner-click-only by default.** Read `.agent-harness/tier.json` live rather than copying its values into operational docs; merge disposition comes from the canonical global pipeline plus explicit task scope. The stale blanket prohibition in the Codex autonomy runbook is retired. Taskdeck's repository-specific exact-head Required CI evidence and separately scoped human decisions/external mutations remain intact.

Taskdeck-owned runtime-hook retirement (2026-07-31, `#1552`):
- **Taskdeck no longer installs repo-owned Claude or Codex runtime hooks.** `.claude/settings.json` retains normal development permissions, worktree symlinks, and project MCP enablement, but has no `hooks` object or `permissions.deny` list; the authority declaration remains in `.agent-harness/tier.json`, and Required CI, global laws, worktree guards, plus any independently configured user/organization/runtime controls remain effective. Absence of project hooks is not a claim that those external layers are disabled.
- **The retired Claude layer was measurable development friction.** It duplicated the global PreToolUse dispatcher with another Python process per Bash call, denied six of eight benign raw-text probes tracked by `#1475`, ran commit-time build/typecheck and reminder hooks, and automatically captured tool failures that could include timeout-shaped false positives. The dead PreToolUse/PostToolUse/PostToolUseFailure/pre-commit/smoke handlers are removed. Manual, deliberate failure recording remains through `render_failure_ledger.py`, its 11-test synchronization suite, and Required Docs Governance.
- **Tracker disposition follows the posture change.** `#1456` and PR `#1538` are closed as not planned; `#1293`, `#1475`, `#1476`, and `#1497` are superseded by removal rather than a wider local floor. Reusable consumer-retirement, duplicate-hook diagnosis, Python-launcher, and guarded-worktree lessons are upstream in `agent-harness#188`–`#191`.

Proxy-safe direct egress shipped checkpoint (2026-07-27, `#1513` / PR `#1516`):
- **At this 2026-07-27 delivery point, the registered OpenAI, Gemini, Ollama, and outbound-webhook primary clients preserved the validated direct origin without exporting protected path/query data through Taskdeck's configured telemetry.** Gemini was subsequently retired by `#1879`; the current protected primary set is OpenAI, OpenAICompatible, Ollama, and outbound webhook. Each primary handler sets `UseProxy = false`, so ambient/system proxy settings cannot replace the `OutboundWebhookConnectCallback` target. Immediately before `SendAsync`, registered providers and webhook delivery mask the request URI from outer .NET HTTP diagnostics; the protected inner handler restores the configured URI for transport and re-masks it for retry/completion. Public caller-owned provider clients do not opt into this mechanism and retain ordinary `HttpClient` URI/body/auth behavior. The protected clients remove default `IHttpClientFactory` request loggers, disable distributed-trace header propagation, and use a private HTTP metric scope; Taskdeck's OpenTelemetry configuration drops marked activities and that scope. When Sentry is enabled, its outbound handler is removed only from these protected clients; unrelated clients such as `GitHubConnectorProvider` retain Sentry instrumentation, while server-side Sentry tracking remains enabled. The proven boundary covers pre-handler `System.Net.Http` EventSource path/query data and Taskdeck's configured exporters, not independently installed process-global activity/meter listeners or transport-stage host/IP observation. Existing localhost opt-ins and automatic-redirect blocking are unchanged. Proxy-aware outbound support does not exist, so corporate proxy-only deployments fail closed.
- **The repair shipped in PR `#1516`:** published head `dc2a099c` merged as `840874ac` after exact-head Required CI passed 16/16 jobs, CI Extended completed with four successes and 11 intentional path-based skips, and CodeQL passed 4/4. The documented local filters passed 66/66 API and 158/158 Application tests (151 provider/policy compatibility plus seven constructor/remasking controls) across registered pipelines, proxy canaries, delivery, EventSource, OpenTelemetry, and scoped-Sentry boundaries. The registered EventSource and Sentry positive/negative controls passed three fresh-process repetitions. At pre-documentation head `dad8d22a`, the full serialized backend passed 7,539 tests with five intentional skips and no failures, including Architecture 20/20 with one intentional skip and Integration 35/35. Independent security and final correctness review were clean.

Realtime column reconciliation (2026-07-27, `#1522`):
- **A successful create-column response can no longer duplicate or overwrite a fresher same-id SignalR update.** Reconciliation now preserves the existing realtime object and its identity when that id is already present, and appends the HTTP response only when the column is absent. The store regression races a newer realtime column against the older POST payload and pins its identity, name, position, WIP limit, card count, and timestamp. The board E2E helper waits for the matching successful POST and the completed form lifecycle before asserting one scoped lane; collision-name mobile coverage selects exact lanes/cards instead of hiding duplicates with `.first()`. Exact local verification passed 13/13 focused store tests, 3,915/3,915 full Vitest tests, 5/5 focused mobile tests on each of Chromium and WebKit, typecheck, build, and lint (zero errors; six pre-existing warnings).

Backend mutation-testing runway repair (2026-07-27, `#1500`):
- **The scheduled Domain mutation lane now reaches a bounded, artifact-backed result instead of failing before execution.** Stryker.NET is pinned to 4.16.0 in a repository-local tool manifest; a 33-check PowerShell preflight rejects obsolete schema keys, non-numeric thresholds, wrong solution/test context, workflow scope/failure-semantics drift, and non-failing artifact uploads; and the workflow runs from `Taskdeck.Domain.Tests` with a 180-minute ceiling. Backend-only run [30236307062](https://github.com/Chris0Jeky/Taskdeck/actions/runs/30236307062) on exact workflow head `307add004fbe142321a6ec11be21fab708824d5d` completed in 192 seconds: 3,682 mutants created, 2,351 killed, 576 survived, 2 timed out, 753 skipped, score 70.75%. The non-empty two-file `stryker-net-report` artifact is 874,386 bytes (SHA-256 `0e8a9a41b8cd484b6c267bd914c57cda0ffa973f59d8989e89038157605f21c8`). The score remains a non-blocking calibration signal; PR `#1503` and merge remain maintainer-held because the workflow changes operational CI posture.

Parked staging-gate rehearsal candidate (2026-07-27, `#1506`, **stacked on unmerged `#1228` and not shipped**):
- **The candidate makes both manual Compose phases self-contained without creating a deployment boundary.** Immediately before each job's first Compose invocation, it asks Docker Compose's effective `config --variables --format json` parser for the variable model, rejects command/JSON/schema/name/`Required` drift, and requires exactly the JWT and connector inputs to be mandatory. Only then does it generate a 48-byte JWT secret and 32-byte connector encryption key with the runner's cryptographic RNG, mask both, and write them to that job's environment file. The former `production` environment job is replaced by a summary-only parked handoff that binds no GitHub environment and creates no deployment.
- **Evidence and boundary:** manual run [30244212896](https://github.com/Chris0Jeky/Taskdeck/actions/runs/30244212896) passed build verification, real S1-S9 Compose smoke plus cleanup, and the parked summary on exact unmerged workflow/helper head `3efb7bd4499c30d0f0b3c3683e43221e46f103f7`; its logs show both inputs masked, `Environment binding: none`, and `Deployment created: no`, and the exact-SHA deployments query returned `[]`. This evidence refresh changes documentation only, not the proven workflow/helper bytes. The combined Node/governance suite 26/26 and local real-Compose probes also pass. The child remains stacked on parked PR `#1502`; `#1504`, topology/merge, and environment activation remain maintainer-owned. CodeQL was not generated for the non-default stacked base and is re-owed after retargeting.

Dockerless Testcontainers gate repair (2026-07-27, `#1518`):
- **The PostgreSQL integration collection now reaches its documented graceful-skip path before Testcontainers validates Docker.** `PostgresContainerFixture` checks `DockerAvailableCheck` before constructing the container, so a Dockerless host runs seven host-native integration tests and reports all 28 container-dependent tests skipped instead of throwing from the fixture constructor. The real public-constructor and injected-factory regressions prove that the unavailable path does not construct or dispose a phantom container. A timed-out Docker CLI probe is killed with its process tree and given a bounded reap before the check returns; the local exact tree finished with 7 passed / 28 skipped / 0 failed and zero `docker info` processes before or after. At reviewed head `6650370b`, the full serialized backend passed 7,465 tests with 33 intentional skips and hosted Container Integration proved the positive startup/connection/teardown path with 35 passed / 0 skipped / 0 failed. Four DCO-signed-off commits merged as `ff1ee066`; exact-main Required CI run `30257236213` and CodeQL run `30257235538` are green.

Windows concurrent-card HTTP-500 diagnostics (2026-07-27, `#1512`; diagnostic-only, root cause remains open):
- **A same-symptom failure recurred after `#1373`, but did not reproduce locally.** Required `API Integration (windows-latest)` on PR `#1505` at source head `6ee17a4849427d679722a9d1e034d4622536f4fe` returned HTTP 500 for 2/5 simultaneous card creates; Ubuntu and one identical-head rerun passed. Pre-change local proof was 20/20 exact runs plus one full API run at 2,128 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed. Post-diagnostic proof includes focused diagnostics 6/6, the final exact race 20/20, a five-case historical/current concurrency matrix 5/5, and five CI-equivalent full API runs: three at 2,129/4/0 before the final formatter regression and two at 2,130/4/0 after it. The review-fix head then passed its 13/13 focused regressions and a 2,132-passed / 4-skipped / 0-failed full API run. No run captured a spontaneous 500, causal exception, or SQLite classification.
- **The new seam improves the next failure's evidence without masking it.** Unhandled middleware emits bounded structured outer/last-inspected exception types, an explicit classification-truncated flag, and SQLite primary/extended codes beside the bounded correlation identity while retaining the generic GP-03 500 response. The bounded graph walk inspects aggregate branches without calling the last inspected wrapper a root. Normal middleware logs are metadata-only; the test harness captures only structured fields. A failed race assertion joins request/response correlation, status/error code, and matching middleware classification into a sanitized 2,048-character diagnostic. It records no request/response body, credential, user content, exception message, exception summary, or exception object. The injected SQLite 5/5 contract proves the diagnostic transport only; it does not classify the original failure as `SQLITE_BUSY`. No retry, quarantine, or product fix was added, and `#1512` remains open.

Workflow-lint runway repair shipped (2026-08-09, `#1510`):
- **The recovered original `#1511` history removes Actionlint's Docker Hub build from CI Extended without weakening its external-linter contract.** The workflow downloads the pinned Actionlint 1.7.12 Linux archive and Pyflakes 3.4.0 wheel over bounded HTTPS, verifies both published SHA-256 digests before use, installs Pyflakes offline, and passes explicit ShellCheck and Pyflakes paths to Actionlint. The job logs tool versions, the checked-out head, and all 32 discovered workflows, and runs a seven-check bootstrap contract before repository lint. `#1511` retained its original ancestry and incorporated the DCO-clean `#1616` lineage through signed merges, with no rebase, force-push, squash, or discarded commit; merge commit `e989e3e0` closed `#1510`. Exact-head CI `31333250323`, CI Extended `31333250401`, and CodeQL `31333249613` passed, and descendant-main CI `31335719120` completed with 19 successes / 2 intentional skips / 0 failures. Runner-provided ShellCheck remains a version-drift residual risk.

Required Docs Governance hardening (2026-07-26, `#1492`):
- **Required CI now enforces failure-ledger projection synchronization.** The reusable Docs Governance job pins Python 3.12 and runs the existing `failure_ledger.jsonl` ↔ `FAILURE_LEDGER.md` synchronization unittest before its governance checks, so a stale checked-in projection fails without any renderer masking it. Local agentic update workflows intentionally render deliberately recorded JSONL entries first and then test; Required CI never renders.

Windows agent-utility launcher hardening (2026-07-26, `#1487`):
- **Historical launcher repair:** before the repo-owned runtime handlers were retired by `#1552`, configured Windows handlers and utilities moved from unusable Microsoft Store aliases to `py -3 -B`, POSIX guidance kept `python3 -B`, and child tests used `sys.executable -B`. The surviving manual renderer/test permissions retain those verified platform launchers; no Python installation, global PATH change, or product-runtime packaging was introduced.

Paper-default regression alignment (2026-07-14, `#1274`): authenticated E2E sessions now follow the production Paper default unless a frozen Legacy-selector suite opts out explicitly. The refreshed core lane proves the real capture → Accept → Review → approve/apply → board path and runs the full axe WCAG 2.1 A/AA ruleset against settled Paper Home, Today, Inbox, Review, and a seeded Board, plus Login and functional skip-link keyboard coverage. The global Vitest Legacy pin remains intentional because those component tests assert the frozen Legacy DOM directly. Focused Chromium verification is 37/37 across `accessibility`, `onboarding`, `capture-loop`, `review-proposals`, `first-run`, and `smoke`.

Direction change (2026-07-10, maintainer-decided, **ADR-0044**): **the 2026-06-13 archive pivot is superseded — Taskdeck is being revived** and shipped as a **free open beta** (adoption/feedback/exposure first; the commercial side is developed in parallel by the maintainer). Positioning: the local-first, review-first **action-item engine** — transcripts/notes in, evidence-linked proposals out, applied to a board only on human approval — with the write-gated MCP server as the developer-facing second act. ADR-0044's original MIT-forever licensing decision was superseded on 2026-08-12 by **ADR-0050**: historical MIT grants remain valid, while the current core is GPL-3.0-only. The active planning spine is **`docs/REVIVAL_PLAN.md`** (Phase 1 "truth + safety before strangers" repurposes the #1278 archive exit criteria as the v0.1 ship gate; Phase 2 is the LLM transcript-triage engine — the largest authorized new-backend-surface slice; Phase 3 is the beta launch; other scoped exceptions include registration gating, feedback/telemetry, and MCP key/identity hardening as enumerated in §7; then a ~8-week traction checkpoint with the archive plan as the documented fallback). New backend surface is authorized only by REVIVAL_PLAN §7 — the Phase-2 transcript-engine grants (ADR-0044) plus the Phase-4 generalist-expansion grants (**ADR-0046, Accepted 2026-07-13**: artefact intake, dossier read model, mode-scoped navigation; tracker `#1327`; the twin generalist app is explicitly deferred behind the GEN-12 `#1326` evidence gate (gate closed 2026-09-03: stay one application, ADR-0046 stands)). Evidence base: `docs/analysis/2026-07-10_revival_assessment.md` (7 code-review dimensions, ~25 adversarially verified claims, live-verified market data). Archive-closeout issues #1269–#1278 are re-scoped in place (same work, new purpose); dogfooding (#1271) remains the acceptance test for either path. **Dogfooding is now instrumented (2026-07-25):** `docs/dogfooding/README.md` holds the protocol — a dedicated `TASKDECK_DOGFOOD_DB` so dev/demo/E2E traffic cannot contaminate the signal, `scripts/dogfooding/dogfood-snapshot.py` (read-only; counts and dates only) for objective metrics, `docs/dogfooding/LOG.md` for friction, and a checkpoint rubric fixed before any real usage data existed. Read it before collecting checkpoint evidence, or the evidence will not be comparable. The measured pre-dogfooding baseline is recorded in `LOG.md`: **8 active days, none in the last 28, last activity 2026-04-23, 10 of 13 boards demo/test residue** — that is the **pre-sprint baseline**, and it is no longer a current-state claim: the dogfooding sprint **started 2026-08-22** (walkthrough q-2/q-8; see `docs/dogfooding/LOG.md`), with the checkpoint floor at 2026-09-01. Even at the baseline the loop itself worked (**17 of 20 proposals reached Apply**, counted by `AppliedAt`; a status-based count reads 1/20 because `Dismiss()` overwrites an `Applied` status).

(CORRECTED 2026-08-22, `#1939`/PR `#1976`: this note originally claimed the Today cover's "Auto-seals in…" copy was "the separate, real day-sealing feature — deliberately untouched". **That claim was false.** No auto-seal exists: `useTodayDossier`'s `buildHonestDossier` hardcodes `autoSealsIn: null` and no code path ever overrides it, so the `v-else-if="autoSealsIn"` branch in `TodayCover.vue` was unreachable; and the only caller of `IDailySealService` is `TodayController`'s explicit `POST /today/seal` — no `BackgroundService` seals a day.
